    - **Live on stdout:** `system/task_progress` of the workflow's task carries `workflow_progress`, the whole list of phases (`workflow_phase`) and agents (`workflow_agent`: `index`, `label`, `phaseTitle`, `agentId`, `model`, `state` `start` / `progress` / `done` / `error`, `startedAt`, `lastToolName`, `lastToolSummary`, …), sent when an agent starts or ends and at most every few seconds while agents only progress.
    - **Live in files:** `subagents/workflows/<runId>/journal.jsonl` (`started` / `result` / `failed` per agent) and per agent `agent-<id>.jsonl` (its transcript; the first user line is its brief inside the CLI's "[Workflow harness …]" frame) + `agent-<id>.meta.json` (label and phase).
    - **At the end only:** the run file `workflows/<runId>.json` (status, phases, the final `workflowProgress`, model, times). A running run has no run file yet (seen on a live run of the developer's session).
    - The run folder and run file sit under the **session's** project folder (the transcript's); only the script (`workflows/scripts/<name>-<runId>.js`) may land under the project folder of another cwd the session moved to.
  - **What was built:**
    - **The model (no migration, nothing stored):** each run is a group, `Session.workflows` (name, summary, status, phase, phases, agent / done / failed counts, times); its agents join `Session.agents` as `kind: 'workflow'` with `Agent.workflow` (run, index, agent id, phase, model, start / end, current action, cwd, a version that grows with its transcript). States map to Switchboard's: queued → `idle` "queued", start / progress → `run`, done → `done`, error / failed → `fail`; agents still running when the run ends were cut off → `idle`. The run is `run` while its process is alive and has not reported its end (or, for a terminal's run, while its files changed in the last 2 minutes), then `done` / `fail` / `idle` (stopped).
    - **Sources:** this process's stream (the launch, every `task_progress` snapshot, the task's end) and the CLI's files, read by the server (`src/server/workflows/service.ts`): the journal, metas and transcripts as they grow (only what was appended; a transcript's head once), the run file once. Files are polled every 1.5 s only while a run runs. The files make it correct after a reload, a Switchboard restart, for History (closed and reopened sessions), attached, hooked (D48 P4) and peer (D48) sessions alike.
    - **Agent overview:** a row per run (name · summary · `—` · `● 3/7 done · phase Review`) with its agents indented under it (label · phase · solution from the agent's cwd, else `—` · its live action). D37's fold applies: done agents leave the table (counted in "✓ N finished"), a done run's row goes once none of its agents is left; running and queued ones always show.
    - **Agent cards:** running workflow agents get cards with their current action; at most 6 per run, the rest one line "+N more in <workflow>" (the overview lists them all).
    - **Chat:** a workflow agent's row or card opens its conversation at `/sessions/{id}/agents/{agentId}` (D36's view): "Brief from the workflow" (without the CLI's frame), its messages and tool steps, its result (its return value from the journal); it reloads while it runs. "← Main chat" / Esc / Back as for subagents; the note reads "Workflow agents take no messages".
    - **D43's line** gains the counts: "⏳ Running a workflow: <summary> · 3/7 agents done · phase Review".
    - **Counts:** the panel summary counts workflow agents like subagents.
    - **API (additive):** `Session.workflows`, `Agent.workflow`, `BackgroundTask.workflow`, `GET /api/sessions/{id}/workflow-agents/{agentId}/chat` (also through the D48 peer proxy).
    - **Security:** only files under `<configDir>/projects/*/<the session's CLI session id>/` are read; run and agent ids are checked before they become path parts; symlinks are not followed; no path is taken from a file's contents (the launch's `Transcript dir:` gives only the run id).
  - Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Workflow agents (D51)*, `docs/session-panel.md` → *Workflow agents (D51)*, `docs/chat.md` → *Subagent chats*; choices where the report is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D51 · Workflow agents are visible*.

## Rulings on D51 (added 2026-09-29)
- **D51-solution: yes.** A workflow agent's Solution is the solution of its first successful write (Write / Edit / MultiEdit / NotebookEdit whose result is no error), like a subagent's (D21); without one, the solution of its cwd, else `—`. Each agent transcript is scanned in order as it grows, at most 4 MB per read and 32 MB in all per transcript; once the first write is found (or the cap reached) it is never scanned again, and a finished run's transcripts are scanned once and kept in memory.
- **D51-card-cap: keep 6 cards per run;** the "+N more in <workflow>" line is a button that opens that run's cards in place, and "Show fewer · <workflow>" cuts them again (kept in memory for the page's life, like the finished line).
- **D51-resume: yes.** A **stopped or failed** run whose script Switchboard found gets **Resume run** on an actions row under its overview row. It sends the session's agent a message asking it to call `Workflow({ scriptPath: "<its script>", resumeFromRunId: "<runId>" })` (plus the run's `args` when its run file has them; the parameter names read in the CLI 2.1.284 binary) through the normal message path: D44 queues it while a turn runs, and a paused session resumes on it. It is disabled, with the reason, for a closed session or an unreachable peer. Hooked sessions take it (their messages go through the hooks). Switchboard never runs the script itself. Additive: `WorkflowRun.resume`.
- **D51-probe: yes, done.** One D11 probe on the real CLI (2.1.284, Haiku, `--max-turns 3`, `.spike/sandbox/wf-probe/`, `--setting-sources project,local`, CLAUDE* env removed, `--allowedTools Workflow`, finished in 11 s) confirmed D51's evidence: the Workflow tool exists in `-p` stream-json, the launch result, `task_progress` lines with the whole `workflow_progress` (and lines without it in between), the journal, the per-agent transcripts and metas, the run file at the end, `task_updated` / `task_notification` `completed`, then the CLI's own turn. Details: `.loop/questions.md` → *D51*.

## Ruling: continuous week pace (added 2026-09-29)
- **Supersedes D23's daily steps.** Developer ruling, 2026-09-29: the Week bar's allowance grows **by the minute** over the 7-day window, like the Session bar's (D46): during minute *n* of the 10 080 it is *n* × 100 / 10 080 % (before: each whole day's 14.29 % from that day's start, which left the last day almost nothing). The tooltip names the next minute step's time only: "On pace: 33% of 57.14% until 17:05" (no "allowed", no weekday). The colors, the marker, the 2-decimal rounding, "on pace = below the allowance" and unknown-stays-unknown are D23's as before; `WeeklyPace.day` (1–7) is still reported. Details: `docs/usage.md` → *Weekly pace*.

## A peer's schedules and loops (added 2026-09-29)
- **D52 A peer's schedules and loops show and work on Schedules & loops.** Developer request, 2026-09-29: "Check whether the remote runs schedules or loops and add them to Switchboard (the Mac) within Schedules & loops." Builds on D48 (proxy, remote ids, offline snapshots, no chains), M7.1 / M7.2 (schedules, loop cards), D42.
  - **Schedules (ruling: full management).** A paired machine's schedules appear in the schedule table with its **machine tag**, and are **created, edited, deleted, run now, paused and resumed** from here; the schedule lives and runs on that machine. The schedule editor (the New-session form in schedule mode) gets D48's **Machine** row (D48 had hidden it for schedules): a peer loads that machine's folders and models and saves the schedule there; an Edit stays on the schedule's machine (the row is locked). **Delete** is new for every schedule (Edit → "Delete schedule", two clicks; `DELETE /api/schedules/{id}`, refused while a run is in progress). A peer's failed-run Inbox items already came through the proxied Inbox: Retry run runs on that machine, and "Open fix session" opens the form on that machine in the schedule's folder.
  - **Loops (ruling: all terminal sessions).** A peer's loops (observed `/loop`, `ScheduleWakeup`, `CronCreate`, Workflow runs) appear with its tag: from its Switchboard sessions and its **hooked** terminal sessions (now tracked from their imported events, like supervised ones), and from its **un-hooked** terminal sessions (`claude agents --json` there), derived read-only from their transcripts on that machine (bounded, cached by size and mtime, no hooks needed). A session's loop card opens its session; an un-hooked terminal's card offers **Hook into…** (D48 P4) instead and says why it cannot be opened. This machine's un-hooked terminal sessions show the same way (ASSUMED D52-local-terminals).
  - **Proxy:** every new route is on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (`GET/POST /api/schedules`, `POST …/{id}/run|pause|resume`, `DELETE /api/schedules/{id}`, `GET /api/terminal-loops`); ids are D48 remote ids; `scheduleRun` is forwarded between peers; `POST /api/schedules` takes `machine` (a new schedule there) or a peer schedule's remote id (an Edit there). **Offline:** a peer's schedules and terminal loops stay listed from the snapshot (`peer_snapshots` kinds `schedules`, `terminal-loops`; no migration), tagged unreachable, with every action refused with D48's reason. **No chains:** a peer answers its own schedules and loops only.
  - Details: `docs/peers.md` → *A peer's schedules and loops (D52)*, `docs/schedules.md` → *Delete (D52)* / *A peer's schedules (D52)*, `docs/derivations.md` → *Loop cards* → *Terminal sessions (D52)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D52 · A peer's schedules and loops*.

## Live activity for remote and hooked sessions (added 2026-09-29)
- **D53 Hooked and peer sessions show the same live activity as local ones; a hooked session's queued message says what it waits on.** Developer request, 2026-09-29: "When a message is sent to the remote session, show the same Claude Code-like statuses in the chat while Claude is thinking on the remote PC — it would help me identify whether the session is stuck." Context: a hooked terminal session on the Windows PC (D48 P4) showed no live activity (Switchboard gets only its hook events and its transcript), so a running tool or a long thinking looked the same as stuck; and a message sat queued (D44 clock) because the session had no waiter yet, with nothing saying why.
  - **Live line (same look and words as D19 / D30):** a hooked session's `Session.activity` is derived from its transcript and hook calls, in the same shape, so the chat line, the sidebar row and the agent cards read it unchanged: a thinking verb from UserPromptSubmit or the transcript's prompt line (timed from the turn's start); `● <Tool>: <summary>` from the transcript's `tool_use` line until its `tool_result` (timed from that line's timestamp); `Writing…` after a text block; `⏸ Waiting for permission: <tool>` while its PermissionRequest is held (a supervised session's wait still reads `Waiting for you`); running subagents on their cards; nothing after Stop / SessionEnd (a Stop or an end line after the turn's start ends it, whichever comes first). No token count (the transcript has none). The transcript is polled every 500 ms while a turn runs (1.5 s otherwise); `activity` events at most one a second per session.
  - **Staleness hint:** `SessionActivity.quietSince` (additive: the newest transcript change or hook call); while a turn runs and nothing changed for 3 minutes, the chat line and the sidebar row add `· no activity for 3m` (muted), not while waiting for the developer.
  - **Delivery state:** additive `Session.hookStatus` `{ waiter, hookSeen, delivery }` on hooked sessions; `delivery` → the queued bubble's clock tooltip and a muted line under it: "Waiting for the session to take it up (delivered to its hook)" (handed to a waiter), "Waiting for the next turn boundary" (mid-turn, before the fold; or a wake-up in flight / rate-limited), "No hook listening yet — type anything in that terminal once (the hooks were installed after this session started)" (idle, no waiter), "Session ended". The header's hooked note adds the no-waiter and ended words.
  - **Peers:** the peer derives the activity of its own sessions (supervised and hooked) and forwards `activity` (D48 already did); found and fixed: this machine's cached peer session kept the activity of its last `sessionUpdated`, so the sidebar list (reloaded on any `sessionUpdated`) fell back to a stale state; it now follows the `activity` events. A machine that is not online shows no live line (`activity: null`; the offline note says why).
  - **Nothing costs model calls, no hook is added** (the transcript's `tool_use` line makes `PreToolUse` unnecessary), nothing is stored, no migration, no new route or event name.
  - Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Live activity* → *Hooked terminal sessions (D53)*, `docs/chat.md` → *Live activity line* and *Queued messages* → *Hooked sessions (D53)*, `docs/peers.md` → *Live activity and delivery state (D53)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Live activity for hooked and peer sessions (D53)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D53 · Live activity for remote and hooked sessions*.

## Pin, re-order and folders in the sidebar (added 2026-09-29)
- **D54 Sessions can be pinned, re-ordered and grouped into folders in the sidebar.** Developer request, 2026-09-29: "Pin and re-order sessions in the left pane; foldable folders for sessions to order them." Builds on D33 (close / reopen), D41 (collapsible sidebar), D48 (peers' sessions, remote ids, offline snapshots).
  - **Order (ruling: manual only where the developer placed things):** SESSIONS lists **Pinned** (the dragged order), then the **folders** (the dragged order; a folder's sessions in their dragged order), then the **loose** sessions in the service's order, newest first, as before; a new session appears at the top of that list.
  - **Pin:** from a row's ⋯ menu (Pin / Unpin) or by dragging onto the Pinned group. A session is in one place only: pinned, in one folder, or loose.
  - **Folders (ruling: drag in / out, one level):** "+" in the SESSIONS label creates one; rename, delete (its sessions become loose), collapse / expand (remembered); sessions move in and out by drag and drop and by the row menu's "Move to folder ▸"; folders re-order by dragging their heads; "Move up / down" in the menus is the keyboard path. A collapsed folder shows its name, its session count and an amber dot while a session inside waits for the developer.
  - **Storage (ruling: Switchboard's database):** migration 0019 (`sidebar_folders`, `sidebar_places`); the same in every tab, the installed app and after restarts; live in other tabs through the additive `/hub` event `sidebarLayoutChanged` (the whole layout). Additive routes `GET /api/sidebar`, `POST /api/sidebar/folders`, `PUT /api/sidebar/folders/{folderId}` (+ `/position`), `DELETE /api/sidebar/folders/{folderId}`, `POST /api/sidebar/place`.
  - **Peers:** a paired machine's sessions can be pinned and put into this machine's folders; the layout is per machine (not on the peer API, not forwarded). Forgetting a machine removes its sessions' places.
  - **Closed sessions:** leave the sidebar as today (D33); their place is kept, so Reopen restores it. Deleting a session record removes its place.
  - **Unchanged:** with nothing pinned or foldered the list is the prototype's; hover ×, status dots, machine tags, folder tags, activity lines, the double-click rename and the command palette work as before.
  - Details: `docs/sidebar.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Sidebar pins and folders (D54)*, `docs/database.md` (0019); choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D54 · Pin, re-order and folders in the sidebar*.

## Updates from GitHub releases (added 2026-09-30)
- **D55 Switchboard checks its GitHub releases and updates a release install itself; a git checkout is only told.** Developer request, 2026-09-30, after the 1.0.0 release (`switchboard-1.0.0.tar.gz` + `.sha256`, installed with `npm ci --omit=dev && npm start`). Builds on D14 (the data folder), M9.1 (the login service), D34 (the installed app), M2.4 (restart recovery), M3.3 (system Inbox items).
  - **Source (ruling):** the GitHub releases of `MarcinGadomski94/switchboard` (one constant, `RELEASE_REPO`; `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATE_REPO` for forks and tests), through the **public REST API, unauthenticated** (`releases/latest`); on 401 / 403 / 404 / 429 (private repository, rate limit) through **`gh`** (`gh release view` / `gh release download`) when it is installed and signed in, else "Can't reach releases: …". Drafts and pre-releases are ignored. Switchboard never sends a credential of the user's (gh uses its own). The repository became public on 2026-09-30, so the API is the primary path.
  - **When (ruling):** on start and **hourly**, plus **Check for updates** in the new **Settings → Updates** section (version, install kind, last check time and result, latest version, release notes as Markdown, the Update button, an error line). Versions compare as semver against `package.json`.
  - **Behaviour (ruling: prompt, then update + restart):** a newer release raises an Inbox system item (`update-available`, What's new / Dismiss, one per version) and a slim dismissible banner "Switchboard <v> is available" with **What's new** (the notes) and **Update**. Update, after a confirmation that names the sessions to be resumed: download the tarball and the `.sha256` (only from this repository's release asset URLs over HTTPS, redirects only to GitHub's asset hosts, size-limited); verify the SHA-256 (refused on a mismatch or a missing checksum); unpack into a staging folder under the data folder with Node's own zlib and a pure tar reader (only entries under `switchboard-<v>/`; traversal, absolute paths, links and special files refused); `npm ci --omit=dev` there (progress in the UI; a failure leaves the current install untouched); switch; restart. The database and the data folder are untouched (migrations run on the new version's start). One update at a time.
  - **Switch (ASSUMED D55-layout):** versions side by side under `<dataDir>/versions/<v>`; the **login service's definition is the stable pointer** (re-registered for the new folder, the M9.1 plan with its rollback); `<dataDir>/updates/installs.json` keeps the current and the previous install; the previous one stays for a manual rollback (`npm run service:install -- --start` in its folder).
  - **Restart:** verified that none of the login services restarts Switchboard on exit (M9.1: `KeepAlive` false, `Restart=no`, no `RestartOnFailure`), and that stays; instead the updater asks the manager for one start of the new definition: macOS a detached helper runs `launchctl bootout` + `bootstrap` once this process exited; Linux `systemctl --user restart --no-block`; Windows a detached helper runs `schtasks /Run` once this process exited. A Switchboard started by hand (`npm start`) never exits: the developer restarts it (the new folder's `npm start` command is shown). Live sessions resume through the existing restart recovery.
  - **Git checkouts (ruling: notify only):** a folder with `.git` gets the banner, the item, the notes and the git commands, no Update button. The developer's Mac runs from a git checkout.
  - **Windows:** paths, npm (`node <npm-cli.js>`, since `npm.cmd` needs a shell), extraction (no system `tar`) and the Task Scheduler restart are written for it; **unverified on Windows**.
  - **Peers:** nothing forwarded (`updateChanged` is this machine's; no update route on the peer API); a peer's version in Settings → Machines was not added (OPEN D55-peer-version).
  - **Releases:** `npm run release:package` writes `switchboard-<version>.tar.gz` + `.sha256` in the 1.0.0 layout; the developer publishes with `gh release create`.
  - **API (additive):** `GET /api/updates`, `POST /api/updates/check`, `POST /api/updates/install { version }` (202), `POST /api/updates/dismiss { version }`; `/hub` `updateChanged`; `SWITCHBOARD_UPDATES=off`. No migration (the state is the settings key `updates.state`).
  - Details: `docs/updates.md`, `docs/service.md` → *Restart after an update*, `docs/security.md` → *Updates (D55)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Updates from GitHub releases (D55)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D55 · Updates from GitHub releases*.

## Simple New-session form (added 2026-09-30)
- **D56 The New-session dialog has a Simple form (the default) next to the Full one.** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "Add a simple session starter for new sessions — the current one is quite extensive and custom to what we use it for (workspace router answers, epic/task branching)." Builds on D14 (folders), D22 (titles), D32 (ticket branches), D40 (task-only worktrees), D42 (model at start), D48 (Machine row).
  - **Fields (ruling):** **Folder** (the saved folders, Browse…; D48's Machine row with a paired machine), **Message** (the first message; a textarea: Enter types a new line, **⌘↩ / Ctrl+↩** starts), **Title** (optional; empty = the message's first line, whole words up to 60 characters), **Model + effort** (D42's picker, on the last choice), **Work in its own git worktree** (one checkbox, only for a git repo folder; the branch is derived from the title as a plain slug, `sb/<short name>`, a title that starts with a ticket key keeps D32's pre-fill; shown read-only with **Edit**; any valid git branch name, no D32 ticket rule).
  - **Not in Simple (ruling):** router answers, solutions, branching (epic, base, parent), Ultracode, QA; also schedules, "Resume a terminal conversation" and "From a remote session" (Full only).
  - **Switch (ruling):** a **Simple / Full** toggle at the top; the mode is **remembered** as the settings row `newSession.mode` (`PUT /api/settings`, editable, like D41's pane rows; D42 keeps `models.last` in the same table); **Simple** on a fresh install; what is typed (folder, message, title, model, worktree) **carries over** (one form state). The palette's **New session** opens the remembered mode; a prefill ("Open fix session", a schedule's Edit) and schedule mode open Full.
  - **API (additive):** `POST /api/sessions` takes `simple: true` (`NewSimpleSession`): no router fields (stored `null`, also in a workspace), no solutions, no branching; `worktrees: true` only in a repo folder (422 in a workspace), its branch as sent or `sb/<name>`, cut by D40's task-only rule. The first message is the message alone: a workspace gets **no "Session-start answers" block** (the agent asks the router's questions itself); a repo worktree session gets only the worktree note. No migration.
  - Details: `docs/new-session.md` → *Simple mode (D56)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Simple New-session form (D56)*, `docs/settings.md`; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D56 · Simple New-session form*.

## Paste and attach images and files (added 2026-09-30)
- **D57 The chat composer and the New-session forms take images and files: pasted, dropped or picked.** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "I need to be able to paste images and files like I do on the claude.ai page." Builds on D44 (queued messages), D50 (Stop), D56 (the Simple form), D48 (peers, hooked terminal sessions).
  - **Input (ruling):** in the chat composer **paste** (⌘V / Ctrl+V of a screenshot or an image copied in the browser, and of copied files where the browser exposes them), **drag and drop** onto the chat or the composer, and a **📎** file picker (several files); the same in the New-session forms' message field (Simple and Full), so the first message carries them. Before sending they are chips above the field (an image's thumbnail; a file's icon, name and size), each removable; the sent bubble shows thumbnails (a click opens them larger) and file chips with name and size (a PDF opens, other files download).
  - **Delivery (ruling: images + PDFs inline, others as files):** images (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, sniffed by their magic bytes) and PDFs go **inline** in the stream-json user message as content blocks (`image` / `document` with a base64 `source`, the shapes CLI 2.1.284 / 2.1.285 accept, read in its binary); every other file is saved and the message names its absolute path under a line `Attached files:` (`- /…/attachments/<session>/<id>-<name> (12 KB)`), so the agent reads it with its tools. The CLI's limits decide what fits inline (2000 px, 5 MiB base64 / 3.75 MB raw per image, 20 MiB and 100 pages per PDF, a 32 MiB request): an image larger than that is downscaled in the browser first, and what still does not fit (or exceeds the message's inline budget of 24 MiB base64) goes as a file instead of being refused (ASSUMED D57-downscale, D57-inline-budget).
  - **Storage (ruling):** Switchboard's data folder, `<dataDir>/attachments/<session id>/<id>-<name>` (folder 0700, files 0600), never inside a repo; the New-session form's uploads are staged in `_staged/` and move into the new session at its start. Inline images and PDFs are stored too, and the message's event lists its attachments by id (no bytes in the database), so the chat shows them after a reload or a restart. Migration **0020** (`attachments`). Retention: a session's rows go with the session; files older than **30 days**, and folders of sessions that are gone, are removed at start (ASSUMED D57-retention).
  - **API (additive):** `POST /api/sessions/{id}/attachments` and `POST /api/attachments` (staged) take one file as `{ name, data }` (base64) and answer the `Attachment` (id, sanitized name, size, sniffed kind and type); `POST /api/sessions/{id}/messages` and `POST /api/sessions` take `attachments: [ids]` (a message may then have no text); `GET /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}` serves it; `InterruptResult.withdrawnAttachments`; `UserPayload.attachments` / `sentText`. Caps: **20 MiB per file (413), 50 MiB and 20 files per message** (ASSUMED D57-caps, constants, not configurable).
  - **Serving (ruling):** behind the cookie guard like every route; a sniffed image or PDF inline with its own type, everything else (SVG and HTML included) as `application/octet-stream` + `Content-Disposition: attachment`; always `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; a CSP that runs nothing (all but PDFs).
  - **Queued messages and Stop:** a message with attachments queues like any other (D44); a Stop gives its text and its attachments back to the composer (still uploaded, so sending again carries them).
  - **Peers (D48):** a peer's session uploads through the proxy to that machine (its data folder); the routes are on `PEER_API_ALLOW`, uploads take the attachments' body limit on the peer listener too, downloads pass through as bytes with their serving headers. **Hooked terminal sessions:** hooks carry text only, so every attachment is a file on that machine and its path goes in the message (ASSUMED D57-hooked).
  - **Transcripts:** a terminal prompt with images (Attach, a move, a teleport, a hooked session) keeps them: an image whose base64 is in the transcript is stored as the session's attachment and shown; one without bytes is a placeholder "image". History reads an image-only prompt as `[image]`.
  - Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Attachments (D57)*, `docs/security.md` → *Attachments (D57)*, `docs/database.md` (0020), `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Attachments (D57)*, `docs/fake-claude.md`; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D57 · Paste and attach images and files*.

## Subfolders in the sidebar (added 2026-09-30)
- **D58 Sidebar folders can hold folders (subfolders).** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "Make a possibility for subfolders too." Extends D54 (pins, manual order, one-level folders), which was deliberately one level.
  - **Nesting:** a folder holds sessions and folders, to at most **5 levels** (ASSUMED D58-depth); each level has its own manual order; under a folder's head its subfolders come first, then its sessions. Each level is indented 10 px further (a top-level folder's sessions keep D54's 12 px); names end in an ellipsis and nothing scrolls sideways, also at 5 levels in the 256 px sidebar.
  - **Create / move:** "+" in SESSIONS makes a top-level folder as before; a folder's ⋯ → **New subfolder**. A folder dragged onto the middle of another folder's head goes into it, onto its upper / lower quarter before / after it at that level, onto the "move to the top level" zone out of every folder. The folder menu adds **Move to folder ▸** (Top level + the tree, indented, without the folder itself and its own subfolders: no loops) and keeps **Move up / down** within its level. A session's **Move to folder ▸** lists the whole tree, indented.
  - **Collapse:** each folder collapses on its own (remembered); a collapsed folder's count and amber "needs you" dot cover everything inside it at any depth (the count does so expanded too).
  - **Delete (ASSUMED D58-delete):** nothing inside is ever deleted: the subfolders move up one level into the folder's place, its sessions go to the end of its parent (loose when it was top level, as in D54); a folder with subfolders asks first in its menu.
  - **Storage:** migration **0021** adds a nullable `sidebar_folders.parent_id` (foreign key to `sidebar_folders`, never itself); existing folders are top level with their sessions in place; positions are per parent. The service refuses a loop or a sixth level (422 on `parentId`) and an unknown parent (404).
  - **API (additive):** `parentId` on `POST /api/sidebar/folders` and `PUT /api/sidebar/folders/{folderId}/position` (absent = D54's meaning); every `SidebarFolder` carries `parentId`, and `folders` is in tree order; `sidebarLayoutChanged` unchanged in name. No new route.
  - **Unchanged:** pins, the loose order, the keyboard menus, second-tab live sync, a paired machine's sessions in (sub)folders, the layout being this machine's only; with nothing pinned or foldered the list is the prototype's.
  - Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Subfolders (D58)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Subfolders in the sidebar (D58)*, `docs/database.md` (0021); choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D58 · Subfolders in the sidebar*.

## Move to worktree on an existing branch (added 2026-09-30)
- **D60 "Move … to worktree" can put the worktree on an existing branch, local or remote.** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "When there is a conflict in Solutions and it suggests a worktree, I should not only be able to create a new branch but also choose from existing ones (remotes too) for that session." Builds on gap #2 (isolate), D32 (ticket branches), D40 (reusing a task branch, the bounded fetch), M6.3 (the conflict card).
  - **Confirm step:** two choices, **New branch** (D32's field, unchanged, the default) and **Existing branch**: a searchable list of the repo's local branches and every remote's branches (`origin/*` and any other remote), shown from the cached refs at once and again after an automatic `git fetch --all --prune` (↻ Refresh fetches again; D40's 60 s bound; a failed fetch is a warning line and the local list still works). Each row: name, local / remote, the last commit's subject and age; a branch checked out in the main checkout or another worktree is listed **disabled** with "checked out in `<path>`" (git checks a branch out once).
  - **Isolate:** a local branch → `git worktree add <path> <branch>`, as it is (never deleted on a discard); a remote-only `origin/foo` → `git worktree add --track -b foo <path> origin/foo`; a remote branch whose local branch exists → the local one, and the message says so (ASSUMED D60-local-wins). The D32 ticket rule applies to new branches only. Refused before anything is made or paused: checked out elsewhere (409 `branch-checked-out`), unknown (409 `branch-not-found`, new code), a name that is not a branch argument (422 on `existingBranch`). The unknown / detached refusals and the double-click idempotence are unchanged.
  - **Move message:** says the worktree is on an existing branch and names its upstream ("tracking `origin/foo`", "made from `origin/foo` and tracking it", or "tracks no remote branch"), so the agent continues that branch's work (read its recent commits first) instead of starting fresh.
  - **API (additive):** `POST /api/solutions/{repo}/isolate { sessionId, existingBranch }` (exactly one of `branch` / `existingBranch`); `GET /api/solutions/{repo}/branches?session=<id>&fetch=1` → `RepoBranches`. Not on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (the isolate route never was). No migration.
  - **Not built:** the same picker in the New-session forms' worktree option (Full's D32 branch, Simple's "own worktree"): recorded as OPEN D60-new-session-picker.
  - Details: `docs/worktrees.md` → *Existing branches (D60)*, `docs/solutions.md` → *Conflicts*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Move to worktree on an existing branch (D60)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D60 · Move to worktree on an existing branch*.

## Simple mode starts in any folder (added 2026-09-30)
- **D59 A Simple session can start in any folder: a workspace, a git repo or a plain folder (neither).** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "I shouldn't be blocked in simple mode from spawning a session in any folder (even if it's not a git repo and has no AGENTS.md)." Builds on D14 (folders, kinds), D18 (folder names), D56 (the Simple form), D57 (attachments).
  - **Kind (ruling):** a third folder kind, `plain`: an existing folder with no `AGENTS.md` that is not a git main checkout (`inspectFolder` no longer answers `unsupported`; not absolute, missing, a file and a linked git worktree are still refused, ASSUMED D59-worktree). No solutions, no worktrees, no router answers. The UI calls it a **folder** (Settings → Folders' kind tag, the header's root line `<cwd> · folder`); its check line reads `✓ folder · no AGENTS.md, not a git repo · Simple sessions`.
  - **Saving (ruling):** saved like the others with the same checks (absolute, exists, a folder, canonical path, no duplicates, D18 names) minus the workspace/repo requirement: from Simple's Browse… (typed or browsed), Settings → Folders → Add… and the setup wizard (ASSUMED D59-save-everywhere); it is then in the Folder dropdown next time.
  - **Simple start:** `claude` runs in the folder; the first message is the message alone plus D57's attachment lines; the worktree checkbox shows for git repos only (as before; `worktrees: true` is a 422 there).
  - **Full (ASSUMED D59-full):** Full cannot start in a plain folder: the Folder section says "This folder has no AGENTS.md and isn't a git repository …" with **Use Simple**, the router sections are hidden and Start stays disabled; `POST /api/sessions` without `simple: true` answers 422 on `folder`. Schedules are Full starts, so a schedule cannot use a plain folder (ASSUMED D59-schedules).
  - **Elsewhere:** Solutions shows a plain folder empty (ASSUMED D59-solutions); History's "Continue in Switchboard" moves a conversation inside a saved plain folder into it (no solutions), and when no workspace or repo encloses a conversation, its start folder is offered as a plain folder to add (ASSUMED D59-history); D38 adoption, D40/D47 branching and remote sessions (D25) are workspace / repo only, as before; a peer's plain folders work the same over the proxy (D48).
  - **Storage (ruling: the safest path):** migration **0022** rebuilds the small `folders` table with `kind IN ('workspace', 'repo', 'plain')` by SQLite's 12-step procedure (every row, id, rowid and index kept; the runner turns foreign keys off for a migration whose first line is `-- switchboard: foreign_keys=off` and checks `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` before committing); earlier migrations untouched. `sessions` (referenced by most tables) is **not** rebuilt: a plain session stores its `root` with `root_kind` NULL, a state nothing wrote before, which the session repository reads as `plain` (ASSUMED D59-root-kind).
  - Details: `docs/folders.md` → *Plain folders (D59)*, `docs/new-session.md` → *Simple mode (D56)*, `docs/database.md` (0022), `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Plain folders (D59)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D59 · Simple mode starts in any folder*.

## MCP servers page (added 2026-09-30)
- **D61 An MCP page: see, check, reconnect, sign in to, enable / disable, add, edit and remove the MCP servers Claude Code loads.** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "An MCP tab where I can see, check and reconnect/re-auth the MCP servers plugged into Claude Code." Rulings: **a global page only** (no per-session tab) and **full management** (also add, edit, remove).
  - **Page:** a sidebar nav item **MCP** after *Schedules & loops*, route `/mcp`. A folder selector (the default folder first, then a paired machine's folders); the servers grouped by scope: local, project (`.mcp.json`, needs approval), user, then read-only plugin / claude.ai / managed servers a check found. Per server: transport, masked command or URL, env / header **names**, status, tools, last check; per row Check · Reconnect · Authenticate / Re-authenticate · Disable / Enable · Edit · Remove (confirmed); Check all and + Add server in the header. Every action shows the CLI command it ran and the CLI's own error; after a change: *live sessions pick it up on their next start*.
  - **Through the CLI only:** listing reads the CLI's config files (`~/.claude.json` / `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `.mcp.json`, the settings' approval lists; read-only). Check = `claude mcp get <name>`; Check all = `mcp_status`, `claude mcp list` as fallback; Add = `claude mcp add-json <name> <json> --scope …`; Edit = `remove` + `add-json` (the old definition restored when the add fails; a rename or new scope adds first); Remove = `claude mcp remove <name> --scope …`; all with the folder as cwd, argv arrays, time limits.
  - **Reconnect / enable / disable / sign-in headlessly:** a short-lived `claude -p --input-format stream-json` helper in the folder, driven by control requests only (no user message → no model call): `mcp_reconnect`, `mcp_toggle` (the CLI writes the folder's `disabledMcpServers`: its own mechanism), `mcp_authenticate` (VERIFIED in the 2.1.285 code: answers the authorization URL without opening a browser and completes through the CLI's localhost callback), `mcp_clear_auth` (Re-authenticate), `mcp_oauth_callback_url` (a pasted redirect). The page opens the URL in a new tab and polls until the server connects; a failure shows the terminal instructions with Copy. The helper is killed after use.
  - **Secrets:** no env or header value ever reaches the browser; secret-looking args and URL parts are masked (`••••`); the Edit form keeps secrets server-side ("unchanged" placeholders, `keep: true`); logs and errors are scrubbed. The `add-json` argv carries the definition (the CLI has no other input): documented in `docs/security.md`.
  - **Peers (D48):** the MCP routes are on `PEER_API_ALLOW`: the page manages a paired machine's servers in its folders; its sign-in callback is on that machine, so the redirect URL is pasted back.
  - No migration (the check cache is in memory).
  - Details: `docs/mcp.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *MCP servers page (D61)*, `docs/security.md` → *MCP servers (D61)*, `docs/fake-claude.md` → *MCP (D61)*; choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D61 · MCP servers page*.

## Fix: sidebar scrolling (added 2026-09-30)
- **Only the SESSIONS list scrolls, not the whole sidebar.** Developer request, 2026-09-30: "When there are a lot of sessions, only the sessions part of the left sidebar should scroll, not the whole sidebar." Builds on D29 (never sideways), D41 (collapsible sidebar), D54 / D58 (pins, folders, subfolders) and D61 (whose sixth nav item made the demo seed's sidebar scroll by 24 px at 1440×900).
  - **Layout:** the brand, + New session, the nav, TOOLS and the SESSIONS label stay at the top, Settings and the machine footer at the bottom; the SESSIONS list takes the rest (at least 160 px) and scrolls down only. The SESSIONS label stays in view above it (ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-header). A long TOOLS list scrolls on its own within a quarter of the window and gives way to two rows before SESSIONS goes under 160 px (ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-tools); a window too short for all of it lets the whole sidebar scroll as before (ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-min).
  - **Kept working:** the opened session's row is scrolled into the list (palette, Inbox, links; ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-reveal); ⋯ menus are not clipped and flip above their ⋯ near the window's bottom, scrolling closes them (ASSUMED sidebar-scroll-menu); drag and drop scrolls the list at its edges (the browser's own; VERIFIED in Chromium); D41 keeps the scroll position. No API, storage or migration change.
  - Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Layout and scrolling*; choices: `.loop/questions.md` → *Fix · sidebar scrolling*.

## Fix: peer reconnects (added 2026-10-01)
- **A paired machine that drops is reconnecting, not offline; the retries show; Reconnect now.** Developer report, 2026-10-01: "For remote sessions I'm sometimes getting: '<machine> is offline — reconnect to continue'. There's no refresh button, so what should I do? I also can't see that it's reconnecting in the background (no loading state), and sometimes it reconnects by itself." Cause: the Mac reaches the PC over a relayed Tailscale path (DERP), which drops long-lived streams (the D48 event stream) more often; every drop blocked interaction at once, the retries backed off up to 60 s unseen, and nothing was logged. Builds on D48 (rulings D48-cache-persist, D48-hello-wakes) and D53.
  - **Retries:** right after a drop at once, then 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, 10 s and every 15 s (was: doubling to 60 s), ±20 % jitter (ASSUMED reconnect-schedule).
  - **Grace period:** 20 s `reconnecting` after a drop (and at start) before `offline` (a new additive state). Reads keep working from the cache and the snapshots; actions are **held** for the reconnection (at most 10 s) and then sent, rather than refused (ASSUMED reconnect-hold). `offline` keeps D48-cache-persist's block.
  - **Stalls:** a stream with no event and no keepalive for 25 s is cut and reconnected (ASSUMED reconnect-stall).
  - **Why and when:** each failed attempt records its kind (refused, timeout, route, auth, HTTP, cut, closed, stalled), in plain words and with its time, and when the next attempt starts; all timeouts → "Check that <machine> is awake and on Tailscale."
  - **Reconnect now:** `POST /api/machines/{id}/reconnect` (never two attempts at once) and a button in the session header note, the blocked composer note and each Settings → Machines row; it spins while trying, the blocks lift at once on success, the reason shows on failure.
  - **UI words:** "Reconnecting to studio-pc… · attempt 3 · next try in 8 s" (spinner, nothing blocked), "studio-pc is unreachable · retrying in 8 s · Reconnect now" with the last error and its time; tags "· reconnecting…" / "· unreachable" / "· auth failed" / "· no address". Live through the additive `/hub` event `machineState` (Settings → Machines no longer polls every 3 s).
  - **Log:** every state change (online → reconnecting → offline → online) and failed attempt, with the machine's name and id, the reason and the attempt count; never a token.
  - Details: `docs/peers.md` → *Connection states*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Peer reconnects*, `docs/hub.md`; choices: `.loop/questions.md` → *Fix · peer reconnects*.

## Fix: long messages cut off (added 2026-10-01)
- **A long reply shows whole; a reply stored cut can be restored from the transcript.** Developer report, 2026-10-01: "some long messages returned in the session are cut off in the chat". Cause: `src/core/event-payload.ts` cut every long payload string at 4,000 characters (`clip`), and the recorder, the transcript import (Attach here, moved and hooked sessions) and a Workflow agent's chat applied it to assistant text and subagent prompts too, not only to tool inputs and results; the chat showed what was stored. The web itself cuts nothing (no max-height, no line clamp, no Markdown limit; VERIFIED).
  - **Stored whole:** assistant text and a subagent's prompt, up to a safety cap of 1,000,000 characters (`MESSAGE_TEXT_LIMIT`, ASSUMED long-messages-cap); past it, stored cut with the additive `truncated: true`. Tool inputs and results, permission inputs and a turn's `result` text stay cut at 4,000 (ASSUMED long-messages-tool-limit).
  - **Stored before the fix:** such a message has no flag and exactly 4,000 characters. Under it: "Message cut at 4,000 characters · Show full message"; the button calls the additive `GET /api/sessions/{id}/events/{eventId}/full`, which finds the whole text in the session's CLI transcript (and its subagents' files) by `message.id`, else the line's uuid, else the stored text as a prefix (closest in time), and **writes it back** into the event (`truncated: false`), published on `/hub` (ASSUMED long-messages-write-back). Live, attached, moved and hooked sessions and their subagents; on the peer API. A transcript that is gone, or no longer has the message, is said in the note.
  - **Tool output in the chat:** a subagent's Result (its Agent call's `tool_result`) shows "Output truncated at 4,000 characters · Show full output", read from the transcript by the same route and only shown, never stored (ASSUMED long-messages-tool-output); a brief cut with its call's input offers "Show full message".
  - **Workflow agents (D51):** their chats are read from their files on every request, so they are now whole (brief, messages, result); nothing to restore.
  - **No background repair** at start (ASSUMED long-messages-no-repair); no migration. Database size: messages over 4,000 characters are about 2 % of a sample of local transcripts, a few KB per long session (`docs/derivations.md` → *What is clipped*).
  - Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Cut messages*, `docs/derivations.md` → *What is clipped*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Long messages*; choices: `.loop/questions.md` → *Fix · long messages*.

## Codex CLI and OpenCode as session providers (added 2026-10-01)
- **D62 A session runs on Claude Code, Codex CLI or OpenCode; it can switch CLIs mid-session with a handover.** Developer request, 2026-10-01: "Add Codex CLI and OpenCode support. I don't want to install them now; I want them supported so I can configure them at any time." Built from the CLIs' source (Codex `rust-v0.159.3`, OpenCode `v1.18.34`), never run; tests drive fakes (`tools/fake-codex`, `tools/fake-opencode`); `docs/spike-providers.md` lists the probes for when they are installed.
  - **Design:** one seam. The supervisor keeps speaking Claude Code's stream-json; Codex (`codex app-server`, JSON-RPC over stdio) and OpenCode (`opencode serve` on a loopback port with a random password, HTTP + SSE) run behind bridges that translate both ways (`src/server/cli/`), so the recorder, status, D44 queue, D50 Stop, Inbox, chat and right panel are one code path. Claude Code's spawn is unchanged behind the same seam.
  - **Configure later (ruling):** Settings → **CLIs**: each CLI's command (`SWITCHBOARD_CODEX_BIN` / `SWITCHBOARD_OPENCODE_BIN`, overridable on the page; Claude Code's stays `SWITCHBOARD_CLAUDE_BIN`), version, sign-in (each CLI's own read-only check), models, and "Not installed" with the install commands and docs link. A missing or signed-out CLI cannot be chosen, with the reason; nothing else breaks.
  - **Per-session CLI (ruling):** the Simple and Full forms have a CLI row (default: the sidebar's default CLI); the model picker follows the CLI's models. `sessions.provider` (migration 0023; every older session is Claude Code), `session_providers` (each CLI's own conversation id), `provider_switches`.
  - **Switch mid-session (ruling):** the header's CLI switcher. With capacity (runnable, not out of usage per what the CLI reports) the outgoing agent writes a handover and is stopped; otherwise (or when that fails) the chat is exported under the data folder and the incoming agent reads it (and the outgoing CLI's own record) first. The incoming CLI starts in the same cwd with the handover as its first message; the chat shows "Switched from Claude Code to Codex CLI · handover by …". **Decided:** switching back reopens that CLI's own earlier conversation (Claude Code `--resume`, Codex `thread/resume`, OpenCode its session) with the handover of what happened meanwhile.
  - **Sidebar (ruling):** the footer's CLI label sets the default CLI and offers **Switch running sessions…** (the live sessions ticked; each handed over; progress per row, failures shown, the others go on); rows carry a CLI badge once the list mixes CLIs.
  - **Parity (ruling: "equivalent or marked"):** a capability matrix (`src/core/cli-providers.ts`, `docs/providers.md` → *Capability matrix*): chat, tool steps, images (PDFs as paths for Codex), permissions to the Inbox (with each CLI's own "always"), questions, Stop, pause / resume, model + effort, the context meter, Codex's usage windows in the footer, subagents, History moves (on request), the MCP page (Codex list / add / remove; OpenCode list), schedules with a CLI, peers. Remote Control, teleport, Workflow agents, background tasks and hooked terminal sessions are Claude Code's: their controls stay visible and are disabled with "Not available in <CLI>: <reason>".
  - Details: `docs/providers.md`, `docs/spike-providers.md`, `docs/fake-codex.md`, `docs/fake-opencode.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *CLI providers (D62)*, `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/security.md` → *Other CLIs (D62)*, `docs/database.md` (0023); choices where the request is silent: `.loop/questions.md` → *D62 · Codex CLI and OpenCode*.

## CLI accounts with automatic switching (added 2026-10-01)
- **D63 Each CLI can have several subscription logins ("account profiles"); a session that hits its session or weekly limit moves to the next account that has allowance, by itself.** Developer request, 2026-10-01: "Automatically switch to another account within the CLI once the session and/or weekly limit is hit. Two accounts with subscription-based allowance (not API). Fully controllable in Switchboard settings, for all CLIs. Sign in through the Switchboard UI." Built on D17 / D46 (usage readings), D62 (providers, handover, capacity) and D61 (the auth page opens in a new tab); lightweight: no new machinery beyond the profile store, the sign-in runner and one switcher.
  - **Profiles (ruling):** per CLI a priority-ordered list (name, folder, enabled, "same settings as Default"); the developer's existing login is the built-in **Default** (no folder override: never signed in / out, renamed, deleted or touched here). Other profiles get `<dataDir>/profiles/<cli>/<id>` (0700) and run with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` / `CODEX_HOME` / `XDG_DATA_HOME`. VERIFIED in the Claude Code 2.1.285 binary (read-only): the Keychain service name is suffixed by a hash of the config folder, so each folder holds its own subscription login. Stored in migration **0024** (`cli_profiles`; `sessions.profile_id` / `profile_pinned`; `usage_readings.profile_id`; an existing session is on its CLI's Default).
  - **Sign in / out from Settings (ruling):** the CLI's own login with the profile's folder (Claude Code `auth login --claudeai [--email]`; Codex `login [--device-auth]`; OpenCode through an `opencode serve` on the profile's data folder and its provider OAuth routes, or an API key sent to that server only). The page the CLI prints opens in a new tab (D61's pattern), the CLI's status command detects completion, 5 minutes at most, then the terminal command with Copy; a paired machine's profile offers the paste-back of the final redirect URL / code. **Switchboard never reads, stores or logs a token.**
  - **Rules, all in Settings → Accounts (ruling):** master and per-CLI toggles; the limit error always acts, and optionally earlier at thresholds (5-hour % and weekly % separately, default 98); after a reset "stay" or "switch back to the first profile"; new sessions: "first profile with allowance" or a fixed one; every profile spent: "stop and notify" (Inbox item, default) or "switch to another CLI" through the D62 handover. A profile known spent is never a target until its reset; periodic switches keep a cooldown; a CLI with one account is never touched.
  - **Per session (ruling):** an Account row in both New-session forms, a header picker (**Switch account**, asks first) and a **pin** that stops automatic switching, the header names the account.
  - **Switching mechanics (ruling):** stop, **copy** (not move) the CLI's own conversation to the new profile's folder (Claude Code: the transcript and its `subagents` folder, then `--resume` with the new `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; Codex: the rollout file, then `thread/resume`; OpenCode's storage is not copyable: the D62 handover into a session on the new data folder), the chat divider "Switched account: A → B (session limit, resets 14:05)", a "continue where you left off" message when a turn was interrupted. Fallback anywhere a conversation cannot be carried over: the D62 handover inside the same CLI.
  - **Usage per account:** Claude Code's poller and `get_usage` run per profile; the footer's bars are the active account's with a compact "Default 62% · Private 10%" line under them. Limit detection: the CLI's own limit text (parsed reset time) plus the readings.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D63 · CLI accounts* (the Default is read-only here, sharing on by default, Codex's login output and resume from a copied rollout unverified, OpenCode has no plan limits, History / Workflow runs look in the Default's folder).
  - Details: `docs/accounts.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *CLI accounts (D63)*, `docs/security.md` → *Accounts (D63)*, `docs/database.md` (0024), `docs/spike-providers.md` (probes), fakes' *Accounts* sections.

## Fix: hook waiter expiring (added 2026-10-02)
- **The hook waiter outlives idle stretches and a Switchboard restart; old installs show "outdated" with Update hooks.** Developer report, 2026-10-02: "I have to re-hook every time I reconnect to a remote session (by typing something in the console on the remote computer). Hooks were installed long ago and I still get 'No hook listening yet'." Ruling D48 said the waiter lives unlimited; it did not. Two causes:
  - **The CLI killed it.** `hookEntry` gave the waiter no `timeout`. VERIFIED on the 2.1.285 binary (read-only `strings`): an async / `asyncRewake` hook runs with `timeout = entry.timeout ? entry.timeout * 1000 : 600000`, a **10-minute default**, and the shell command's own timer kills the process when it fires; the CLI re-arms a waiter only at the next `SessionStart` / `Stop` (a turn), i.e. when the developer types. (`asyncTimeout || 15000` exists only in the registration log line; the 15 s is never reached because the timeout is always set.) The entry now carries `timeout: 604800` (7 days, `WAITER_HOOK_TIMEOUT_S`, ASSUMED waiter-timeout-7d). VERIFIED: the hook schema takes any positive number, with no maximum; the practical ceiling is `setTimeout`'s 2^31-1 ms (about 24.8 days; larger would fire at once), so 7 days is safe. A waiter killed after 7 days re-arms at the next turn like before.
  - **The script gave up.** `sb-hook.ts` did one long-poll and exited 0 on a refused / reset connection (Switchboard on that machine restarting or updating) or any non-message answer. It now **retries** (backoff 1 s doubling to a 30 s cap, reset after a connection that was held 30 s) on everything except the host's explicit stop, and exits when its CLI process is gone. **The explicit stop is `204`** (unchanged for old scripts): a newer waiter superseded it, the session ended (`SessionEnd`, the process gone), the session was **unhooked** (new: `unhooked()` answers its waiter 204), or it is not a session Switchboard hooks. Shutdown now answers **`503`** instead of 204, so a restarting host is not a stop. Exactly-once is untouched: messages are marked delivered before the waiter is answered, so a retried long-poll never takes one twice; the delivery rate limit and one-waiter-per-session are unchanged.
  - **Host answers that end a waiter, checked:** 204 (the stop cases above); `WOKEN_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` only gates when the next wake-up may be released (the waiter stays held); no server-side time limit on a held waiter.
- **Existing installs:** entries written by 1.5.0 lack the timeout, so the comparison (`switchboardHooksState`, which compares the whole entries) already reports them **outdated**; Settings → Machines shows "Terminal hooks: outdated (update them so idle sessions stay reachable)" with **Update hooks** (the same reinstall: backup first, only Switchboard's entries). A hooked session's header adds "Hooks are outdated on <machine> — Update hooks so idle sessions stay reachable" (`Session.hookStatus.hooksOutdated`, additive). A running terminal session picks the new entries up from the settings file; its current waiter keeps the old timeout until it re-arms at its next turn.
- **Status wording:** `hookStatus.delivery` gains `waiter-stopped` ("The hook stopped listening (it expired or Switchboard restarted) — it re-arms at the next turn; update the hooks to prevent this") next to `no-waiter` ("No hook listening yet — type anything in that terminal once (the hooks were installed after this session started)"). `waiter-stopped` = a waiter was held (or the session's hooks reported) since Switchboard started and none is now.
- Details: `docs/peers.md` → *Waiter lifetime*; choices: `.loop/questions.md` → *Fix · hook waiter expiring*.

## Standing instruction for agents (added 2026-10-02)
- **Every session's agent gets one standing instruction, on by default, editable in Settings.** Developer request, 2026-10-02. Problem: an agent sometimes asks a question about content it never wrote (a question about "the table above" when the table only existed in the model's head). Fixed at the source, with an instruction to every agent. The default text (short, because it costs tokens in every session): "Before you ask the user a question that refers to a proposal, table, list, plan or comparison, write that content out in a message first, then ask. Never refer to content 'above' that you have not actually written in this conversation."
- **Where it lives:** two rows of the existing `settings` table, `agents.standingInstruction` (text, at most 4,000 characters, empty passes nothing) and `agents.standingInstruction.enabled` (default `true`); no migration. UI: **Settings → Sessions & worktrees → Standing instruction for agents** (the instruction applies to every session whichever CLI runs it, so it sits with the session settings, not under Claude Code): a toggle, a multi-line field, **Save** and **Reset to default**; the note under it says a change applies to sessions started or resumed afterwards.
- **Read at every spawn** (`SessionSupervisor.#spawn`, `standingInstructionFor`), so new sessions, resumes, restart recovery, Attach here, moves, account switches (D63) and CLI switches (D62) all carry it, and a changed text reaches the next spawn without a restart.
- **Per CLI:** Claude Code `--append-system-prompt <text>` (one argv entry; VERIFIED in `claude --help` on 2.1.285); Codex `developerInstructions` on `thread/start` and `thread/resume` (ASSUMED D64-codex-developer-instructions: the field name is from the app-server's thread parameters as remembered, not run; no first-message fallback was needed because the field exists, `docs/spike-providers.md` lists the probe); OpenCode `system` in every `POST /session/:id/prompt_async` body (ASSUMED D64-opencode-system: the prompt body's `system` field, read from the API schema, not run). None of them goes through a user message, so the chat shows nothing extra and no hide marker is needed.
- **Not affected:** hooked terminal sessions (D48) and terminal conversations the developer runs themselves: Switchboard does not start those processes. "Attach here" and "Continue in Switchboard" do start one, so they get it like any resume.
- Details: `docs/settings.md`, `docs/supervisor.md`, `docs/providers.md` → *Standing instruction (D64)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Standing instruction for agents (D64)*.

## Taking a session over to another machine (added 2026-10-04)
- **D65 A session can be taken over from a paired machine and resumed on this one (or moved to one), with its conversation and its uncommitted work.** Developer request, 2026-10-04: "Once Switchboards are connected peer-to-peer, take over a session: e.g. a session running on my Windows PC is stopped there and resumed on my Mac." Works in both directions: a peer's session comes **to this machine** (**Take over to this machine**), this machine's session goes **to** a paired machine (**Move to <machine> ▸**). Built on D16 (the chat is imported from the copied transcript), D48 (the peer API, remote ids, hooked sessions), D62 (CLIs, the handover), D63 (account profiles, the transcript / rollout copy) and D44 (the first message's queue mark); lean: no new transport, every route is a local `/api` route on `PEER_API_ALLOW`.
  - **Code travels through git (ruling): a WIP push.** On the source, for every repo the session works in (its folder's repo; each worktree of a worktree session; each solution's repo of a workspace session): a temporary WIP commit of everything uncommitted (untracked files included, ignored ones not) on top of the session's branch tip, pushed to a **temporary remote branch** `switchboard/takeover/<session short id>/<branch>`. On the target: `git fetch` of that branch, the **real branch** checked out (or its worktree recreated next to the matched repo, same branch name), brought to the pushed tip (so unpushed commits travel), the WIP commit **undone** (`git reset --mixed`: the changes are uncommitted again, untracked files untracked again), then the temporary branch **deleted** from the remote. No upstream is needed (the temp branch carries everything); no changes means no WIP commit; the real branch is never pushed or forced, no other branch is touched. Every git command of both machines is in the run's log (shown in the dialog; never a credential).
  - **Missing repo (ruling): offer to clone.** Repos are matched to the target's saved folders (and the repos of their workspaces) by **normalised remote URL**. Without a match the dialog asks where to clone (default: next to the target's default folder), clones from the same remote and saves the clone as a folder (D14). A workspace session needs every repo resolved or cloned, else it stops before anything changes.
  - **Scope (ruling): Switchboard-run sessions and hooked terminal sessions (with a confirmation).** A hooked session's dialog says the terminal's `claude` on the source is stopped; after the conversation and the code are captured Switchboard stops exactly the process `claude agents --json` names for that session (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 10 s; Windows `taskkill /T`, `/F` after 10 s); the terminal window stays. The new session is a Switchboard-run one (origin `terminal`).
  - **Conversation:** Claude Code: the transcript and its `<id>/` folder (subagents, Workflow runs) are copied in chunks (512 KiB, sha256 per file, 200 MB cap) to `<config dir>/projects/<target cwd slug>/` and resumed with `--resume <same id>` on the target's account (D63 pick), the chat imported as D16 does. Codex: its rollout under `CODEX_HOME` + `thread/resume`, the D62 handover when the rollout is gone. OpenCode: the D62 handover. The agent's first message is a service message (D44 `resuming` mark): "This session moved from <machine> to <machine>. Paths changed: <from> → <to> (<repo>). The working tree was restored as it was." (plus the interrupted turn and any queued messages).
  - **Order and safety (ruling):** (1) checks on both sides → (2) stop the source session (D7 pause; hooked: after the capture) → (3) WIP push → (4) conversation copy → (5) target: fetch / checkout / reset, worktrees, clones → (6) resume on the target → (7) the source session is marked **moved** (closed, read-only, "Moved to <machine>" with a link, `reopen` refused) and the temp branches are deleted. A failure before (6) **rolls back**: the temp branches go, the target's checkouts / worktrees / clones / copied files are undone, and a Switchboard-run source session runs again; the source ends exactly as it was (its branch, index and working tree are never touched: the WIP commit is built outside them, ASSUMED D65-wip-plumbing). A failure after (6) keeps the take-over and reports a leftover temp branch with a one-click **Delete it**.
  - **UI:** the session header (**Take over to this machine** / **Move to <machine> ▸**) and the sidebar row's ⋯ menu open a dialog: the repos and what happens to each (branch, uncommitted files, unpushed commits, "use" / "clone first" with an editable path, blocked with the reason), the CLI and account on the target, the hooked-terminal warning with its confirmation; then one line per step; the new chat starts with the divider "Taken over from <machine>", the old session shows "Moved to <machine>" with a link.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D65 · Session take-over* (the WIP commit is built outside the branch, the reset is mixed, hooked stop order and signals, workspace clones, a clean target checkout, tombstones, Codex resume, submodules / LFS / line endings, no real CLI run).
  - Details: `docs/peers.md` → *Taking a session over (D65)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Take-over (D65)*, `docs/database.md` (0025), `docs/security.md` → *Take-over (D65)*.

## Footer usage grid (added 2026-10-04)
- **D66 The footer's usage is a compact grid: one line per account, each with its 5-hour and its weekly window.** Developer-approved design ("compact grid"), 2026-10-04: the D17 Session / Week / model rows (the active account's) and the D63 one-line account list under them become one grid, so every account's two windows are visible at once. Built on D17 (windows), D23 / D46 (pace), D62 P7 (Codex windows) and D63 (accounts, `accountUsage`); lean: no new route, no migration, two additive API fields.
  - **Layout:** under CPU / RAM (unchanged) a small header (`5h`, `Week`), then one fixed-height line per account: a `●` on the active account (the one new sessions of its CLI start on; shown only while that CLI has more than one), the label, and per window a 4 px mini-bar and its % (`—` while unknown, never derived from `pct`). A Claude Code account's bars keep the pace color and the allowance marker (`data-pace`) where the pace is known; Codex's have none (as before). A spent account (`exhaustedUntil` ahead) shows **out until HH:MM** across both bars. A click on a line opens **Settings → Accounts**. With a single account overall the grid is one line, so the footer is shorter than the D17 rows.
  - **Which lines:** Claude Code: each enabled account while it has more than one (`accountUsage`; the active one's bars are the meter's `usageWindows`), else one line from `usageWindows` named after that account (`activeAccounts`, e.g. **Default**; **Claude** only while unknown, as in demo mode), always listed (`—` before `/api/system` answers). Codex / OpenCode: **every** enabled account of a CLI with more than one (label `Codex <name>`, `—` for an unknown window), else one line from its `cliUsage` while it has any, named `Codex <name>` after its account (`Codex` while unknown); OpenCode reports no limits, so it has no line.
  - **Rulings (developer, 2026-10-04, on the first build's questions):** a single account's line shows its **profile name** (so `activeAccounts` was added); the `5h` / `Week` header **stays** with a single line; **every** account of a CLI with several gets a line, `— —` while its windows are unknown.
  - **Tooltip (`title`) per line:** each window's % and time to its reset (`5h: 62% · resets in 1h48`), its pace under it, each model's weekly limit in use (`Fable week: 93% · resets in 74h12`, an old reading `as of 25m`): these no longer get rows of their own.
  - **API (additive):** `AccountUsageRow.windows` (`session`, `week`, and for a Claude Code profile its model limits: the meter's `profileWindows`, the same reading rules as `usageWindows`, from that profile's own readings; a Codex profile's from the windows its sessions reported) and `CliUsageWindow.key` (`session` up to 10 hours, `week` longer, `model` when the CLI did not say how long). and `SystemInfo.activeAccounts` (`{ cli, profileId, name }` per CLI with an enabled account, Claude Code and Codex, also with a single one). Existing fields unchanged; `accountUsage` is still sent only while a CLI has more than one enabled account.
  - **ASSUMED:** the `●` shows only for a CLI with more than one account; a single Codex account's line is `Codex <name>` like the several-account ones (`Codex Default`).
  - Details: `docs/accounts.md` → *Usage per account*, `docs/usage.md` → *Footer*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *CLI accounts (D63)*.

## Session todo lists (added 2026-10-04)
- **D68 Every session has a todo list that the agent fills when the developer says "add it to the todo list", and that the developer keeps in the session and on a Todos page.** Developer request, 2026-10-04: a list per session of things that still need doing, created and changed by the agent on request. Rulings: the agent works the list through **MCP tools**; the list is a **strip above the composer**; a done item is **removed one hour after it was ticked, or earlier on request**; there is **also a Todos page**. Built on D64 (standing instruction), D61 (MCP), D62 (provider bridges), D48 (peer proxy) and D65 (take-over); lean: one migration, one service, one stdio helper, no new dependency.
  - **Storage:** migration **0026** `session_todos` (text, open / done, added by developer / agent, position, created / updated, `done_at`; cascades with the session). Text 1–1,000 characters; at most 200 items per session.
  - **Agent tools (ruling: MCP):** every session Switchboard starts or resumes gets the built-in `switchboard` MCP server (`src/hook/sb-mcp.ts`, a dependency-free stdio helper run by Switchboard's Node) with `todo_list`, `todo_add`, `todo_update`, `todo_done` (also reopens), `todo_remove`, calling `/agent/v1/todos` on loopback. Scoped by a per-session agent token (HMAC of the session id under the install secret): it opens that session's list only. Injected through each CLI's own mechanism, added to the developer's MCP config and never writing their files: Claude Code `--mcp-config <0600 file in the data folder>` + `--allowedTools mcp__switchboard` (VERIFIED by one real probe: the server connects with its five tools), Codex `-c mcp_servers.switchboard.*` with `env_vars`, OpenCode `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` `mcp.switchboard` (both UNVERIFIED: not installed). Hooked terminal sessions get no injection: their list is the UI's only. The D61 MCP page does not list it.
  - **Standing instruction:** the D64 default gains "Todo list: when asked to add to it, use the switchboard todo tools; mark items done when finished; check it when asked what's left." A stored earlier default reads as the new one; an edited text stays the developer's (ASSUMED D68-instruction-edited).
  - **Done items (ruling):** stay visible struck through under a collapsed **Done (n)**, and are removed **1 hour after they were ticked** by a server-side timer and a sweep at start (from the stored `done_at`, so a restart keeps the hour), earlier by ✕ or **Clear done**; unticking before the hour cancels it.
  - **Session UI (ruling: strip above the composer):** **TODO (n)** collapsible strip: tick, edit inline, ↑ ↓, ✕, who added it (`you` / `agent`), **Add a todo…**, **Done (n)** and **Clear done**; live through the `todosChanged` hub event. Empty: no strip, a small **+ Todo** pill above the composer's top edge (out of the flow, ASSUMED D68-empty-add).
  - **Sidebar and Todos page (ruling: also a page):** a session row shows **☐ n** open items; the nav gains **Todos** (after History, like D61's MCP) with the total open count; `/todos` lists every open session's open items grouped by session (title, machine, where it works), done ones behind **Show done**, ticking there too; a title or an item opens the session.
  - **Peers and take-over:** a paired machine's lists are read and edited through the peer proxy (`r~<machine>~<id>`), its groups listed on the Todos page as last known; the list travels with a take-over (`SourceInspect.todos`, re-created before the agent's first turn).
  - **API (additive):** `GET/POST /api/sessions/{id}/todos`, `PUT/DELETE …/todos/{todoId}`, `POST …/todos/clear-done`, `PUT …/todos/order`, `GET /api/todos`, the agent's `/agent/v1/todos[/{todoId}]`, `Session.openTodoCount`, `/hub` `todosChanged`.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D68 · Session todos*.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Session todos (D68)*, `docs/database.md` (0026), `docs/security.md` → *Agent todo tools (D68)*, `docs/spike-providers.md` → *Todo tools (D68)*.
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-19 (a directory scan reported tools without all four hints):** every tool of the `switchboard` server (the always-on todo / artifact / loop tools and the Team, Plans and Project memory ones) now declares `title`, `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint` and `openWorldHint` as boolean literals both in its definition (`src/core/*`, no shared spread or computed hints) and where it is registered, so a static scanner resolves all four. Hints reviewed against the handlers: `plan_start` is open-world (the plan's runs work in git repositories and merge pull requests) and idempotent (a started plan is refused), `team_propose_charter` and `memory_propose` are idempotent (an open proposal is replaced; a near-identical one is refused). `docs/mcp.md` → *Tool annotations*; test `tests/server/todos/tool-annotations.test.ts`.
  - **Note 2026-10-05:** since 1.11 the helper is built on the official MCP TypeScript SDK (`McpServer.registerTool`, zod schemas; `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` and `zod` are runtime dependencies), so "no new dependency" and "dependency-free" above no longer hold for it; see `docs/todos.md` → *The agent's tools*.
- **D69 A todo item has a title, a description for the developer and a handover plan for an AI agent, shown as cards; ▶ Start puts it into the message box.** Developer request and approved spec, 2026-10-04, on top of D68. Rulings: **three fields** per item; **cards as sketched** (strip and Todos page); **▶ Start fills the composer** and never sends; the **agent fills all three** from the conversation when asked to add something. One migration, no new dependency.
  - **Storage:** migration **0027** renames `session_todos.text` to `title` and adds `description` and `plan` (nullable). Title 1–120 characters, one line; description ≤ 4,000 and plan ≤ 8,000 characters, Markdown, blank = none. An existing text with a line break or over 120 characters keeps all of it as the description, its first line (cut to 119 + `…`) as the title.
  - **API (additive, compatible):** items carry `title`, `description`, `plan`, and still `text` (= title); `text` is accepted as the title's alias on input, so a 1.7.0 peer keeps working through the peer proxy (D48; it drops the description and plan). New agent route `GET /agent/v1/todos/{todoId}`. A take-over carries the three fields; a 1.7.0 source's text is split like 0027.
  - **Agent tools:** six: `todo_list` (compact: id, state, title, which notes exist), **`todo_get`** (one item in full), `todo_add` {title, description?, plan?}, `todo_update` {id, title?, description?, plan?}, `todo_done`, `todo_remove`, with JSON-schema descriptions that say what each field is for (description: for the human, plain and brief; plan: for an agent picking it up without this conversation: context, files, steps, acceptance criteria). The MCP `instructions` and the D64 default standing instruction tell the agent to fill all three (1.7.0's default counts as an earlier default; an edited text stays the developer's; the "short" bound went from 400 to 450 characters). Codex / OpenCode injection unchanged apart from the tool list (still UNVERIFIED).
  - **Cards (ruling: as sketched):** the strip's header **▾ TODO · 3 open · 1 done**, a thin progress bar, **+ Add**; each item a card: round box, bold title, the description (D20 Markdown) muted and clamped to two lines, who added it and its age, a **⋯** menu (Edit, Move up, Move down, Delete), **▸ Handover plan** when there is one, **▶ Start**; a click opens the card in place (full description and plan). Edit and + Add use one inline form (title, description, plan behind "Add handover plan"; Esc cancels, ⌘ / Ctrl + Enter saves). Done cards: filled box, struck-through title, `removed in 42m`, under **Done (n)** with **Clear done**. Empty: the **+ Todo** pill stays. The Todos page shows the same cards under a session header with **Open session**.
  - **▶ Start (ruling: fills the composer):** `Work on todo [<id>]: <title>`, a blank line, the plan (else the description), focused, not sent; a draft already there is kept and the message added after it, no dialog (ruling after review). On the Todos page it opens the session first.
  - **Review rulings (2026-10-04, after the screenshots):** opening a card or its plan keeps its title row in view; **▸ Handover plan** sits above the plan; a card without a plan has **▶ Start** on its title row; the expanded strip is at most ~a third of the window (32vh); the form's Save / Cancel always stay in view; **+ Add stays open** after a save (cleared, title focused; Esc / Cancel closes it); **done cards have no Edit**; the 447-character default standing instruction is kept.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D69 · Todo cards*.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Title, description and plan (D69)*, *In the session*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Todo title, description and handover plan (D69)*; `docs/database.md` (0027).
- **D70 A todo item has a priority (light tints) and an estimate, and its handover plan is mandatory.** Developer-approved spec, 2026-10-04, on top of D68 / D69. Rulings: **the plan is mandatory**, with **`No plan` allowed** (an agent writes `No plan: <one-line reason>`); **four priorities, Urgent / High / Medium / Low, shown with light tints** (a 3px left edge, a barely visible tint, a small label; done cards untinted); **open items sort by priority, the manual order applies within a level** (Move up / down stays within it); **the UI's add form is prefilled** (`No plan`, Medium, no estimate) so adding stays quick; **the agent may revise priority and estimate** with `todo_update` when it learns more; **the estimate is the AI agent's development time in minutes** (how long an AI agent would take, rough is fine). One migration, no new dependency.
  - **Storage:** migration **0028** adds `session_todos.priority` (NOT NULL, default and existing items `medium`, CHECK the four) and `estimate_minutes` (nullable INTEGER, CHECK 1–10,080; existing items NULL), and sets `plan = 'No plan'` where it was NULL or blank. Titles and descriptions (0027's split of long texts) are untouched.
  - **Compatibility (conservative):** a create without a plan (any route: an older UI, a 1.7.0 / 1.8.0 peer through the proxy, an older agent tool) stores `No plan`, without a priority `medium`, without an estimate none: never a 422 for them.
