Only the agent's MCP tool schema requires plan, priority and estimate (and the stdio helper refuses a `todo_add` without them, saying what is missing). An update can change the plan but not empty it (422). Older peers' items read as medium / none / `No plan`; a take-over carries the new fields.
  - **Agent tools:** `todo_add` {title, description?, plan, priority, estimate_minutes}, `todo_update` adds priority / estimate_minutes; descriptions say how to judge the priority (urgent = blocking or breaking now: production down, data loss, blocks other work; high = should be next; medium = normal; low = nice-to-have / cleanup) and what the estimate is. `todo_list` lines carry `HIGH ~45m` (`~?` when not estimated), in priority order; `todo_get` adds Priority and Estimate. The MCP `instructions` and the D64 default standing instruction say to fill all fields and revise priority and estimate (1.8.0's default counts as an earlier default; the "short" bound went from 450 to 510: the text is 502 characters).
  - **UI:** card label + `~45m` / `~2h` / `~1h 30m`; header `3 open · ~2h 15m · 1 done` (`+` when some open items have no estimate, nothing when none has one); the Todos page shows each session's total. The edit / add form gains a Priority select and an Estimate field and always shows the required plan; open cards' ⋯ menu gains a **Priority ▸** submenu for one-click changes (ARIA menu with radio items; → / ← / Esc / Enter; strip and Todos page). `No plan` is not offered as a handover plan (no **▸ Handover plan**, ▶ Start sends the description).
  - **Review rulings (2026-10-04, after the report):** an emptied plan on update stays refused (422, Save disabled); the strip header keeps the done count; the agent helper keeps refusing `todo_add` without plan, priority and estimate; **add a ⋯ → Priority submenu** alongside the form select.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D70 · Todo priority and estimate*.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Priority, estimate and the mandatory plan (D70)*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Todo priority, estimate and the mandatory plan (D70)*; `docs/database.md` (0028).

- **D71 Shared sidebar layout: a paired machine's sessions go into folders and subfolders and are re-ordered; loose sessions get a manual order; the folder structure is synced peer-to-peer.** Developer request, 2026-10-05: drag and drop remote sessions into folders / subfolders and re-order them; sync the folder structure between paired machines. Rulings: **the bug is "no drop indicator"** (a paired machine's session dropped on a folder showed nothing and did nothing); **loose sessions are manually orderable**; **a shared two-way layout** (the same pins, folders, subfolders and order on both machines; one session is one item on both); **live** while connected, **last write wins per item**, **offline catch-up** on reconnect, sessions known on one side only keep their place (hidden where unknown); **on / off per paired machine**; **always merge on first sync** (union; same-named folders at the same level combined); forgetting a machine stops the sync and keeps the layout; an older peer: sync unavailable ("update <machine> to sync folders"), nothing breaks.
  - **Fix (M1):** not reproduced in a fresh two-machine world, in Chromium or WebKit (drop on a folder, subfolder, row, Pinned, the ⋯ menu, a busy or reconnecting peer, the session open on screen: all worked; the server takes peer ids). Found and fixed instead: the SESSIONS list relied on the browser's drag auto-scroll, which WebKit (Safari, the "Add to Dock" app) does not do for a scrolling box, and a paired machine's sessions are always listed last, so in a list taller than its box a folder was out of reach for them: the list now scrolls itself while a drag is held at its edge (every engine). The peer session cache re-sorted oldest first on each update (rows jumped against the newest-first refresh). Regression E2E with two servers. The developer's exact steps remain an open question (`.loop/questions.md`).
  - **Loose order (M2):** the loose list is the unplaced sessions (service order, newest first, so a new session shows at the top), then the stored loose order (`SidebarLayout.loose`); dropping before / after a loose row or Move up / down places a session at that position of the whole list (the unplaced ones above it get their places); Unpin / Out of the folder = unplaced again.
  - **Storage:** migration **0029** rebuilds `sidebar_folders` / `sidebar_places` as records with order keys (base-36 strings, a new key between the neighbours' for a move) and hybrid-clock timestamps; folders keep tombstones; no foreign keys (a peer's folder may name a parent not here yet). Existing rows keep their places (clock `''` = older than any).
  - **Sync (M3):** per paired machine **off until switched on** (Settings → Machines → *Share sidebar layout*; existing pairings too); shared only when both machines are on (one side on = *waiting*). One peer route, `POST /peer/v1/sidebar` (pairing auth, Tailscale-only listener; nothing added to the API allow-list or the event stream): a full exchange when switched on and at every (re)connect, the changed records live after each change; merges change nothing more than the records. Sessions keyed `<origin machine>:<session id>`; folders by UUID. Last write wins per folder name / folder place / deletion / session place. **Collapsed stays per machine** (not synced). First merge: union, same-named siblings combined into the smaller id (top-down), a session placed differently takes its later placement. Older peer → 404 → *unsupported*.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D71 · Shared sidebar layout*.
  - Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Loose order (D71)*, *Shared layout (D71)*; `docs/peers.md` → *Shared sidebar layout (D71)*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Loose order and shared sidebar layout (D71)*; `docs/database.md` (0029).

## Continuing hooked sessions in Switchboard (added 2026-10-05)
- **D72 A hooked terminal session can continue in Switchboard: it becomes a Switchboard-run session in place.** Developer report, 2026-10-05: a Windows PC terminal `claude` session was hooked into Switchboard; even with the terminal window closed there was no way to make it a normal (supervised) Switchboard session: History does not offer D16's **Continue in Switchboard** because the conversation is already a (hooked) Switchboard session, and the hooked session itself had no such action. **Ruling: build it as described.**
  - **Where:** the hooked session's header (in the note that says what stays in the terminal), its sidebar row's ⋯ menu, and its History row: **Continue in Switchboard**.
  - **Terminal already gone** (judged from the existing liveness facts: `claude agents --json`, the hook's reported pid, SessionEnd; "unknown" is refused, never taken as gone): converted **in place**: the same record (id, title, sidebar place, todo list, history, attachments, account / profile) stops being hooked and is resumed as a supervised session on the same machine with `--resume <claudeSessionId>` in its cwd and the normal injections (standing instruction, the `switchboard` MCP todo tools, the account's env). The chat shows the divider "Continued in Switchboard (was a terminal session)". Armed waiters and held PermissionRequest calls are released (no decision). Messages still in the mailbox go to the new process as one message; **ruling (2026-10-05, D72-pending): a message handed to the terminal's waiter that never appeared in the transcript is shown as not sent, with a one-click Resend** (it is not re-sent automatically).
  - **Terminal still running:** the take-over's kind of confirmation ("its `claude` in the terminal will be stopped"); confirmed, D65's terminal stop (SIGTERM / `taskkill`, the force after 10 s) runs, Switchboard waits (bounded, 15 s) until it is gone, then continues as above. A failed stop changes nothing and shows the reason.
  - **Peers:** a paired machine's hooked session offers the same action; it runs on that machine through the peer API (the route is on `PEER_API_ALLOW`, D48's model), remote ids `r~<machine>~<id>`.
  - **Closed (ruling 2026-10-05, D72-closed):** a closed (unhooked) hooked session offers it directly; continuing reopens it (D33) and then converts it.
  - **Codex / OpenCode:** hooked sessions are Claude Code only; nothing to do there. **No migration** (`sessions.hooked` flips to 0, the process fields are set).
  - Details: `docs/peers.md` → *Continuing a hooked session in Switchboard (D72)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Continue a hooked session in Switchboard (D72)*; assumptions `.loop/questions.md` → *D72 · Continue a hooked session*.

## Devices: phones and tablets (added 2026-10-08)
- **D73 Paired phones and tablets reach Switchboard over Tailscale through `tailscale serve` (HTTPS) and a second loopback listener, each with its own revocable credential; web push on them.** Developer request, 2026-10-08: "Make Switchboard fully responsive so I can use it on mobile devices and tablets" (the layout is D74, a parallel lane). Rulings: **access = Tailscale + device pairing** (an optional listener reachable over Tailscale, **off by default**; each device paired individually with a QR code + one-time code and given its own revocable credential; nothing on the LAN or the internet; loopback keeps working exactly as now); **web push = yes** (permission requests, questions, finished turns and other Inbox items worth a ping, on paired devices).
  - **Transport (chosen: `tailscale serve` → loopback device listener):** a listener on the Tailscale IP with plain HTTP is no secure context (no install, no push on iOS); `tailscale serve --bg --https=<port> http://127.0.0.1:<device port>` gives a real `*.ts.net` certificate while Switchboard keeps binding only 127.0.0.1. Device listener default **13003**, HTTPS port default **8443** (ruling 2026-10-08: kept; it leaves a 443 the developer may serve untouched; 443 / 10000 selectable). Switchboard turns off only the serve entry it made; a port served by something else is reported, not overwritten. Missing MagicDNS / HTTPS certificates / Serve consent are shown in Settings with what to enable. The UI listener (13001) is unchanged.
  - **Security:** the device listener never trusts loopback (all its traffic comes from `tailscaled` on 127.0.0.1): its requests are marked by the listener (a Symbol, not a header) and go through their own guard: Host / Origin = the devices' origin, `/hook` and `/agent` refused, the device credential (`__Host-sb_device` cookie, HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, 400 days refreshed on use; sha256 stored; the `Tailscale-User-Login` recorded at pairing checked when present), unpaired → only the pairing page, its exchange and the app files. Codes: 10 minutes, single use, 5 wrong tries, 10 attempts per 10 minutes overall. Revoke closes the device's sockets (its `/hub` stream).
  - **Device allow-list (ruling 2026-10-08, orchestrator recommendation approved):** a device's `/api` request is refused (403 `local-only`) unless its method + path are on `DEVICE_ALLOWED` (the work a phone needs: sessions, Inbox, todos, sidebar, history + Continue, schedules and loops, the UI's reads and preferences, its own push settings, peer-proxied equivalents through `/api/machines/{id}/api/<rest>` judged as `/api/<rest>`); the rest is listed in `DEVICE_REFUSED` (devices, machines administration, hooks, MCP edits, updates, Start at login, CLIs, accounts, tools, frame helper, folders writes and setup, take-over (stays desktop-only), attach / detach, teleport, isolate, reindex, test hooks). A test enumerates every registered route and fails on an unclassified `/api` route.
  - **Push:** VAPID keys `<dataDir>/vapid.json` (0600); RFC 8291 `aes128gcm` + RFC 8292 VAPID with **Node's crypto, no dependency** (checked against RFC 8291's example vector and an independent decrypting fake push service). Per-device opt-in and toggles (permission, questions, turn finished, session errors, other Inbox items); iOS needs the Home Screen app (explained). Endpoints only on the browser vendors' push services. 404 / 410 remove the subscription. **Paired machines included (ruling 2026-10-08):** a peer's questions, permission requests and its sessions' finished turns / errors notify this machine's devices through the existing peer stream (no echo), each item once and only while fresh, linking `r~<machine>~<id>`. HTTPS port stays 8443 (ruling). Nothing is sent while device access is off. `PRIVACY.md` updated.
  - **PWA:** the same manifest / icons / service worker on the device origin (the worker gains `push`, `notificationclick`, `pushsubscriptionchange`).
  - **Storage:** migration **0030** (`devices`, `device_pairing_codes`, `device_push_subscriptions`).
  - **AGENTS.md** loopback rule amended (approved by this ruling) to describe the device listener and its auth.
  - **Peers:** a device talks to this machine only; a paired machine's sessions show through the existing peer proxy as on the desktop.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D73 · Devices*.
  - **Fix (developer bug report, 2026-10-13: "the mobile phone loses the binding to Switchboard"):** the device cookie is `SameSite=Lax`, no longer `Strict`. The log showed the paired phone's page load answered 302 → `/pair` while the very next request from the same page carried a valid credential: the browser leaves a `Strict` cookie off a page load started outside the site (a notification tap, a link from another app, the installed app's launch). Cross-site POSTs, fetches and frames still get no cookie, and the Origin check is unchanged. `docs/devices.md` → *Why the cookie is Lax*.
  - **Fix (developer bug report, 2026-10-13: "notifications stopped after re-pairing"):** a push subscription belongs to one device record, so re-pairing a phone left Switchboard with none while the browser kept the permission and its subscription, and the phone only subscribed on a tap of the switch. A paired device's page now sends its existing subscription back by itself (at start, so also right after pairing, on becoming visible, and in Settings → Devices) when the permission is granted, the subscription was made with the current VAPID key, notifications were not turned off on purpose (Disable unsubscribes the browser and sets a local marker; Enable clears it), and the server stores none for the device or another endpoint (`GET /api/device` gains `pushEndpoint`, additive). No prompt and no new subscribe; the restored subscription gets the default toggles. A subscription for another key is left to Enable and shown as off. `docs/devices.md` → *Notifications* → *Restoring the subscription*.
  - **Gone endpoints (developer ruling 2026-10-13: "remember it"):** an endpoint the push service answered 404 / 410 for is remembered (sha256 only, one `settings` row `devices.push.gone` for all devices, since re-pairing makes a new device record; newest 200, each for 90 days; no migration) and `PUT /api/device/push` refuses it from then on with 409 `push-gone`. On that refusal the page unsubscribes the dead subscription, notes its endpoint locally (`switchboard.push.gone`) and stops; Settings shows notifications as off, and Enable subscribes anew (a new endpoint, always taken). `pushEndpoint` stays exposed to the device itself, and the off marker survives re-pairing (both confirmed).
  - Details: `docs/devices.md`, `docs/security.md` → *Device listener (D73)*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Devices (D73)*, `docs/database.md` (0030).

## Responsive layout (added 2026-10-08)
- **D74 Switchboard is fully responsive: phones and tablets as well as the desktop.** Developer request, 2026-10-08: "Make Switchboard fully responsive so I can use it on mobile devices and tablets too." Rulings: **everything responsive in the first version** (all pages and dialogs, Settings included); **long-press drag on touch too** (besides the ⋯ menus) to move sessions into folders and re-order them in the sidebar.
  - **Breakpoints:** desktop ≥ 1280 px unchanged (pixel-identical: the visual oracle's specs pass unchanged; compact-only elements are mounted only on compact layouts, touch rules key on `(pointer: coarse)`); tablet 768–1279 px; phone ≤ 767 px; the session header's ⋯ menu below 1024 px (`src/web/shell/viewport.ts`, the CSS in `src/web/styles/responsive/`).
  - **Shell:** a top app bar (☰, page title, Inbox with its count); the sidebar a slide-over drawer (D41's slide; full-screen on phones) that closes on a pick, its scrim, Escape or a dialog; the session's right panel a drawer from the right (tablet) or a bottom sheet (phone), opened from the header. On the compact layouts D41's state is the drawers' (closed at first, not saved); the stored desktop choice is untouched.
  - **Session view:** a condensed header (☰ with the Inbox count, dot, title, panel button, ⋯ holding the actions: pickers, Close, Remote, take-over, Pause, Continue in terminal); chips and tabs scroll sideways; the composer pinned at the bottom above the keyboard (`100dvh`, `visualViewport`, safe areas); quick replies on one scrolling line; code blocks, diffs and tables scroll inside themselves.
  - **Pages:** list → detail on phones (Inbox, Solutions, Settings via `/settings` → `/settings/<section>`, with ‹ Back); tables become cards (Schedules, Artifacts, History, Settings' scan and schedules); page heads wrap. **Dialogs:** full-screen sheets on phones (the Close confirmation a bottom sheet, the palette keeps a strip of backdrop), centered and fitted on tablets; New session (Full) stacks its side column under the form; the setup wizard's rail becomes a row. Menus and toasts stay in the window.
  - **Touch (coarse pointer):** tap targets ≥ 44 × 44 px; a sidebar row's / folder's ⋯ always shown, the row's hover-only × replaced by **Close** in its ⋯ menu; a long press shows a `title` tooltip; fields ≥ 16 px. **Long-press drag:** a ~400 ms hold without moving lifts a session row or folder; a finger that moves first scrolls and never drags; the drop targets and indicators are the mouse drag's (`resolveDrop`, `indicatorOf`), with edge auto-scroll; the mouse drag is unchanged. `prefers-reduced-motion` drops the slides.
  - **Follow-up (developer bug report, 2026-10-08):** on the desktop with the right panel open the header's actions ran under the panel (the rules keyed on the window's width only). The header now measures its own width: long labels shorten first ("Move ▸"), then actions move into a ⋯ menu (Move, Continue in terminal, Remote, Close first; the pickers and Pause last); the tabs scroll and the chips wrap inside the column. Unchanged when everything fits. `docs/responsive.md` → *Header actions in a narrow session column*.
  - No new dependency, no migration, no API change. Details: `docs/responsive.md`; assumptions `.loop/questions.md` → *D74 · Responsive*.

## Todo progress (added 2026-10-08)
- **D75 ▶ Start sends the todo's message; a todo can be in progress; the agent marks it done and is reminded once.** Developer request, 2026-10-08: "When clicking Start on a todo item, it's fine to just send the message (instead of me clicking Start and then Send). It would be nice if we could follow the status of the todo in the sense of 'in progress' too." On top of D68 / D69 / D70 (supersedes D69's "▶ Start fills the composer and never sends").
  - **▶ Start sends (ruling 1):** the start message (`todoStartMessage`) goes to the session as a normal user message: queued while the agent is busy (D44 / D50), the composer and its draft untouched. On the Todos page it sends without opening the session and shows a small toast with **Open**. Peers' sessions through the proxy; a hooked terminal session's message through its mailbox like any message (the existing send path; refusals are shown and the item is put back). New route `POST /api/sessions/{id}/todos/{todoId}/start` (on the peer and device allow-lists).
  - **In progress (ruling 2):** a third state `in_progress` (open → in progress → done; in progress → open; done → open). Set by ▶ Start, by the agent's new **`todo_start`** tool, and by hand (⋯ → **Mark in progress** / **Mark not started**). Several at once; **priority order kept** (not moved to the top). Look: ◐ half-filled box, an **IN PROGRESS** label next to the priority, a subtle pulsing left edge while the session works (none with `prefers-reduced-motion`); a tick marks it done; phones included. Counts: `1 in progress · 2 open · ~2h · 1 done`; the sidebar's ☐, the Todos nav and page counts include in progress; the estimate total counts open + in progress. `todo_list` / `todo_get` show it; REST, peers (an older peer's item reads as open) and take-over carry it.
  - **The agent must mark it done (ruling):** the start message ends with "When it's finished, mark it done with todo_done [<id>]; if you stop before it's finished, say what's left."; the `todo_start` / `todo_done` descriptions, `AGENT_MCP_INSTRUCTIONS` and the default standing instruction say to mark an item in progress when starting it and done when finishing it, always (the 1.9–1.11 default reads as the new one); and when a turn ends while an item started in that session (▶ Start or `todo_start`) is still in progress and the agent did not touch it in that turn, Switchboard sends **one** reminder for it, at most once per item per start (recorded), never for a hooked session without a waiter. A setting, **Settings → Sessions → Remind the agent to finish started todos** (`sessions.todoReminder`), **default on** (choice recorded here).
  - **Storage (ruling 3):** the state column had a CHECK, so **one** migration, **0031**, rebuilds `session_todos` with `in_progress` and adds `started_at`, `started_by` (start / agent / developer), `reminded_at`.
  - **Devices (ruling 4):** the new route is allowed for paired devices (normal use).
  - **Fix on the way:** Esc in a todo card's ⋯ menu stopped the running turn (D50's window-level Esc did not see the menu); it now only closes the menu, which matters now that ▶ Start begins turns.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D75 · Todo progress*.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *In progress (D75)*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Todo in progress and ▶ Start that sends (D75)*; `docs/database.md` (0031); `docs/settings.md` (`sessions.todoReminder`).
## Todo runs, board and actuals (added 2026-10-08)
- **D76 A todo can run in its own session; its run's work waits in review.** (D102, 2026-10-14: the `review` state is gone with review cards; a run item marked done is done.) Developer request, 2026-10-08 (relayed with the rulings by the orchestrator): run a todo item in a new supervised session. Rulings (2026-10-08):
  - **Where:** the card's ⋯ → **Run in new session**, and a multi-select on the strip / the Todos page / the board → **Run N in new sessions**.
  - **Where it runs:** a new worktree on branch **`todo/<slug>`** (from the title; unique) **cut from the source session's current branch**, in the same repo / folder; a source that is not in a git repository runs in the same folder without a worktree, and the UI says so. **Ruling 2026-10-08 (D76-workspace):** a source in a **workspace** folder gets a `todo/<slug>` worktree **in each solution repo it uses** (its `solutions`), each cut from that repo's current branch, working at the workspace root like a Full start with worktrees per solution (a source with router answers starts a Full run with the same answers; a simple one a simple run with a worktree note); only a source with no solution repo runs in place, with the note. Same CLI, model / effort and account as the source, computed by one small pure function **`todoRunOptions`** (`src/server/todos/run-options.ts`; D82's rules route it since the 1.13 integration). Title = the item's title; first message = the item's ▶ Start message (D75, with the "mark it done" line).
  - **Link:** the item records `runSessionId`, the session `todoLink { sourceSessionId, todoId }`; the run's agent token reaches that one item (get, start, update, done) and nothing else of the source's list; the item goes in progress when the run starts. The card shows the run session (link, live status); the sidebar shows it normally. Peers: a paired machine's item runs on that machine through the proxy.
  - **Review state** (shared contract with lane B's review queue): a fourth state `review` (migration **0032**, a table rebuild like 0031). An item **with a run** marked done (by its run's agent or the developer) becomes `review`. Lane B publishes **`reviewResolved`** `{ sessionId, outcome: 'merged' | 'committed' | 'discarded' | 'sent-back' | 'dismissed' }` (type `ReviewResolvedEvent`, `src/core/reviews.ts`, kept minimal; the merge reconciles the two definitions); merged / committed / dismissed → done, discarded → open, sent-back → in progress.
  - **Choice recorded (discarded):** the link is **kept as history** and the run marked `discarded` (`run_state`), not cleared: the card still links the discarded run ("Run (discarded)"), the item can run again, and done no longer means review for it.
  - **Run sessions are reminded (confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08):** the D75 finish reminder of a run item goes to the run session.
  - **Choices recorded (orchestrator brief open points):** "done → review" applies to an item whose run is **active** (also the source session's own agent's or the developer's tick), and review → done is done; review counts as neither open nor done (`reviewCount`); the finish reminder (D75) of a run item goes to the run session (`startedBy: run`), not the source; 409 `already-running` while the item's run session is open and active; a take-over carries an item in review as done.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Run in a new session (D76)*, *Review (D76)*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Todo run in a new session, review, board and actuals (D76, D77, D78)*; `docs/database.md` (0032); assumptions `.loop/questions.md` → *D76–D78 · Todo runs*.
- **D77 The Todos page has a board.** (D102: its Review column went with review cards; the columns are Open · In progress · Done.) Developer request, 2026-10-08 (relayed): a Kanban view of the todos. Rulings: a **List | Board** toggle (remembered); columns **Open · In progress · Review · Done** across all sessions (and the paired machines'); the cards as now (compact), labelled by session; filters priority, machine, folder, session, and a search; **drag a card between columns changes its state**; within a column priority then position; mouse drag + D74's long-press touch drag; ⋯ menu alternatives.
  - **Drag semantics (recorded):** to **In progress** marks it in progress **without sending** anything (▶ Start sends); to **Done** means done (`skipReview`), also for an item with a run; into **Review** only for an item with a run session; to **Open** reopens. ⋯ → **Move to ▸** is the keyboard / no-drag alternative.
  - **Phones (ruling 2026-10-08): swipeable columns**: one column per screen width (scroll snap) with a tab strip that follows the swipe and can be tapped; cards move with ⋯ → Move to, and (simple enough, so built) by a long-press drag onto a column tab. Tablets and the desktop unchanged.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Board (D77)*.
- **D78 Actual vs. estimate.** Developer request, 2026-10-08 (relayed): record what an item really took. Rulings: the actual duration (first in progress → done or review, **excluding time back in open**) and the tokens of the session(s) working on it during that span (the run session's if it has one, else the source session's; from the existing per-turn token data; approximate, documented); on the card `est ~45m · took 32m · 41k tokens`; per-session totals of actual vs. estimate for done items on the Todos page / board; a short calibration line in `todo_add` / `todo_update` (and `todo_list`) once ≥ 3 completed items have estimates; storage in 0032.
  - **Choices recorded:** tokens per turn = the `result` line's `usage` `input + cache creation + output` (cache reads left out; **confirmed by the developer 2026-10-08**), stored on the result event as `tokens`; time in review does not count; a `todo_actuals` table keeps the completed items' records so totals and calibration outlive the done hour; calibration = summed actual time / summed estimates over the last 10 (this session's with ≥ 3, else the folder's), "about right" within 1.15×.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Actual vs. estimate (D78)*.

## Review queue (added 2026-10-08)
- **D79 A session with changes gets a Review card when it goes idle; the card merges, opens a PR, commits, sends back or discards.** **Superseded by D102 (2026-10-14): review cards were removed.** The record below is kept as history. Developer rulings, 2026-10-08 (lane B). Reviews are **post-hoc and advisory**: they never block an agent (agents may still commit and do anything on their own); a card reflects the current state when shown and is read from git again before every action.
  - **Trigger:** a session's status goes `run` → `idle` / `done` (a turn ended) and it has changes: uncommitted changes in its working tree, or commits on its branch not merged into its base (a folder session: commits since it started that are on no remote). **Once per change set** (a fingerprint over each repo's HEAD, uncommitted diff and untracked files): the same change set never raises a second card, an open card is refreshed, and a new change after a resolved / dismissed card raises a new one. Setting **Settings → Sessions → Raise review cards when a session with changes goes idle** (`sessions.reviewCards`, default on).
  - **Base (how it is determined, recorded):** the worktree's recorded `base_ref` (what it was created from): `origin/<b>` → the local branch `<b>` (at `origin/<b>` while it has none; Merge creates it); a local branch name → itself; none → the repo's default branch (`origin/HEAD`), else the branch the main checkout is on; a commit (detached cut) → no base (Merge / Open PR / Discard refused). "Commits not merged" = `<base>..HEAD`.
  - **The card:** in the Inbox (new item kind `review`) and as a badge on the session's header: session, folder / repo, branch → base, diff stats, changed files (open the Diff tab), unmerged commits, the agent's last message as its summary, the tests line (best effort: the transcript's last test-like Bash command and its exit code, else "not reported").
  - **Actions, worktree / own-branch sessions:** **Merge** = local only, into the base (where the base is checked out with `git merge`, hooks run; else by moving the ref: fast-forward or a merge commit from `git merge-tree`); refused with the conflicting files, while the worktree has uncommitted changes, or when the base's checkout is dirty; never pushed, never forced. **Open PR** (separate click: pushes the branch to `origin`, `gh pr create`). **Send back** (the comment goes to the session as a message; the card is `sent-back`). **Discard** (confirmed; resets the worktree to its base; the branch then points at the base). **Clean up** after Merge / Discard (offered, not automatic: removes the worktree and the local branch, never forced). **Dismiss**.
  - **Actions, sessions in a folder:** **Commit** (message drafted from the agent's summary, editable; all current changes of the repo; hooks run; never pushes), **Discard** (confirmed; reverts the card's uncommitted files), Send back, Dismiss. Everything already committed → the card shows its commits and offers Send back / Dismiss.
  - **Shared contract with lane A:** the bus event **`reviewResolved`** `{ sessionId, outcome: 'merged' | 'committed' | 'discarded' | 'sent-back' | 'dismissed' }` (`ReviewResolvedEvent`, `src/core/reviews.ts`), exactly this shape, once per resolution; not forwarded between peers.
  - **API:** `GET /api/reviews` (open, then recent), `POST /api/reviews/{id}/merge|open-pr|commit|send-back|discard|cleanup|dismiss`; hub `reviewResolved`, `reviewsChanged`. **Peers:** a paired machine's cards are listed with remote ids and their actions run on that machine. **Devices (ruling):** allowed: list, Merge, Open PR, Commit, Send back, Dismiss; **Discard and Clean up stay desktop-only**. **Push (D73):** a new per-device toggle **Ready for review** (default on).
  - **Ruling (2026-10-08, D79-gone-outcome):** a pending card whose changes disappear without a click (the agent pushed, reverted or merged itself) **counts as done**: closed in a distinct state shown as **Handled by the agent** (`Review.handledByAgent`, not *Dismissed*), and `reviewResolved` is emitted with outcome `dismissed` (contract unchanged; lane A maps it to todo done).
  - **Storage:** migration **0033** (`session_reviews`: id, session_id, fingerprint, state, outcome, created / updated / resolved, data).
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-13 (developer bug report: change tracking missed agent-made worktrees and repos outside the workspace):** most workspace sessions run at a workspace root that is not a git repo, and their solutions are worktrees the agents made themselves (`microfrontends/acme-front-wt-proj-123`: `.git` is a file, no `worktrees` row). The solution resolution accepted main checkouts only, so those sessions had no repositories at all: no cards, no checkpoints, an empty Diff. A session whose edits went to a repo outside the workspace was never watched there either. Approved fix: (1) **one shared answer** to "the repositories a session works in" (`WorktreeManager.repos`, `src/server/worktrees/session-repos.ts`) that review cards, D80 checkpoints and the D90 Diff all read: its worktrees; in place, deduplicated by real path, its solutions resolved **for watching** (a main checkout or a **linked worktree**), the git working trees of the files it **edited** (its Write / Edit / MultiEdit / NotebookEdit calls, D90's source; outside the workspace too; never `deprecated/` or `infrastructure/`), else its cwd's repo. Creating worktrees keeps the strict resolution (main checkouts only). A linked worktree is reviewed in folder mode (uncommitted changes, commits since the session started on no remote, the branch's upstream shown): Commit, Send back, Discard (uncommitted files only), Dismiss; no new merge action. Cached per session (edits read incrementally, solutions resolved again when they, the folder or the edits change). (2) A pending card whose changes are gone now closes as **Handled by the agent** without being opened: at a turn end, when the session goes idle / done or is closed, and once at startup. (3) The Inbox title names the card's repositories (`1 file changed (+2 −0) · acme-front`) and every repo gets a chip. Not a feature: no What's-new entry. `docs/reviews.md` → *Which repositories*; `.loop/questions.md` → *D79/D80/D90 follow-up · repositories a session works in*.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D79 · Review queue*. Details: `docs/reviews.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Review queue (D79)*, `docs/database.md` (0033), `docs/settings.md` (`sessions.reviewCards`), `docs/devices.md`.

## Undo a turn (added 2026-10-08)
- **D80 Checkpoint before each turn; revert to before any turn; Redo.** Developer rulings, 2026-10-08 (lane C).
  - **Checkpoint:** when a supervised session's user message goes to its process, every git working tree the session uses (its cwd repo, its worktrees, its solutions in place) is saved to a hidden ref `refs/switchboard/checkpoints/<session>/<turn>`: tracked + untracked files, never ignored ones, built with a temporary index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`, D65's technique: the developer's index copied, `add -A`, `write-tree`, `commit-tree` with HEAD as parent), never touching the developer's index, HEAD, branch or files; HEAD and the branch are recorded. Unchanged trees reuse the previous commit. Non-git folders get none (the action says why). About 0.15 s per turn on a 5,000-file repo.
  - **Revert to before this turn** (on each user turn in the chat) and **Undo last turn** (the sidebar row's ⋯ menu once the session has a turn, disabled with the reason when the newest turn has no checkpoint; also the compact header's ⋯ menu below 1024 px, the same way; never in the header row: ruling 2026-10-08, int-header-overflow; ruling D80-q1): files back to the checkpoint (tracked + untracked as captured; files made later and not ignored removed; ignored files untouched); the branch moved back over the agent's commits with `update-ref` only when it is the same branch and none of them is pushed, otherwise refused with the reason and **Revert files only** offered; any earlier turn; a safety checkpoint first, so **Redo** undoes it; refused while a turn runs; a confirm dialog lists the files that change.
  - **Conversation stays:** the chat gets the divider "Reverted to before turn N"; the agent gets, with its next message, "Switchboard reverted the files to before turn N (<first line>); changes made in turns N..M are gone. Don't rely on them."
  - **Retention:** 7 days or the last 100 turns per session, whichever keeps fewer; hourly; a closed session keeps them under the same rule (Reopen + revert works, ruling D80-q2), a deleted session's go; refs deleted, `git gc` left to the repo.
  - **Peers / devices:** through the proxy (it runs on the session's machine); allowed from phones. **Hooked terminal sessions: not supported** — their `UserPromptSubmit` hook is asynchronous, so a snapshot from it races the turn (recorded, the action says why).
  - **Storage:** migration **0034** (`turn_checkpoints`); setting `sessions.checkpoints` (Settings → Sessions, default on).
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-13 (change tracking missed agent-made worktrees and repos outside the workspace; see D79's follow-up):** the working trees checkpointed are now the shared set (`WorktreeManager.repos`): its worktrees, its solutions in place (linked worktrees too), the repos it edited (outside the workspace too), else its cwd's repo. A workspace session's cwd (the workspace root) is no longer checkpointed unless it edited files there or nothing else applies. A repo first edited during a turn gets its first checkpoint at the next turn (checkpoints are taken before a turn). `docs/undo.md` → *Which working trees*.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D80 · Undo a turn*. Details: `docs/undo.md`; contract → *Undo a turn (D80)*; `docs/database.md` (0034); `docs/security.md` → *Checkpoints (D80)*.

## Quick capture (added 2026-10-08)
- **D81 Quick capture of todos; the agent fills them in when idle.** Developer rulings, 2026-10-08. On top of D68 / D69 / D70 / D75, D73 (devices, PWA) and D74 (responsive).
  - **Ways to capture** (a title, optionally a note): (1) **⌘K palette**: typing `todo <text>` (or picking **Add todo…**) adds a todo to the current session, or, outside a session, to a session picked in the palette, recent first; (2) **select text in the chat → Add to todo**: a small floating action on a selection (desktop, and a touch long-press selection) adds a todo with the selection as its description (quoted) and a generated short title (the first line, clipped), both editable in a small popover before saving; (3) **the phone's share sheet**: D73's app is a **Web Share Target** (`share_target` in the manifest, POST with title / text / url) on the device origin only (paired devices, the device auth applies): it opens a small **Add to which session?** picker, then saves.
  - **The agent fills it in when idle:** a captured item is saved with the title only (plan `No plan`, Medium, no estimate) and marked **needs-enrichment**. When that session's agent is next idle (not running, not waiting on the developer), Switchboard sends ONE short message: "The developer added todo [<id>] '<title>' (<note if any>). Fill in its description, handover plan, priority and estimate with todo_update — don't start it."; several pending captures → one message listing them. Not for hooked sessions without a waiter (it waits), not for closed sessions. The card shows **✎ waiting for the agent to fill in** until enriched (the agent's `todo_update` on it clears the mark). Setting **Settings → Sessions → Let the agent fill in captured todos** (`sessions.todoEnrich`), default on; off = items stay bare.
  - **Storage:** migration **0035**, additive (`ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`: `needs_enrichment`, `captured_from`, `enrich_asked_at`), so it composes with an earlier rebuild of `session_todos`.
  - **Peers:** capture into a paired machine's session goes through the peer proxy (the route is on `PEER_API_ALLOW`); that machine asks its agent.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D81 · Quick capture*.
  - Details: `docs/todos.md` → *Quick capture (D81)*; `docs/devices.md` → *Share to Switchboard (D81)*; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Quick capture (D81)*; `docs/database.md` (0035); `docs/settings.md` (`sessions.todoEnrich`).

## Model by task and fresh sessions (added 2026-10-08)
- **D82 Model by task: rules pick the CLI, model, effort and account of a todo by its priority and estimate.** Developer ruling, 2026-10-08. Builds on D31 / D42 (model and effort), D62 (CLIs), D63 (accounts) and D70 (todo priority and estimate).
  - **Rules** in **Settings → Sessions → Model by task**, **off until the developer adds a rule**: an ordered list; each rule matches on priority (any / urgent / high / medium / low) and estimate (≤ / > N minutes, or unknown) and sets any of CLI, model, effort, account profile. **First match wins; no match = the normal choice.** Validation: the model must exist for that CLI (its reported model list, else the New-session form's fallback), the profile must be enabled for that CLI; a model, effort or account needs the rule's CLI.
  - **`applyModelRouting(defaults, { priority, estimateMinutes }, rules) → { settings, rule }`** (`src/core/model-routing.ts`, pure, generic over the launch settings `{ provider, model, effort, profileId }`) for lane A's `todoRunOptions`; **`routingExplanation(rule)`** makes the line the UI shows ("Routed by rule: low ≤30 min → Sonnet"). Another CLI starts from its own defaults; a new model without an effort drops the effort.
  - **Storage:** the editable setting `sessions.modelRules` (`GET` / `PUT /api/settings`, default `[]`), no migration.
  - **ASSUMED:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D82 · Model by task*.
  - Details: `docs/model-routing.md`; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Model by task (D82)*; `docs/settings.md` (`sessions.modelRules`).
- **D83 Fresh session when the context fills.** Developer ruling, 2026-10-08. Builds on D49 (context meter), D62 / D63 (the handover mechanism), D33 (close), D54 / D71 (sidebar places), D68 (todos), D48 (peers).
  - **The offer:** when a supervised session's context reaches the threshold (**Settings → Sessions**, default **80 %**, configurable **50–95**, **on by default**), a **bar above the composer** (no Inbox item, no push kind) says **"Context 82% — Continue in a fresh session"** with **Continue** / **Not now** (snoozed until **+10 points**). Refused while a turn runs (offered after it ends); not for hooked terminal sessions (the reason is said). Also a manual action in the session's ⋯ menu: **Continue in a fresh session**.
  - **Continue:** the agent writes a handover in one turn (goal, state, decisions, files touched, open questions, next steps; the D62 / D63 handover request mechanism); a **new session in the same folder / worktree / branch**, same CLI / model / effort / account, starts with the handover as its first message; it takes the old one's **sidebar place, todo list (moved, ids and states kept) and pin**; the **old session is closed** with "Continued in <new session>", the new chat starts with "Continued from <old session>" (links both ways, History links). Works for a paired machine's session via the proxy.
  - **Storage:** migration **0036** (`sessions.continued_to` / `continued_from`); settings `sessions.freshOffer` / `sessions.freshOfferPct`; route `POST /api/sessions/{id}/fresh` (peer and device allow-lists).
  - **ASSUMED:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D83 · Fresh session*.
  - Details: `docs/fresh-session.md`; `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Continue in a fresh session (D83)*; `docs/database.md` (0036); `docs/settings.md`.

## Integration 1.13 (added 2026-10-08)
- **Lanes A–E merged; the cross-feature seams (choices recorded, orchestrator brief):**
  - **One `reviewResolved`:** `ReviewResolvedEvent` `{ sessionId, outcome: ReviewOutcome }` is defined once, in lane B's `src/core/reviews.ts`; one `HubEvents` / `HUB_EVENT_NAMES` entry; lane A's `TodoReviewLink` listens to it.
  - **D82 → D76:** `todoRunOptions(source, todo, rules)` applies the *Model by task* rules (`modelRulesOf(store.settings)`); `TodoLaunchSettings` = `LaunchSettings`; the run's answer carries `routing` and the Run toast shows `Routed by rule: …` (`docs/model-routing.md`).
  - **D83 × D76 / D78 (fix):** `moveAll` moved the rows but not the links: a continued source's run sessions lost their item (the agent token checks `todoLink.sourceSessionId`), and a continued run session's review never resolved the item (matched by `run_session_id`). Now the links follow (`TodoRepository.followContinuation`), the fresh run session takes the old one's `todoLink`, and the completed items' `todo_actuals` follow the list.
  - **D80 × D52 (fix):** the checkpoint row of a turn is stored in the background after the message is written (the git work had delayed every send's answer: a scheduled Run now answered `ok` instead of `running`); the checkpoint list, preview and revert first wait for the session's capture / record in flight.

## Clean-up (added 2026-10-08)
- **D84 Clean-up page: Switchboard's own leftovers, listed with sizes and ages, removed only when ticked and confirmed.** Developer rulings 2026-10-08. Groups: worktrees (merged, closed/deleted session + no change for 14 days, folder missing; uncommitted changes need an extra confirmation listing the files), local branches (`session/*`, `todo/*`, D40/D47 branches marked Switchboard-created; merged or worktree / session gone; unmerged ones need an extra confirmation), remote branches (take-over leftovers, branches Switchboard made and pushed; never ticked by default, each ticked by hand, separate confirmation), closed sessions older than 30 days (configurable) with their events and attachments, attachments / data files past retention. Dry run first; **Clean up selected** shows progress and a result; failures are listed and nothing else is affected. Never `git gc`.
  - **Where (choice recorded):** Settings → **Clean-up**, the last section (`/settings/cleanup`); it is occasional, so no sidebar entry.
  - **Switchboard-created (choice recorded):** worktrees = live `worktrees` rows (adopted D38 ones included: made by a Switchboard session's agent); branches = named in a `worktrees` row with `session/` / `todo/` naming, or in the new **created-branches record** (`cleanup.createdBranches`, a settings value like the take-over leftovers, **no migration**) that the worktree manager now writes whenever it runs `git worktree add -b` itself (`new`; `tracking` when the new local branch tracks an existing `origin/<task>`, whose remote copy is then not Switchboard's). A reused or picked branch is never recorded. **Older task branches (ruling 2026-10-08, answering D84-old-task-branches: "list them if merged"):** a ticket-named branch with neither naming nor a record is listed only when a `worktrees` row (live or removed) names it **and** it is merged into its base; unticked by default; its remote copy is not listed.
  - **Dry run equals the run:** each item carries a fingerprint; a run re-scans and refuses (that item only) anything that changed since the preview. Branch deletes are pinned to the previewed sha (`git update-ref -d <ref> <sha>`, `git push --force-with-lease=<ref>:<sha> … :<ref>`).
  - **Peers (choice recorded): this machine only.** Proxying would add destructive routes to the peer API for a rare task; a paired machine is cleaned on its own Switchboard.
  - **Devices:** refused (`DEVICE_REFUSED`). **No scheduled clean-up** in this version.
  - **Open-session worktrees** are listed only once their PR is merged (a fresh worktree is trivially "merged" into its base); a running session's worktrees and sessions are never listed.
  - **ASSUMED / OPEN:** `.loop/questions.md` → *D84 · Clean-up*. Details: `docs/cleanup.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Clean-up (D84)*.

## Tutorial (added 2026-10-08)
- **D85 Interactive tutorial: an explain-only spotlight tour shown once per machine, What's-new mini-tours after updates.** Developer request and rulings 2026-10-08 ("An interactive tutorial (with a flag shown/not-shown in the db; we show it once, or per rerunning it in Settings) highlighting the items and then describing what it is and what steps the developer should take").
  - **Steps:** the page dimmed except the element (rounded cutout that moves smoothly between steps; no motion with `prefers-reduced-motion`), a card next to it with the title, what it is, **what to do**, "3 of 15", **Back · Next · Skip tour**, → ← Esc; an ARIA modal dialog with a live region, focus kept in it. Clicks on the page do nothing (explain-only). A step whose element is missing shows a centred card with its own text, or (optional What's-new steps) is skipped; never stuck. Phones and tablets (D74): sidebar steps open the ☰ drawer, others close it; on phones the card is a bottom sheet.
  - **Main tour (15 steps):** sessions; + New session; the chat; Inbox; the todo list; Run in new session; Todos page + board; review cards; Undo a turn (the per-message ↶ revert, not a header button); usage + accounts; ⌘K + quick capture; schedules & loops; MCP + tools; machines & devices; Settings + where to replay.
  - **Once per machine, in the database (migration 0037, `tutorial_tours`):** one flag per machine, shared by its browsers and paired devices (a phone sees it in its own layout). Replay: **Settings → Tutorial** (a new section after Updates; there is no "General" section) and **⌘K → Tutorial**; a replay records nothing.
  - **New vs existing install (choice recorded):** decided by migration 0037 from the data present when it ran (a session, a schedule, a saved folder or a finished setup = existing; it writes `tutorial.lastVersion = 1.12.0`). Existing installs get the What's-new mini-tours of what came after their last version (D76–D84 now), **not** the main tour; new installs get the main tour and no What's-new. `tutorial.lastVersion` moves to each build's version, so later updates queue only their new features.
  - **Registry:** `WHATS_NEW` (`src/core/tutorial.ts`): feature id → version → 1–3 steps over `data-tour` anchors; seeded for D76–D84 (Run in new session, Todos board, Estimates against actuals, Review cards, Undo a turn, Quick capture, Model by task, Fresh session, Clean-up), all `1.13.0`. Every new user-facing feature adds an entry (AGENTS.md).
  - **Tests and the demo:** tours open by themselves only when `autoOpen` (not demo mode, not `SWITCHBOARD_TUTORIAL=off`); every test server is started with it off, so the visual oracle and the E2E specs are unaffected. `data-tour` attributes change no layout.
  - **Devices:** `GET /api/tutorial` and `PUT /api/tutorial/tours/{id}` are normal use (`DEVICE_ALLOWED`). Not on the peer API.
  - Details: `docs/tutorial.md`, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Tutorial (D85)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D85 · Tutorial*.
- **D86 No quick replies; the 📎 moves next to Send.** Developer request, 2026-10-08: "You can get rid of the quick replies, I am barely using them". Overrides the prototype's composer row (SPEC → Session → Chat, prototype `quickDef` / `quick`).
  - **Removed:** the `QUICK REPLIES` label, its four pills and their data (`QUICK_REPLIES`, `QUICK_REPLIES_LABEL` in `chat.ts`), their CSS (desktop, the phones' sideways scrolling, the 44 px touch target, D74's short-window rule that hid the label). There was no server or core part.
  - **📎:** in the field's row, between the field and Send (36 px wide, as tall as the one-line field, at the bottom like Send while the field grows; 44 px on touch screens). Same button and behavior: a real button (Tab: field → 📎 → Send; Enter / Space open the picker), paste and drop unchanged, disabled while the machine is offline.
  - **Unchanged places:** the context bar (D49) stays the composer's first row, now directly above the field's row; the todo strip (D68+) and "+ Todo" keep theirs. The composer is 34 px shorter (the row + its 8 px gap at 1440×900), and the chat gets that height.
  - **Visual oracle (deliberate deviation):** `session-chat.spec.ts` no longer compares the row, its label and pills (nor their SPEC tokens); it expects the composer 34 px shorter at the same bottom, the chat area 34 px taller, the field 44 px narrower, and checks the 📎 on its own; `context-bar.spec.ts` places the bar against the field's row; `full-pass.spec.ts` lists "Quick replies" as removed (present in the prototype, absent in the app) instead of as a landmark (`docs/visual/README.md` → *Deliberate deviations from the prototype*).
  - **Tutorial:** the chat step no longer mentions quick replies (it names the paperclip next to Send). **No What's-new entry** (choice recorded): it is a removal, there is nothing new to point at, and a mini-tour about a missing row would only cost a click.

## No notifications while Switchboard is open (added 2026-10-08)
- **D87 No system notifications on a device while Switchboard is in front there; a toast instead.** Developer report 2026-10-08 ("On mobile, even if I am in the browser and in the conversations, I'm getting notifications in the Android system — it shouldn't happen when I'm in the app; only the notifications that show as a toast should appear"). Until now every push-worthy happening went to every subscribed device (D73), whatever the device was showing.
  - **Presence:** each page (tab or installed app, device origins and this machine's UI) reports `visible` / `focused` (`PUT /api/device/presence`) on visibility and focus changes, on `pagehide`, when its hub stream (re)opens and every 30 s while visible; its `/hub?client=<id>` stream ties it to the page. Recorded per paired device, in memory (no migration): a page is in front while its last report said visible, at most 75 s ago, and its stream is open. Devices only (the desktop gets no push).
  - **Server suppression:** a device with ≥ 1 page in front gets no push (this machine's and the paired machines' happenings alike). Happenings that occur while it is in front are **not queued** and sent later when it goes to the background (they were seen in the app). The test notification is always sent.
  - **Toasts:** every push-worthy happening is published as the `/hub` `notice` event (the push payload plus an id); a paired device's page shows it as a toast (permission requests, finished turns, errors, review cards, other Inbox items; question batches keep the M3.4 question toast), once per id, per the device's toggles. This machine's own UI is unchanged.
  - **Service-worker guard:** a push that arrives while a window of the origin is visible is posted to that window (toast, deduped by id) instead of shown, except the test notification and on WebKit (Safari / iOS, where a silent push can cost the subscription). Relies on Chromium's userVisibleOnly rule: no notification is required while a tab of the origin is visible.
  - Settings → Devices (the device's own page) says *No notifications while Switchboard is open on this device*.
  - Details: `docs/devices.md` → *Notifications*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Devices (D73)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D87 · Quiet pushes*.

## Drafts follow you (added 2026-10-09)
- **D88 What is typed and not sent stays with the session, kept on the server.** Developer request 2026-10-09 ("The text fields for inputting my feedback — when I switch from session to session, it shouldn't clear"). Ruling: drafts are kept **on the server**, so they follow the developer between this machine and its paired devices (D73) and paired machines (D48).
  - **Kept per session:** the composer's text and its chips already uploaded (restored only while the upload still exists in the session; else the text only), a question batch's picks and **Other…** own answers (D39; the chat's and the Inbox's card share them), a review card's **Send back** comment (D79) and (ruling below) edited **Commit message**, the todo strip's **+ Add** form and an open **Edit** form (D69–D70: title, description, plan, priority, estimate). Restored when returning to the session, after a reload and on another device.
  - **Save / clear:** debounced ~400 ms, and at once on blur, `visibilitychange` (hidden) and `pagehide` (`keepalive`) and when the field goes away; cleared when sent, saved or cancelled, or when the field is emptied.
  - **Conflict rule (recorded):** last write wins on the server, but a field that has focus is never overwritten by another device's change: the change waits, and on blur the field saves its own text if it changed since (so it wins), else shows what the server holds then. A field without focus follows at once.
  - **Live:** `/hub` `draftChanged { sessionId, field, client }` (forwarded between peers); the writing page skips its own.
  - **Storage:** migration 0038 `session_drafts` (session, field key, value JSON, `updated_at`, `updated_by` device / page), removed with the session; at most 64 KB per value, 200 drafts per session. Routes `GET /api/sessions/{id}/drafts`, `PUT` / `DELETE …/drafts/{field}`; a paired machine's session's drafts live on that machine (proxied); allowed to paired devices.
  - **Performance:** the text stays the field's own state; saving never re-renders the message list (the D86 typing guard stays green).
  - **What's new (1.14.0):** "Your drafts follow you", one step on the composer.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-09 (follow-ups):** (1) the **New session** dialog (Simple and Full) and the review card's **Commit message** are drafts too. The New-session form is no session's: one draft **per machine** (`new-session`, migration 0040 `machine_drafts`, `GET /api/drafts`, `PUT` / `DELETE /api/drafts/{field}`; same limits, devices sync, focus rule; not on the peer API), restored when the dialog opens again; **Cancel keeps it** (like a mail draft), Start clears it, and an explicit **Clear** in the dialog clears it. The Commit message is `commit:<reviewId>`. (2) **Orphaned drafts are cleaned up at once** on the server, with `draftChanged`: a question batch answered or closed (anywhere, incl. claude.ai / the terminal), a review resolved (any device), a todo deleted or done; **closed sessions keep their drafts** until deleted. (3) No change: + Add on another device stays "open on (re)open"; a refused question answer's draft is cleared at Send (accepted as is).
  - Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Drafts*, contract → *Drafts (D88)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D88 · Drafts*.

## Artifacts saved on purpose (added 2026-10-09)
- **D89 Artifacts are saved on purpose, like Claude's artifacts on claude.ai; the automatic rows are gone.** Developer request 2026-10-09 ("The artifacts are showing a crazy amount of stuff. It should work more like Claude artifacts within claude.ai — when the agent saves the artifact deliberately."). Rulings: remove the automatic rows; artifacts are only what is saved deliberately, by the agent (a new MCP tool) or by the developer from a chat message. Replaces gap #9 (`docs/derivations.md` → *Artifacts (gap #9): replaced by D89*). Details: `docs/artifacts.md`.
  - **Agent tools** in the `switchboard` MCP server (same helper, token and injection as D68; Codex / OpenCode still UNVERIFIED on the real CLIs): `artifact_save` {title, kind, content? / path?, language?, id?} (not read-only, not destructive, not idempotent, not open-world; `id` adds a version), `artifact_list`, `artifact_get` {id, version?}. Kinds: markdown, code (+ language), html, mermaid, svg, image (by path: png / jpg / gif / webp), csv. `path` is copied at save time, only from inside the session's folders (symbolic links resolved), 2 MB text / 10 MB image.
  - **Developer save:** ⋯ on an agent's message → Save as artifact (title from the first heading or line, kind markdown, editable), and Save as artifact on each code block (kind code with its language).
  - **Standing instruction:** one sentence added ("Save deliverables the developer should see … update the same artifact instead of creating new ones."), D68's earlier-default convention (1.13.0's default reads as the new one); the "short" bound went from 560 to 740 characters.
  - **Storage:** migration 0039 (`artifacts_saved` + `artifact_versions`; images in `<dataDir>/artifacts/<id>/<n>.<ext>`). The old `artifacts` table is no longer written or read and stays as it is (conservative; no drop).
  - **UI:** the session's Artifacts tab is a list + viewer (rendered / source / compare between versions; Copy, Download, Full screen, Delete with a confirmation; live on the new `/hub` event `artifactsChanged`). HTML runs in `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` (never `allow-same-origin`) of a raw route served under a sandboxing CSP: the app's cookie, storage and API are out of reach (`docs/security.md` → *Artifacts*). SVG and images as `<img>` of the served file, never inline. CSV as a table (first 500 rows). The Artifacts page lists every session's (and the paired machines') saved artifacts with kind and session filters and search; a row opens the session's tab on it; the sidebar counts them.
  - **Mermaid:** first shown as source (no dependency); then the developer ruled (2026-10-09) **draw it**: `mermaid@12.1.0` pinned exactly as a devDependency, copied by the build into `dist/web/vendor/mermaid.min.js`, inlined into a page the raw route serves with `?render` under the HTML sandbox CSP and shown in `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` (opaque origin, never the app's), `securityLevel: 'strict'`, source on a parse error; Download stays the `.mmd`. Release: `dist/web` +5.49 MB, the `.tar.gz` about +1.57 MB, installed `node_modules` unchanged (`npm ci --omit=dev`).
  - **Rulings 2026-10-09 (on the D89 questions):** the Solutions detail lists the saved artifacts of the sessions whose solutions include the solution (a row opens the session's Artifacts tab on it), with the follow-up files; Clean-up (D84) offers artifacts whose session was deleted, with their image files and sizes, never ticked for you, desktop only.
  - **Peers / devices:** a paired machine's session's artifacts through the proxy (read, a version's bytes with their headers, save from a message, delete); a paired device may read, save from a message and delete (normal use). A machine before D89 sends derived rows: they are left out.
  - **Removed:** the recorder's derivation (file / DIFF / PR / BRANCH rows) and its helpers, the worktree manager's PR-state update of PR rows, `src/core/artifacts-view.ts`, the old tab model; the Solutions detail's *Artifacts & follow-ups* shows the sessions' saved artifacts and the `mobile-followups/*.md` files. The Review cards read the session diff, not artifacts (unchanged). The visual oracle of the tab is retired and the page's compares the chrome D89 keeps (`docs/visual/README.md` → *Deliberate deviations*).
  - **What's new (1.14.0):** "Artifacts are saved on purpose" (two steps: ⋯ → Save as artifact in a session; the Artifacts page). ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D89 · Saved artifacts*.

## Diff since the last commit (added 2026-10-09)
- **D90 The Diff tab shows the work since the last commit.** Developer request, 2026-10-09: "The diff is going all over the place instead of showing the current diff for the code that the agent is currently working on, from the last commit." Until now a worktree was diffed against the merge-base with its base branch (every commit of the branch) and an in-place solution against HEAD (every uncommitted change in that repo, whoever made it). Rulings:
  - **Default view "Since last commit":** the uncommitted changes against HEAD (staged, unstaged, untracked; never ignored) in the session's own working trees: its worktrees and the solutions it works on in place.
  - **In place, only the files this session touched:** the paths of its Write / Edit / MultiEdit / NotebookEdit calls, plus, where D80 checkpoints exist, the files that changed between a turn's checkpoint and the turn's end (Bash-made files and deletions too); intersected with the uncommitted changes. A toggle **All uncommitted changes in this repo** shows everything, with a note that it includes other people's and sessions' edits.
  - **Whole branch** (worktree sessions): the behavior before D90, against the merge-base with the base branch.
  - The pick is remembered per session in the browser. A header line says what is shown ("Since last commit · 4 files · +120 −8", "Whole branch vs origin/dev · …"); the empty state is "No uncommitted changes since the last commit." with a hint to Whole branch when the branch has commits.
  - Hunks are separated by their `@@ -a,b +c,d @@` header as a subtle row (`FileDiff.lines` now carries it).
  - API: `GET /api/sessions/{id}/diff?scope=head|branch|repo` (default `head`); `branch` keeps the old behavior (`SessionDetail.files`, the Artifacts counts and the D79 / D80 git reads are unchanged). Additive `GET /api/sessions/{id}/diff/targets` tells the tab which views exist (and `…/diff/count` the tab count, see below). Peers pass the query through; devices may read them all.
  - What's new (1.14.0): "Diff shows the work since the last commit", one step on the Diff tab's toggle.
  - **Tab count (ruling 2026-10-09, D90-q1):** the session tab's **Diff · n** follows the view the Diff tab shows: Since last commit by default, Whole branch / All uncommitted changes when picked (the same choice remembered per session in this browser). Read from an additive light `GET /api/sessions/{id}/diff/count?scope=` (file names only, no patches), fetched again on the Diff tab's triggers; a machine without it falls back to `SessionDetail.files`. `SessionDetail.files`, the Artifacts counts and the Review card keep the whole branch.
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-13 (change tracking missed agent-made worktrees and repos outside the workspace; see D79's follow-up):** the Diff's in-place working trees are the shared set (`WorktreeManager.repos`): its solutions (a main checkout or a linked worktree, which used to be skipped), the repos it edited (outside the workspace too; named after their folder), else its cwd's repo; a repo folder session with no declared solution shows its repo. `docs/worktrees.md` → *Diff* → *Which working trees*.
  - Details: `docs/worktrees.md` → *Diff*, `docs/derivations.md` → *Diff tab*, `docs/handoff/contracts/local-api.md` → *Diff views (D90)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D90 · Diff since the last commit*.

## Apply the standing instruction to open sessions (added 2026-10-09)
- **D91 The standing instruction can be applied to the sessions already open.** Developer request 2026-10-09: "A place where I can put a prompt that's embedded at the beginning of the session, or if the session didn't have it, it adds it for existing sessions." The D64 standing instruction reaches a session only when Switchboard (re)starts its CLI process. Ruling: **Apply to open sessions** next to the standing instruction.
  - **Settings → Sessions & worktrees → Standing instruction:** a button **Apply to open sessions** with a count, "3 open sessions use an older instruction" (enabled when there is at least one). While it runs: "Applying…"; then a result line ("Applied to 3 sessions (2 restarted, 1 not running: on their next start) · 1 after its turn · 1 failed") and each failure's session and reason.
  - **Which sessions are "older":** each live process keeps the instruction it was started with (the D64 value read at that spawn, `null` = none) **in memory** on the supervisor's live record; a session is older when that differs from what a spawn would get now (text and on/off). No migration: a process never outlives the Switchboard that started it (restart recovery spawns again with the current setting), so the in-memory value is exact; a session without a running process gets the current one at its next start and is never "older". `Session.instructionOutdated` / `instructionPending` (additive) carry it; a change of the setting republishes the live sessions (`sessionUpdated`).
  - **Per session, by state:** **idle** → its CLI is restarted with `--resume` (same conversation id, model / effort, account, cwd and injections; no message), through the D7 stop and the usual spawn (the D63 account-switch shape, without the copy), the chat's divider **Standing instruction updated**, the status it had kept; **busy or waiting on the developer** (a turn, a queued message, an open question or permission) → marked and restarted right after it goes idle, never interrupted (a message sent meanwhile waits for the restart and goes to the new process); **no process** (paused, ended) → nothing to do, counted as applied ("not running"); **hooked** terminal sessions and sessions continued in a terminal → skipped; **closed** → left out. A restart that cannot run (the session's folder is gone, its CLI is unavailable) is refused before anything stops: the session keeps its process as it was; the reason shows in the result and as an error line in its chat.
  - **Per session:** the sidebar row's ⋯ → **Reload instruction**, shown while that session is on an older instruction (disabled with "Applies when the running turn ends" while one waits).
  - **Codex / OpenCode:** the same restart through their bridges (`thread/resume` / the session id), as for D62's switches: unverified on the real CLIs, as before.
  - **API:** `POST /api/settings/standing-instruction/apply` → `InstructionApplyResult`; `POST /api/sessions/{id}/reload-instruction` → `{ outcome, session }`. **Devices (D73):** Apply is a settings action and desktop-only (DEVICE_REFUSED); Reload instruction is a session action like Pause / Resume and allowed. **Peers (D48):** neither is on the peer API: a paired machine's sessions are applied on that machine from its own Settings (the count and the ⋯ item leave them out). An "also on <machine>" through the proxy was not added (recorded).
  - **What's new (1.14.0):** "Apply the standing instruction to open sessions", one step on the button.
  - Details: `docs/settings.md` → *Apply to open sessions*, contract → *Apply the standing instruction (D91)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D91 · Apply the standing instruction*.

## Copy code (added 2026-10-09)

- **D92 Every code block in the chat has a Copy button.** Developer request 2026-10-09: "When the agent returns code snippets, it would be nice if I could copy them with the click of a button." Every fenced code block rendered by `ChatMarkdown` (main chat, subagent chats, todo cards) gets **Copy** at its top right, beside D89's **Save as artifact** where the chat offers it: shown on hover / keyboard focus, always on touch screens. It copies the block's code (the async Clipboard API, else a hidden textarea), then reads "Copied ✓" (or "Copy failed") for 1.5 s, announced through a polite live region. The labels are drawn by CSS, so selecting or copying the message text never includes them. What's-new entry `copy-code` (1.14.0). Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Markdown*.

## Fix: stale loop cards (added 2026-10-09)

- **D93 Schedules & loops lists live loops only.** Developer bug report 2026-10-09: a session that started as a hooked terminal session and continued in Switchboard (D72) showed four cron cards, but only one job existed in its CLI process: one had been cancelled ("Cancelled job <id>"), two were past the 7-day auto-expiry, one had died with the earlier process (CronList showed only the newest one-shot; CronDelete on the others answered "No scheduled job with id …"). Causes: the tracker read the session's events in insert order (imported terminal turns can be stored after later events), a new process starting without a recorded end (D72 `continued`, resume, recovery, take-over, …) did not end the earlier process's schedules, a `CronDelete` naming an unknown id could end the only live cron, a one-shot could swallow an unrelated firing, and rows were never deleted. Fix (`docs/derivations.md` → *Loop cards*): events in time order; a process start (and, in a transcript, an `entrypoint` change) ends the schedules made before it; `CronDelete` matched by id from its input or its result (`Cancelled job <id>`, "No scheduled job with id …" ends it too) to the `CronCreate` result's job id; a one-shot counts a firing only from 2 min before its minute; **ended loops have no card and their rows are deleted** (cancelled, expired, died with an earlier process, fired one-shot / wake-up; Workflow cards stay); an expired row is hidden before the tracker gets to it; the start sweep re-derives every session with a tracker row, so existing installs clean up on the first start after the update. Firings no observed live loop accounts for are not attributed and get no card (the half-hourly prompts of the report come from the CLI process, not from Switchboard: nothing in Switchboard sends a timed message to an existing session, and Switchboard's own messages are stream-json stdin prompts the CLI records as `promptSource: "sdk"`). No migration (0041 not used). `loop_list` for agents not added. Questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D93 · Stale loop cards*. **Ruling on D93-q1 (2026-10-09): yes, show an "unlisted schedule" card**: a series of prompts the CLI starts itself with no visible job (≥ 3 at a roughly regular interval, not explained by a live loop) gets a card "Unlisted schedule in the CLI" with the prompt, the count, the estimated interval, the last time and an estimated next time, no next firing or expiry; it goes when the series stops (> 2× the interval) or the process ends (`docs/derivations.md` → *Loop cards* → *Unlisted schedules*; What's-new `unlisted-schedules`).

## Switchboard-owned loops (added 2026-10-09)

- **D94 Switchboard owns the loops: agents create them through MCP, Switchboard fires them.** Developer decision 2026-10-09: CLI-native session crons (`/loop`, `CronCreate`) are invisible / unreliable to Switchboard (they die with the process, survive invisibly in some resumes, expire after a hidden 7 days, and `CronList` / `CronDelete` do not always agree with what fires). Details: `docs/loops.md`.
  - **Tools** in the `switchboard` MCP server (D68 helper and token): `loop_create` {prompt, one of cron / every_minutes / at, expires_at?, max_runs?, label?}, `loop_list`, `loop_update`, `loop_pause`, `loop_resume`, `loop_cancel`, annotated; descriptions and the server instructions say to prefer them over CronCreate / ScheduleWakeup / `/loop`.
  - **Firing:** the prompt as a message (origin `service`, `UserPayload.loop` → the chat chip "⟳ <label> · run n") through the usual path: idle now, busy queued, paused resumed, hooked through the mailbox; at most one firing waits (the next due time is skipped meanwhile); no catch-up after downtime (missed due times skipped, the next one fires); ends at `expires_at` (default none), after `max_runs`, a one-shot after its firing, or when the session closes. Survives restarts, D72 (same record), D83 (moved to the fresh session) and D65 (re-created on the target, ended on the source).
  - **Storage:** migration 0041 `session_loops`.
  - **UI:** first-class cards on Schedules & loops (Schedule, exact Next, Expires or "no expiry", Runs + skipped, state; Pause / Resume, Run now, Edit, Cancel with a confirmation), a strip in the session above the composer, **+ New loop** and the session's ⋯ → **New loop…**; the CLI's own loop cards are tagged "Managed by the CLI" (the unlisted-schedule card stays).
  - **Standing instruction:** one line added ("For recurring or scheduled work use the switchboard loop_create tool, not CronCreate or /loop."), D68's earlier-default convention (1.14.x's default reads as the new one); the length bound in the tests went from 740 to 830.
  - **Peers / devices:** a paired machine's session's loops through the proxy (they fire there); devices may list, create, edit, pause / resume, run now and cancel.
  - **What's new (1.15.0):** "Loops run by Switchboard" (two steps). ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D94 · Switchboard-owned loops*.
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-13 (developer request):** a firing due while the session waits for the developer (status `need`: question or permission pending) is skipped and counted (`the session is waiting for your answer`); Run now is refused then (409 `waiting`).

## Performance with long sessions (added 2026-10-10)

- **D95 Long sessions stay fast: the chat loads its newest events, derivations read only what changed.** Developer report 2026-10-10: "When using Switchboard longer, it slows down. Investigate and optimize." Measured first with a harness on a synthetic world (`tools/bench/`, a 13k-event / 32 MB session like the developer's biggest); details, numbers and profiles: `docs/performance.md`.
  - **Chat window:** the chat fetches the newest 1,000 events (additive `GET /api/sessions/{id}/events?limit=&before=&agent=`; without them every event, as before) and the page before them when scrolled near its top, or through a row above the messages, **Show earlier messages** (Loading… / Could not load · Retry); the message in view stays put while a page lands. A subagent's chat loads that subagent's events whole. `/hub` events older than the loaded window wait for their page; an answered question batch whose call is not loaded yet is not appended at the end. The per-tab cache keeps two pages a session and 6,000 events in all. Opening the big session: 8.7 s → 1.7 s until quiet, 371k → 44k DOM nodes, 143 → 24 MB heap.
  - **Renders:** `ChatMarkdown` no longer parses every message again on each event (D89's closure defeated its memo); checkpoints are read again per turn instead of per event; the scroll anchor is measured only while scrolled up. A soak of streaming + switching: 92 % → 10 % main-thread busy.
  - **Memory:** a focused composer no longer keeps the left view alive (React kept the field until a focusout it ignored during the commit); back on the Inbox: 367k → 283 renderer nodes, 149 → 26 MB heap.
  - **Server:** the events repository keeps per-session write revisions and a log of the ids written; the loop tracker keeps slim events and reads only the changed rows (12.1 → 6.2 ms CPU per streamed event in a 13k-event session); the reported status table and the turn count are kept until an event that can change them (`EventMemo`); a session's agents come from memory; the main agent from one query. No migration (0042 not used).
  - **Database:** at start a bounded `PRAGMA optimize`; every 10 minutes, once no event was written for a minute, `PRAGMA optimize` and `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`.
  - **Behavior changes:** only the row above the loaded messages, and that the browser's find (⌘F) sees the loaded messages only until earlier ones are loaded. No What's-new entry (not a feature).
  - Not done (questions): the `sessionUpdated` payload's size (every agent of the session on each update) and the Timeline tab (every event, a lane per agent). ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D95 · Long sessions*.
