  - **Follow-up (developer's rulings on D95-q1 / D95-q2, 2026-10-10):** (1) *send only changes*: a stream that asks with `?agents=delta` (`/hub`, `/peer/v1/events`) gets a session's whole agent list once, then only the agents added or changed plus `agentsDelta` (`removed`, `order` when it changed); the page and the peer client expand it back to the whole list before any handler; older tabs and machines get whole lists as before (additive; contract → *Agent deltas over `/hub`*). `/hub` per 100 short turns with ≈430 agents: 27.1 → 4.2 MB. (2) *window the Timeline*: the last 50 turns from the paged events route, lanes only for the main agent, the agents active in them and the ones still working, **Show earlier** adds 50 turns. Big session's Timeline: 1.3–1.8 s → 0.8 s until quiet, 435–507 → 56–63 ms main thread, 428 → 28 lanes, 12,657 → 961 elements. No What's-new entry (as D95's chat window: not a feature). `docs/performance.md` → *Agent deltas*, *Windowed Timeline*; `.loop/questions.md` → *D95 follow-up*.
  - **Follow-up 2 (developer's rulings on D95-q3 and the session list, 2026-10-10):** (1) a delta stream (now `?delta=1`; `?agents=delta` was never released) leaves `loops` and `model` out while unchanged (`Session.unchanged`), and `Session.loops` carries a loop's newest 30 iterations (what a card shows): `/hub` 4.17 → 0.81 MB per 100 turns. (2) The views that list sessions share one in-memory copy per page, patched from `sessionUpdated`, read again only after the hub reopens, a paired machine's state changes or an unknown session appears; `GET /api/sessions` leaves the agents out (`?include=agents`; a paired machine still gets them). While 100 turns stream: 191 list reads / 61.7 MB → none, 925 → 215–321 ms main thread; the list 281 → 10.9 KB. `docs/performance.md` → *Heavy fields only when they change*, *Session list in memory*.

## Progress notes (added 2026-10-13)

- **D96 The agent's text between tool calls (a progress note) shows in the chat.** Developer report 2026-10-13: "I need to deliberately tell the agent to print the message and the question in separate messages, because otherwise I'm losing the message from the agent." Cause: on the newest models (Claude Code CLI 2.1.296 with Opus 5.5; Sonnet 5.5 and Fable 5.1 per the report) the text a model writes between tool calls arrives as a `thinking` block whose `thinking` field holds the text (the progress-update thinking display), one line per block sharing `message.id`: `[thinking ""]`, `[thinking "<the message>"]`, `[tool_use AskUserQuestion]`. Switchboard dropped thinking text (older CLIs sent it empty). Fix:
  - a non-empty thinking block is agent text: an `assistant` event with `note: true` (additive `AssistantPayload.note`), one per message's notes, apart from its text; empty, blank and redacted thinking stay hidden. Supervised sessions, hooked / attached terminal sessions (transcript import), Workflow agents' chats and peers;
  - shown as a normal agent bubble (Markdown, copy, Save as artifact), `data-note="true"`, **not muted** (ASSUMED D96-style: the model meant it for the developer); a note before a question sits directly above the card;
  - the Inbox question item's `detail` is the agent's message right above the AskUserQuestion call (note or text, ≤ 2,000 characters, line breaks kept); before, it was empty. Push text and the question toast unchanged;
  - never twice: repeated note lines (one stream, one sync or a later sync) merge into the one event, identical text skipped;
  - the live activity line reads a note as `Writing…`; History's last text (facts version 4) and terminal loop labels count notes; everything reading `assistant` events (D79 review summary, D21 status table, D62 handover, loop tracker) gets them for free;
  - ASSUMED D96-summarized: Claude Code does not request summarized thinking, so every non-empty thinking text is shown; a summarized-reasoning block could not be told apart (the `note` flag keeps them distinguishable for a later rule);
  - not recovered: events recorded before D96 (the stream is gone; a hooked session's sync point is past them); History summaries are re-read from the transcripts, so they do pick notes up;
  - **Ruling on D96-q1 (2026-10-13): yes.** A permission request's Inbox item carries the agent's message right above its call too: additive `InboxItem.context`, shown above the model's description (D6), the same rule as the question context (`callContext`; ≤ 2,000 characters, line breaks kept, scrolls past 240 px, a rule on its left). Supervised, hooked (the PermissionRequest hook names no `tool_use` id: the newest open call of that tool, same input first; the hook service syncs the transcript before raising the item, which also gives hooked questions their context) and peers (the item passes through). The chat shows no permission card (a step line under the agent's message), so it is unchanged;
  - the standing-instruction default has no workaround line (checked; unchanged). fake-claude `[fake:note "<json>"]`. No migration, no What's-new entry (a fix). Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Progress notes*; questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D96 · Progress notes*.

## Search across everything (added 2026-10-13)

- **D97 One search finds anything said or saved, on every machine, and opens the exact message.** Developer decision 2026-10-13 (v2 plan, What's new 1.16.0). Details: `docs/search.md`; contract → *Search (D97)*.
  - **Index:** migration 0042, one SQLite FTS5 table (contentless with deletes, `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2`) over event text (user messages, agent text and D96 progress notes, subagent prompts, tool calls: name, input and result, each cut at 2,000 characters; binary-looking text left out), session titles, saved artifacts (latest version), todos and History's terminal conversations. Events are indexed when written (append, and an update that changes the payload); the other sources and every delete (event, session, todo, artifact or version, History row) by triggers, in the same transaction, so the index cannot outlive its source.
  - **Backfill:** what existed before 0042 is indexed in the background after startup: timer steps of ≥ 1 batch of 250 rows and ≤ 15 ms, waiting while events are written (at most 1 s), progress stored per step (a restart resumes). Answers say `indexing` (the page: *Indexing older history… n%*).
  - **API:** `GET /api/search?q=&scope=&session=&machine=&limit=&cursor=`: bm25 (titles × 4) with a recency boost, highlighted snippets as segments, scope `all` / `conversations` / `artifacts` / `todos` / `history`, a session filter, cursor paging per scope. Words, `"phrases"` and `prefix*`; FTS syntax is searched as text, never run.
  - **Peers (D48):** the search asks every online paired machine at once (2.5 s each), merges their namespaced results by score, tags them with the machine and lists the machines not searched and why; a peer's request never fans out; History stays on its machine. **Devices (D73):** allowed.
  - **Deep link:** the events API takes `around=<eventId>` (a page centred on it) and `after=<eventId>` (pages after it), both compatible with `before=` paging. `/sessions/{id}/events/{eventId}` opens the chat at that message: loaded around it when the newest page does not hold it (the window then has *Show later messages* / *Jump to latest* under it and does not follow new items until it reaches the end), scrolled to the middle and highlighted for 2.6 s; a subagent's message opens its chat.
  - **UI:** the Search page (`/search`, following the Artifacts page's design: field, scope chips with counts, groups by type and, within a group, by machine; ↑ ↓ Enter; the query in the address), ⌘⇧F / Ctrl+Shift+F, the app bar's search button on tablets and phones, and `?text` in the ⌘K palette (the best results, then *All results*). No sidebar item and no new palette row for a normal query (both are compared with the prototype by the visual oracle).
  - **Performance** (`node tools/bench/search.ts`, D95 bench world: 31,819 events in 5 sessions, seeded before start): search p50 41 ms / p95 55 ms over 270 queries (the worst, a word in ≈27,700 messages: p95 ≈ 55–60 ms; a phrase 16 ms; a rare word 4.6 ms); backfill done 4.0 s after start (1.5 s start delay included) while the usual reads kept answering (`GET /api/sessions` p95 31 ms, the big session's newest page p95 37 ms); index 17.1 MB of a 127 MB database.
  - **What's new (1.16.0):** "Search across everything" (two steps). ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D97 · Search across everything*.

## Accounts shared across paired machines (added 2026-10-13)

- **D98 Accounts are shared and merged across paired machines.** Developer request 2026-10-13: "If we have multiple Switchboards connected between the PCs, the accounts can be shared between them? Also, we should merge the accounts from multiple PCs if the accounts are the same." Approved scope (all three parts), What's new 1.17.0. Details: `docs/accounts.md` → *Shared across paired machines*; contract → *Accounts shared across paired machines (D98)*.
  - **One list:** the footer usage grid and Settings → Accounts show the accounts of every connected paired machine (D48) as well as this one's, each labelled with its machine; a machine that is away drops out, never blocks.
  - **Same account = one row**, by the identity the CLI reports: Claude Code the email (`claude auth status --json`; the plan is only shown); Codex never (`codex login status` names no account: "Logged in using ChatGPT"); OpenCode never (no identity). A merged row ("dev@… · max · on Desk + Studio") uses the **newest reading from any machine**: the limits belong to the account.
  - **Sync design:** peers exchange `POST /api/accounts/shared` (peer allow-list) every 30 s, on connect and 300 ms after a local change, both ways in one call (each side keeps the other's accounts in memory and merges the other's prefs). Names and order are an **identity-keyed map, last writer wins by timestamp** (setting `accounts.shared`: a name per identity, an identity list per CLI; equal times by value); an adopted name / order is applied to the same accounts here without a new timestamp (no echo); an order moves only the accounts both machines have, within their own slots.
  - **Limits across machines:** an account a machine finds spent (a limit error) or reads at its limit is skipped by every machine's automatic switcher (D63) until that reset: the switcher's view of a profile takes the latest spent mark and the newest reading from any machine, new sessions start elsewhere, and the periodic check moves a live session off an account known spent before a turn fails on it.
  - **Out of scope, never done:** copying credentials or logins. No `.credentials.json`, token, `auth.json` or config folder travels; each machine signs in on its own.
  - **Compatibility:** a Switchboard before D98 refuses the exchange (403): it is read with `GET /api/accounts` and shown unmerged. Without paired machines nothing changes (the desktop visual oracle is pixel-identical). Devices (D73): the exchange is refused like every `/api/accounts/…` route. No migration (two `settings` rows). ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D98 · Accounts shared across paired machines*.

## Plans (added 2026-10-13)

- **D99 Plans: todo items in a dependency graph that a scheduler runs as todo runs, each merged into the epic's target branch; experimental, switchable and revertible.** Developer decision 2026-10-13 (core lane; the Plans page and the Jira import are their own lanes). Details: `docs/plans.md`; contract → *Plans (D99)*; removal: `docs/plans-removal.md`.
  - **What it does:** a plan groups a session's todos with "B after A" edges; the scheduler starts ready items as D76 todo runs (own session and worktree, branch `<KEY>-<slug>` or `todo/<slug>`, cut from the plan's target branch, the plan's start answers), at most `maxConcurrency` (default 3) at once and only while the account is below `usageThreshold` (default 85 %); an item is done when its work is merged into the target under the merge mode; the last one raises "Plan ready: <target> → <base>" in the Inbox. REST under `/api/plans`, `planChanged` on `/hub`, the MCP tools `plan_create`, `plan_add_item`, `plan_add_dependency`, `plan_status`, `plan_start`, an `external { system: 'jira', key }` field and `PlanService.createFromExternal` / `findByExternal` for the Jira lane.
  - **Merge modes, required at start with no default:** `agents`, `agents-with-holds` (the UI's recommendation), `developer`. `plan_start` tells the agent to ASK the developer.
  - **Safeguards:** agents merge only into the plan's target (never `dev` / `develop` / `master` / `main`, the default or a protected branch: 422 `target-forbidden`; since the 2026-10-15 follow-up below, allowed with the developer's acknowledgement); green checks first (gh); red checks go back to the session; a second conflict goes to the Inbox; failed / discarded / blocked items block their dependents with a reason; a cycle is refused (409 `cycle`).
  - **Experimental (developer decision 2026-10-13, "exactly like Team"):** Settings → Experimental → **Plans** (`experimental.plans`), off by default. Off: plan routes 404 `plans-off` except `GET /api/plans/state`, no scheduler listener, no PR-check poll, no plan tools, nothing in the background. All Plans data is in a separate `<dataDir>/plans.db` with its own migrations (Team's runner), created only when switched on; nothing in `switchboard.db` (the unreleased migration 0043 was dropped). Switching off pauses running plans ("Plans was switched off"; running items finish their turn, nothing new starts); switched on again they show paused until resumed. A broken `plans.db` answers 503 `plans-unavailable` and nothing else is affected. Code in `src/server/plans/`, `src/core/plans.ts`, `src/hook/sb-plan-tools.ts`, `src/web/plans/`, wired through hooks marked `Plans (D99) hook` and listed in `docs/plans-removal.md` (verified by removing Plans from a scratch copy and type-checking it; a test keeps the list complete). Settings → Experimental became a shared section rendering Team's and Plans' rows.
  - **Item history (Plans page lane, D99-UI-history):** each item's spans (`running`, `waiting-you`, `waiting-dependency`, `paused-usage`, `paused-plan`) are kept in `plans.db` (`plan_item_spans`, its migration 2), opened and closed with every state change and closed when the plan ends, and answered additively as `PlanItem.spans` (the newest 200). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Item history*.
  - **Continue in a fresh session (D83), developer answer 2026-10-13 "Yes, follow it":** a plan follows its source session to the fresh one (todos keep their ids, so items keep their todos and pending items still start); items run by a continued run session follow it too. A hook in the fresh-session flow does it while Plans is on; while off, the move is made from the sessions' `continuedTo` / `continuedFrom` links when Plans is switched on (nothing extra is recorded). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Continue in a fresh session*.
  - **Team (D100):** hand-offs are todos and work without Plans; Team never calls Plans (`TeamHandoff.planRef` is stored text). Any Team → Plans use must check both switches.
  - ASSUMED items and open questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D99 · Plans*.
- **D99 follow-up · Plans for anything (2026-10-15).** An agent reported: "I skipped plan_create because its target has to be a feature branch: it refuses dev, master and main." Developer: "can we allow to make plans for anything?" Decision (supersedes D99's hard `target-forbidden` refusal):
  - **Any target branch**, including `dev`, `main`, `master` or the default branch (also equal to the base). `developer` mode: no restriction. `agents` / `agents-with-holds` on such a target need the developer's **acknowledgement**, stored on the plan (`allowProtectedTarget`; plans.db migration 3): the start / mode dialog's warning checkbox "Agents will merge straight into `dev`", or `allowProtectedTarget: true` on `start` / `mode` (the `plan_start` tool's `allow_protected_target`, whose description tells the agent to ASK the developer first, like the merge mode). Without it: 422 `target-forbidden`. The other safeguards stay (green checks first, the conflict handling, holds).
  - **No target branch** (missing, `null`, `''` or `none`), or the fourth merge mode **`none`** ("No merging — an item is done when its agent marks it done"): no merge step. Items run in dependency order within the concurrency and usage limits; an item is done when its todo is done (its turn over); no git, PR or checks are read; a held item waits for the developer's ✔ Done (`to-confirm`, an Inbox "Confirm done" item). Without a target the runs are D76's own (the source's branch, or the source folder; a plain folder may be a source). The last item raises "Plan done".
  - **MCP:** `plan_create`'s `target_branch` is optional; `plan_start` takes `merge_mode: none` and `allow_protected_target`; their descriptions say plans are for anything and when to ask the developer.
  - **UI:** New plan's target is optional ("No target — items finish when marked done"); the header chip shows "⎇ dev (agents merge — acknowledged)" or "No merge step".
  - **Compatibility:** existing plans are unchanged; plans.db gets migration 3 (`no_merge`, `allow_protected_target`; no applied migration edited). The Jira import still requires a target. What's new `plans-any` under 1.22.0 (shown only while Plans is on). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Plans for anything*; contract → *Plans (D99)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D99 follow-up · Plans for anything*.
- **D99 / D76 follow-up · runs appear in the source's sidebar folder (2026-10-19).** Developer: "when plan starts new sessions for implementation, it would be nice that it would start those within the same folder, instead of starting them in root folder of sessions". Decision: a plan item's run and a todo's ▸ Run session go to the **sidebar folder** (D71) of their source session, right after it (a later run after the earlier ones of the same source); a root source keeps its run at the root after it; a hidden Team member source uses the folder it had (`originalPlace`, else the root); a chat source, a pinned source: the root. Written through the D71 layout store (synced like a manual move), placed once, by the machine that owns the run and by a paired machine looking at it. No setting. New session, Continue as session (D106), Continue in a fresh session (D83, which already keeps the place) and Team's create member are unchanged. Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Where runs appear*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D99 / D76 follow-up · runs in the source's folder*.

## Team (added 2026-10-13)

- **D100 Team: run a set of role agents ("members") from one page; experimental, switchable and revertible.** Developer decision 2026-10-13 (core lane T1; access requests are T2, the UI, suggestions analysis and templates UI T3). Generic (any organisation), single-user. Details: `docs/team.md`; contract → *Team (D100)*; removal: `docs/team-removal.md`.
  - **Switchable and revertible (hard rule):** Settings → Experimental → **Team**, off by default (`experimental.team`). Off: only `GET /api/team/state` answers, no nav entry, no timer, nothing hidden; Switchboard behaves exactly as before. All Team data is in a separate `<dataDir>/team.db` with its own migrations (none in the main database); its code is in `src/server/team/`, `src/core/team*.ts`, `src/web/team/`, wired through hooks marked `Team (D100) hook` and listed in `docs/team-removal.md` (verified by removing Team from a scratch copy and type-checking it; a test keeps the list complete).
  - **Members** wrap an ordinary session (adopt one, or create a new one in a folder): role, charter (owns / produces / never does), template, machine and session, workplace, model / effort, rhythm (Switchboard loops and / or "on hand-off"), budget (share of the daily usage, account), gates ("needs you for"; "anything outward-facing" always on), derived status, pause. Adoption keeps history, worktrees, todos and loops; the charter reaches the agent through the D64 standing instruction (D91 reload: from its next message); hooked terminal sessions (D72) and sessions already in a team are refused; CLI `/loop` jobs are only flagged. Member sessions leave the sidebar through one filter (their sidebar place untouched, the original recorded); Search, the Inbox and notifications label them with the role. Return puts the session back as a plain session in its place and withdraws the charter with a note. Switching Team off shows every member again; a broken `team.db` hides nothing.
  - **Hand-offs** become todos (D68) in the receiver's session (a `planRef` seam for Plans, D99); members get `team_handoff`, `team_members`, `team_report` in the `switchboard` MCP server, only in member sessions while Team is on. No agent-to-agent chat. **Activity** feed in `team.db` (capped). **Needs you** derives the members' Inbox items and adds Team requests (gates, budgets; access later). **Budgets** pause a member's Team loops and raise a request when over its daily share. **Gates**: wired for a member's review-card merge into a protected / default branch; external writes are T2's seam.
  - **Sync** with paired machines (D48 / D71 / D98 style): the team definition is shared, last writer wins per record; a member runs on its session's machine and is acted on there through the peer API; an offline machine's members show offline; older peers are left out; secrets never travel. A member can be **moved** to another paired machine with the D65 take-over (checks first, refused while a turn runs; the machine id changes, everything else is kept; its loops move without double firing; access entries "need setup" on the target).
  - **Not in v1:** a coordinator / team-lead agent (ASSUMED). Version bump, CHANGELOG and What's new: T3.
  - **The Team page (T3):** `/team` while Team is on, the nav's Team item and ⚑ Team entry above SESSIONS, member cards, Needs you answered in place, the member panel (the real session chat, an Access slot for T2, Settings), the review of suggestions with on-demand charter drafts, templates, phone / tablet layouts; What's new `team` (1.17.0). Details: `docs/team.md` → *The Team page*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100-T3*.
  - ASSUMED items and open questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 · Team*.
- **D100 follow-up · Access requests (lane T2).** Developer decision 2026-10-13: "For each team member, a dynamic settings page where the agent can request either MCPs or API keys in order to fetch the data for analysis." Details: `docs/team.md` → *Access requests*; contract → *Team access (D100-T2)*; security → *Team access (D100-T2)*.
  - **The agent asks** with `request_access` (Team MCP tools: member sessions only, Team on): kind (`mcp`: catalog / custom / shared from the MCP page, or `api-key`), service, host, why, the smallest scope, and the fields the developer fills (name = environment variable, label, secret, help, help URL, pattern). The description: ask only when blocked, ask for the minimum, never for credentials in chat.
  - **Needs you**: a `requests` row of kind `access` (the `external` seam) showing the exact service and host; Set up… / Deny / Not now. Never auto-approved; a catalog host mismatch warns; 5 new requests per member per hour.
  - **The Access tab** is built from the member's requests: one section per data source with exactly the declared fields, Test connection (MCP: start and list tools with a timeout; key: one HEAD / GET to a declared test URL on the same host only), status, last used, used by, Revoke, Replace, optional expiry, audit trail. T3 mounts it (`src/web/team/access/`).
  - **Secrets in the OS keychain only** (pluggable `SecretStore`: macOS `security`, Windows Credential Manager through PowerShell, Linux `secret-tool`; in-memory under tests); `team.db` keeps keychain references (`Switchboard Team`, `<member>/<access>/<FIELD>`) and metadata. The DPAPI-like file fallback is documented, not built (ASSUMED).
  - **Delivery to that member only**: API keys as environment variables at its spawn; MCP servers in its MCP config through a launcher that fetches the values from Switchboard when it starts (no value in a config file); a D91 restart applies it, and the agent is told the access by name. **Masking** of the member's recorded output (`•••• (FIELD)`).
  - **Paired PCs**: the records sync, values never: "Set up on Mac only" + Set up here too; after a move the access needs setup on the target. **Devices (D73)**: phones read the tab and may Deny / Not now; setup, test, revoke and expiry are refused ("set this up on a computer"); the peer API carries metadata reads only.
  - ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100-T2 · Access requests*.
- **D100 follow-up · member order and a scrollable cards strip.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "I need to be able to reorder the agents in a team. also it should be horizontally scrollable." Members carry an explicit `order` (team.db migration 3; new members go last), part of the synced team definition (last writer wins per member; an older peer keeps creation order); `PUT /api/team/order { memberIds }` (phones may reorder; on the peer allow-list). The cards become ONE row that scrolls sideways (cards keep ~196 px, a soft fade on the side with more, the selected card scrolled into view); reorder by mouse drag (drop line), long press on touch (D74), ⋯ Move left / right (up / down on the phone's list, which now follows the order) and Alt+← / →, with edge auto-scroll. Details: `docs/team.md` → *The Team page* → *Order*; contract → *Team (D100)*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · member order*.
- **D100 follow-up · multiple teams.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "I also need to be able to create multiple teams." The first team keeps `id: "team"` and its data; teams are created, renamed, reordered and deleted (asks first; its members return to the session list as with ↩ Return to sessions, never deleting a session; any team while another remains, the first one too: rulings below). Each team has its own name, members, order, hand-offs, activity and Needs you; a session is in one team only; a member moves with ⋯ → Move to team ▸ (charter, rhythm, budget, access and hand-offs kept). The Team page gets a scrollable, reorderable team switcher with needs-you counts, **All teams** (every Needs you, labelled) and ＋ New team; `/team/{teamId}`, `/team` = the last-used team; the sidebar lists one ⚑ entry per team; the Adopt / Create form has a Team picker. `team_handoff` / `team_members` act in the caller's team; another team's member is "Role (Team)" or `team`. Teams sync as part of the definition (last writer wins); an older peer sees only the first team. API: `GET/POST /api/team/teams`, `PUT/DELETE /api/team/teams/{id}`, `PUT /api/team/teams/order`, `POST /api/team/members/{id}/team`, `teamId` on the existing routes (none = the first team). Details: `docs/team.md` → *Teams*; contract → *Team (D100)*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · multiple teams*.
- **D100 follow-up · the member's model from a dropdown.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "I need to be able to choose from dropdown the model that the team member is going to be running with." The member Settings tab and the Adopt / Create form replace the free-text model / effort with the session header's model and effort picker (D31 / D42's shared `ModelChoicePicker`) over the models the member's CLI reports (effort only for models with levels). A change goes to the member's session the way the header changes it (from its next turn) and is stored on the member, so it survives a restart and syncs (a choice made in the session header is stored too: rulings below); the machine that runs the member applies a choice made on the other one. A template's model is pre-selected when the CLI offers it, else the session's current model; the card and panel keep showing what the session runs with. Details: `docs/team.md` → *Model and effort*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · model dropdown*.
- **D100 follow-up · rulings 2026-10-14 (on the follow-ups' questions).** (1) Any team, **the first one too**, can be deleted while another team remains (409 `last-team` for the last one); once `team` is gone, the routes without a team id act on the first remaining team in the teams' order, and it is not recreated. (2) The phone keeps Needs you on top, then the cards in the team's order (as built). (3) The member's saved model follows a model / effort the developer picks in the member's session header (`PUT /api/sessions/{id}/model`; new hook `teamSessionModelChanged` in `src/server/api/sessions.ts`) and syncs; D82's routing and D63's account switching never change it. (4) What's new: the `team-update` mini-tour under 1.18.0 (team switcher / ＋ New team, reordering the cards, the model dropdown). Details: `docs/team.md`.
- **D100 follow-up · activity redesign (2026-10-14).** Developer request 2026-10-14: "I need this part from screenshot to be nicer to the eye, it's annoying to read. also it should be collapsable" (the Team page's left column). Each activity entry is two lines (kind icon · actor in violet · time right-aligned, then the text across the column); the server's activity texts are actor-less ("new charter accepted", "paused", "handed off to Developer: …"; the sender is a same-team hand-off's actor), and the page strips a leading "<actor>'s " / "<actor>: " / "<actor> " from older lines; no new activity kind, no migration. Runs of the same event by different members within 5 minutes are one row ("✓ 4 new charters accepted · 09:12–09:13", member chips, expands on click). Activity and Needs you collapse (▾ / ▸, kept per install in `localStorage`; Activity collapsed counts "· N new"; a new Needs-you item expands it); an empty Needs you is one line on its head; Show more after 20 rows. Details: `docs/team.md` → *Activity* and *The Team page* → *Activity on the page*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · activity redesign*.
- **D100 follow-up · the sidebar lists each team's members (2026-10-16).** Developer request 2026-10-16: "I should be able to expand the teams in order to see listed out agents in the sidebar, to have a clear overview on which agent is currently working and which one is idle — because right now I cannot follow up clearly where are we at with deliveries that are being made by team members." The developer chose the sidebar list only (no separate Deliveries view). Each ⚑ team entry gets a ▾ / ▸ chevron; expanded (default) it lists its members in the team's order with a status glyph, the role name and a short status line (`PROJ-14 · 40m`, `working · 12m`, `needs you`, `idle · 2h`, `paused`, `offline`); a member opens on the Team page, selected, on its chat; folded, the row adds "2 working · 1 idle". Each team's fold is remembered per install (`team.sidebarCollapsed`, `PUT /api/team/teams/{id}/sidebar`); `TeamSummary` gains `roster` and `collapsed` (additive). No new agent reporting: todos in progress, Plans' item keys (new Plans seam `plansItemsOfTodos`), the session's status, turn start and last activity. What's new `team-sidebar` under 1.23.0. Details: `docs/team.md` → *The Team page* → *Sidebar member rows*; contract → *Team (D100)*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · sidebar member rows*.
- **D100 follow-up · the Team page's left column folds away (2026-10-16).** The developer chose: "The whole left column — fold the Needs you + Activity column away to a slim strip (with the needs-you count), giving the member's chat the full width; remembered." On desktop and tablet a **‹** at the column's top-right corner (or the **[** key on the Team page, free in the app) folds Needs you + Activity into a 44 px strip: **›**, the needs-you count as an amber badge, and a violet dot when activity arrived since it was folded; the member panel takes the freed width (a 180 ms slide, none with `prefers-reduced-motion`). A click on the strip or › unfolds it; the badge unfolds it, opens Needs you's own fold and scrolls to it. A new Needs-you item does **not** unfold it: the badge pulses once (the developer's choice stands). Remembered per install in this browser (`sb.team.leftCollapsed`, `sb.team.leftSeenAt`), expanded by default; the expanded layout is unchanged, the individual folds keep working inside it; the phone is unchanged. No server change. What's new `team-left-column` under 1.24.0. Details: `docs/team.md` → *The Team page* → *Folding the left column*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · the left column folds (2026-10-16)*.
- **Waiting on subagents is working (2026-10-16; D30 / D43 / D51 / D100 follow-up).** Developer report 2026-10-16: "when team member is waiting for its subagents to finish the implementation, they appear as idle (they shouldn't)". Fixed at the source: a pending async agent or background workflow is a running subagent, so the session stays `run` until every subagent finished and the main turn is over (async agents' `local_agent` tasks already did; workflows did not). Team counts such a member as working ("Waiting on 3 subagents" on the card, `◐ 3 subagents · 25m` in the sidebar). The stale-subagent guard ends a subagent with no sign of life for 2 hours (and, as before, every subagent when the process ends), logged. A message to the main agent is still delivered at once while subagents run; loops and held messages are unchanged; the finish reminder, D73's push and Plans now wait for the subagents too. Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Session status* → *Waiting on subagents*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *Waiting on subagents*.
- **Waiting on subagents follow-up · background waits are working (2026-10-16).** Developer report 2026-10-16: a Team member whose line read "⏳ Waiting for GitHub Actions: gh pr checks $p --watch …" (a background `Bash` it waited on) showed idle in the sidebar and on its card. A background task the agent waits on now keeps the session `run` like a subagent: a GitHub wait (D30's classifier) until its notification; a scheduled wake-up until it fires (its own sub-state, "waking up at 18:40", not idle); any other background shell / monitor / task for at most 30 minutes without a sign of life; never a dev server or watcher it left running (`npm run dev`, `vite`, `dotnet watch`, `tail -f`, … : those now read "Running in the background"). A GitHub wait or a wake-up with no sign of life for 2 hours stops counting (the subagents' stale guard), logged; every wait ends at once with its notification or the process's end. Team: "Waiting on GitHub Actions" / "Waiting on a background task" / "Waking up at 18:40" on the card, `⏳ GitHub Actions · 12m` / `⏳ background task · 12m` / `⏰ waking up at 18:40` in the sidebar row. The finish reminder, Plans and every other `run` reader wait for it too; message delivery is unchanged. Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Session status* → *Waiting on background work*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *Waiting on background work*.
- **D123 · a refused SendMessage is no resume (2026-10-20; Waiting on subagents follow-up).** A `SendMessage` to an agent the user had stopped came back as a non-error result `{"success":false,"message":"Agent … was stopped by the user and won't be resumed. …"}`; its words matched the resume pattern ("Agent … resumed"), so a run was registered that never ended and the session read as "waiting on subagents" until the 2-hour stale guard. Now the structured result (the line's `tool_use_result`, else the text when it is JSON) decides first: `success: false` is no run; `resumedAgentId` or a resumed status is a run under that agent's id; a message "queued for delivery" adds no run while that agent's run is pending (the running agent gets it) and is one when that run has ended (the CLI resumes it with the message). The wording fallback ignores negations ("won't / will not / not be resumed", "stopped by the user"). `src/core/derive/background.ts` (`sendMessageOutcome`), `tests/core/background.test.ts`. Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Session status* → *Waiting on subagents*.
- **D100 follow-up · drag to move between teams (2026-10-14).** Developer request 2026-10-14: "id like to be able to move the team members between the different teams im going to create if possible". The ⋯ → Move to team ▸ of 1.18.0 stays; moving gets more direct: a member card dragged (mouse, or a long press on touch) onto another team's tab moves it there (the other teams' tabs light up as drop targets while a card is held; never its own team nor All teams; the tab row scrolls at its edges; a drop on the strip still reorders), and the member's Settings tab gets a **Team** select. Every move shows "Moved <member> to <team>" with **Undo** (back to its old team and place: the move route, then the old team's order). The ⋯ menu now opens by keyboard on a card (Enter / Space) and takes the focus. No new route or migration; a move made on the other PC shows live through the team sync's `teamChanged`. Details: `docs/team.md` → *Teams* → *Moving a member between teams*; assumptions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D100 follow-up · drag to move between teams*.

## Plans · Jira (added 2026-10-13)
- **D99 follow-up · Jira import and optional status sync (Jira lane).** Developer decision 2026-10-13. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Jira import and status sync*; contract → *Plans · Jira (D99)*; security → *Jira credentials (D99)*; removal: `docs/plans-removal.md`.
  - **Import**: paste an epic key or link; Switchboard reads its child issues (summary, description, acceptance criteria from a custom field or a description section, components, status, assignee, estimate). "Blocks / is blocked by" links between children become dependencies; links outside the epic are notes; a link that would close a cycle is left out and named. **Preview before anything is created** (items, "PROJ-12 waits on PROJ-11", unlinked items flagged). **Create plan** calls the core's `createFromExternal` seam: title "KEY Summary", description with the acceptance criteria, the key, no solutions (the developer picks them; components are a hint), the dependencies, `external { jira, epicKey }`; a **draft** (the merge mode is still chosen at Start).
  - **Propose missing order: not built.** Switchboard has no sanctioned one-shot agent run (the usage poller and the MCP helper make no model call; todo enrichment asks an existing session). Recorded as a question.
  - **Status sync** per plan, `off` (default) / `read-only` / `write`, chosen in the dialog and changeable on the plan. Read-only: title, status and description on demand and every 30 min while the plan runs, a drift note on the item. Write: In Progress at run start, In Review and a comment with the PR link when it opens, a comment while it waits on a developer question, a comment "Merged into <target>" (never Done: the team's process). Transition names per plan (defaults "In Progress", "In Review"), resolved by name through the issue's transitions; a missing one is a soft failure on the item, never blocking. Every write is in the plan's Jira activity; writes are idempotent (an event once; comments carry a marker a retry finds).
  - **Credentials**: Jira Cloud REST v3, basic auth; Settings → Integrations → Jira in the OS keychain (the Team access lane's `SecretStore`), else `ATL_BASE_URL` / `ATL_USER` / `ATL_TOKEN`. Never logged, never answered, never sent to peers; requests only to the configured base URL.
  - **Machines and devices**: import and sync run on the machine that owns the plan; routes on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (not the credentials); phones may preview and import (D73), the credential settings are local-only; every route classified.
  - **Experimental with Plans**: 404 `plans-off` and no sync while Plans is off; the Jira tables live in `plans.db`.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-13 on the lane's questions.** (q1) **(b)**: the missing order is proposed by the plan's source session: after an import with unlinked items (and on Propose order for a draft plan) it is sent a normal message asking it to call the new `plan_propose_dependencies` tool (with the plan tools, only while Plans is on; proposals, not changes); proposals are stored in `plans.db` (pending / accepted / rejected), accepting adds the edge through the core (cycles refused there), rejecting records it; `PlanEdgeProposals { planId }` on the plan, phones may decide. (q3) the Jira connection (`JiraCredentialsForm`) is part of Settings → Experimental → Plans, shown only while Plans is on. (q2) the Plans What's-new entry gains an "Import from Jira" step (page lane); the dialog's epic field is the anchor `data-tour="plans-jira-epic"`.
  - ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D99-Jira*.

## Sidebar navigation as an icon grid; collapsible Tools (added 2026-10-14)
- **D101 Sidebar navigation as an icon grid; collapsible Tools.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "the inbox, solutions, schedules & loops, mcp, artifacts, history, todos, team and plans are taking lots of space on the sidebar. can we present them as icons? also, tools should be collapsible". The developer chose the **icon grid** layout. Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Navigation grid*, *TOOLS*; the visual oracle's deviation: `docs/visual/README.md` → *D101*.
  - **Grid:** the views (Inbox, Solutions, Schedules & loops, MCP, Artifacts, History, Todos; Team and Plans while switched on, through their existing hooks) are a grid directly under "+ New session": 5 columns of 40 px square icon cells spread over the button's width, 6 px between rows (two rows today, also with Team and Plans). Inline SVG icons (18 px, stroke 1.6, `currentColor`, `src/web/shell/nav-icon-glyphs.ts`; no dependency): tray, layers, loop arrows, plug, page, clock turned back, ticked box, ⚑ flag, three joined nodes (and a magnifier for Search, unused while Search has no nav entry).
  - **Each cell:** `aria-label` = the name, `title` = the name and the badge's full text ("Solutions · 1 conflict"; on a touch screen D74's long-press bubble shows it); the selected nav style of the current page; Tab focus with a 2 px ring, Enter and Space open it; the `data-tour` anchors kept. **Badges** on the top-right corner: Inbox count (amber), Solutions' conflicts as an amber "!", failed schedules (red count), Artifacts and open Todos (neutral count), Team's needs-you (amber), Plans running (blue); "99+" above 99. Team's ⚑ takes the role colour only while the Team page is open.
  - **Drawer (D74):** on tablets and phones the same grid in 4 columns with the name under each icon (two lines at most; the name is mounted only there), cells at least 56 px high; on a coarse pointer at desktop width the cells are 44 px.
  - **TOOLS:** the header is a toggle, ▾ expanded / ▸ collapsed with the tools' count "(2)" and, when a sidebar tool's probe says it is down, a small red dot; "+ Add" still opens Settings → Embedded tools without toggling. Stored as the editable setting `ui.toolsCollapsed` (per install, not synced, like D41's panes), read before the first paint with the pane state; expanded by default.
  - Unchanged: Settings at the bottom, Team's "⚑ Team · N members" entry above SESSIONS, the SESSIONS list (it gets the freed height).
  - What's new 1.18.0: a one-step tour on the grid. ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D101*.

## Review cards removed (added 2026-10-14)

- **D102 Review cards removed (supersedes D79); Plans get their own Merge step.** Developer decision 2026-10-14: "it's not working as it's supposed to"; of *remove the feature* and *narrow it*, the developer chose **remove**. Reasons (what the developer saw):
  - cards came from sessions working in shared checkouts (folder mode), where Merge is not offered;
  - they stayed *pending* for days, because uncommitted leftovers never disappear;
  - the Team page's Needs you showed Merge for them, which did nothing;
  - the agents commit, push and merge themselves anyway.
  - **Removed:** the review service, its git adapter and wire (`src/server/reviews/`), `GET /api/reviews` and `POST /api/reviews/{id}/…`, the Inbox item kind `review`, the hub events `reviewResolved` / `reviewsChanged`, the turn-end trigger and the stale-card checks (startup, idle, close), Settings → Sessions → *Raise review cards* (`sessions.reviewCards`), the reviews repository, their entries in `PEER_API_ALLOW`, `DEVICE_ALLOWED` and `DEVICE_REFUSED`, the per-device push toggle *Ready for review* (D73; a stored one is ignored), the web's `ReviewCard`, the session header's Review badge (`SessionReview`), the drafts `review:<id>` / `commit:<id>` (D88), the What's-new tour *Review cards* and the main tour's step, and the Team gate `review-merge` with its Needs-you review items. The shared repository set (`session-repos.ts`) stays: checkpoints (D80) and the Diff tab (D90) read it (`docs/worktrees.md` → *Which repositories*).
  - **Storage:** migration **0043** (`0043_drop_session_reviews.sql`) drops `session_reviews`, turns todo items still in `review` into done (with a done time now), and deletes the cards' drafts and the `sessions.reviewCards` setting. **Choice recorded (least risky):** `session_todos` keeps `review` in its state CHECK (changing a CHECK needs a table rebuild, as 0031 / 0032 did); nothing writes it any more, and a value read back (an older peer's) is read as done (`todoStateOf`). The API's `TodoState` no longer has it; `PUT …/todos/{id}` refuses it (422).
  - **Todo runs (D76) and the board (D77):** a run item marked done (by its agent or the developer) is **done**; `skipReview` and `reviewCount` are gone; the board's Review column went; a run's link reads **Run** (no "(discarded)" / "(merged)" variants).
  - **Plans (D99): their own Merge step** in `plans.db` (no main-database change). In `developer` mode and for held items, a run that finished (its todo marked done with its turn over, or every PR open with green checks) makes the item **`ready-to-merge`** ("Waiting for your merge", the violet the review state had; stored as `waiting_on = 'review'`, the value plans.db's CHECK allows, answered as `waitingOn: 'merge'`). Its actions (`POST /api/plans/{id}/items/{itemId}/merge | open-pr | send-back`): **Merge** merges the item's branch into the target locally, never pushed, never forced, every repo checked first, refused with clear reasons (`uncommitted`, `target-dirty`, `conflicts` + the files, `target-missing`, `no-branch`, `busy` while its session works); the safe merge moved from the old `ReviewGit` to `src/server/worktrees/branch-merge.ts` (`BranchMergeGit`); **Open PR** pushes the branch and runs `gh pr create` (reused code); **Send back** `{ comment }` sends it to the item's session, puts its todo back in progress and the item runs again; **Open diff** is the session's Diff tab. An Inbox **system item** "Ready to merge: <item> (<plan>)" with **Merge** / **Open plan** is raised when it becomes ready and closed when it is merged, sent back or fails. `agents` mode (and non-held items): unchanged detection of the merge the agent made itself (`git merge-base --is-ancestor <tip> <target>` or the PR's merged state, checks green where configured); in developer mode a merge the agent made itself also counts (done, with a note). `ready-to-merge` counts as `waiting-you` in `plan_item_spans`. Devices may Merge / Open PR / Send back (as they could on a card); the routes are on `PEER_API_ALLOW`.
  - **Team (D100):** the `review-merge` gate and Needs-you review items are gone. The `merge-default-branch` gate stays a charter rule for the agent; a merge into a protected / default branch from Plans is refused for agents anyway (D99's `target-forbidden`), and the plan Merge is the developer's own click. Needs you shows a plan's "Ready to merge" Inbox item of a member's session with **Merge** / **Open plan** (it is an ordinary Inbox item of that session), besides questions, permissions and Team requests.
  - Not a feature: no What's-new entry (the *Review cards* one was removed). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Merge step*, `docs/todos.md`, `docs/database.md` (0043), contract → *Plans (D99)*; `docs/reviews.md` is a pointer. ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D102*.
- **D102 follow-up · stale Solutions and conflicts (2026-10-14).** Developer report: "I also have stale information in Solutions that I cannot solve because of moving sessions to the team members. also I think that the conflicts are wrong / stale as well". Causes: writers were "not ended" sessions, so **closed**, moved (D65) and continued (D83) sessions kept writing; a repo name a session kept after its worktree was removed (D38) read as "in place"; agent-made linked worktrees were invisible; worktrees removed outside Switchboard stayed listed; Team members (D100) are hidden from the sidebar, so the developer could not reach them. Fix: Solutions decides where a session writes from the shared repo set (`session-repos.ts`, `SessionRepoSet.editedTops` added): only open, not moved, not continued, not ended sessions write (members count); a linked worktree makes a session isolated and shows as a branch; the main checkout counts only while the session has its own uncommitted edits there (D90 `head`; developer ruling 2026-10-14 on the follow-up's question: naming the checkout or committed edits do not count; the other machine's sessions stay unshown); a worktree whose folder is gone disappears, a closed session's worktree is idle, a continued one's belongs to its successor; isolate refuses a closed / moved session (409 `closed`). The card's names and the branch cards' owners open the session (a member too, with its role and a note), and the header has **↻ Refresh**. Details: `docs/solutions.md` → *Conflicts* → *Who writes a repo (2026-10-14)*; `.loop/questions.md` → *Stale Solutions and conflicts (2026-10-14)*.

## Credits: on credits, and allowance first (added 2026-10-14)

- **D103 Switchboard shows when an account runs on credits and how much is left, and prefers subscription allowance over credits.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "switchboard should be able to also track whether the subscription/account that is being used is now running on credits (when weekly or 5h limit hits 100%) … once [the other account] returns …, switchboard should switch back to using an account on a subscription rate instead of … using the paid credits. I also should be able to see whether the account has credits and how many credits are left (in euros in my case)". Built on D17 / D46 (readings), D63 (profiles, the switcher), D66 (footer grid), D98 (shared accounts) and D73 (push through the Inbox). Details: `docs/accounts.md` → *Credits (D103)*, `docs/usage.md` → *Credits (D103)*; contract → *Credits per account (D103)*.
  - **Signals (VERIFIED, Claude Code 2.1.296's schema read-only, and the stored readings' JSON shape):** `rate_limit_event.rate_limit_info` carries `isUsingOverage` / `overageInUse` (true while a request past the plan limit is served from credits; seen with `status: "rejected"` on the 100 % weekly window), `overageStatus`, `overageResetsAt` (epoch s: the credit period's reset, the 1st of the month), `overageDisabledReason` (`org_level_disabled`, `out_of_credits`, …), `overageEnabled`; the `get_usage` answer has `rate_limits.extra_usage` = `{ is_enabled, monthly_limit, used_credits, utilization, currency, decimal_places, spend_limit_reached, … }` with the amounts in **minor units** (equal to `spend.limit.amount_minor` with `exponent` 2). No prepaid balance is read (`spend.balance` was null; its shape is unknown): "left" is the monthly credit limit minus what was used.
  - **State per account** (`src/core/credits.ts`, read from the readings already stored, no migration): **on credits** while the newest event reports overage in use and the window it names has not reset, or a plan window of the newest reading is at 100 % while credits are on (and their limit not reached); it ends by itself at that window's reset. Plus enabled, currency, limit / used / left (major units) and the credit period's reset. `ProfileUsage.credits` carries it, so the D98 exchange shares it (the newest reading wins). Unknown values stay `null`.
  - **UI:** the footer grid adds an amber **"€ on credits"** badge row under the line of an account on credits, with "€12.40 left of €50" (or "credits enabled · balance unknown"); the tooltip says until when, or the credits of an account that is not using them. Settings → Accounts: the credits line on the account ("On credits until 13:00 · €12.40 left of €50 · credits reset 1 Nov"; "Credits: …" while not in use; nothing while credits are off). Without credit data nothing changes (the desktop visual oracle is unchanged).
  - **Allowance first (the D63 switcher):** an account on credits is available only at cost: new sessions and limit switches take any account with allowance (here or, by D98's shared readings, as a paired machine read it) and only then one paying from credits; the 30-second check moves a live session on credits to an account with allowance as soon as one has it, **only between turns** (never mid-turn), also with early switching off; pinned sessions and the cooldown still hold. The chat divider reads "Switched account: Work → Private (moved off credits: Private has allowance again)".
  - **Every account out of allowance:** new setting Settings → Accounts → *When every account is out of allowance*: **Use credits** (default: sessions stay on, or move to, an account on credits) or **Pause new turns until an account resets** (developer ruling 2026-10-14 on D103-q2: a new turn is **not refused**: it waits in the session like a D44 queued message, `queued: "credits"`, with "Waiting for an account with allowance — Private back at 23:30" and Edit / Remove; new sessions, loop firings and todo / plan starts wait the same way without duplicates; once an account has allowance the session moves to it and the messages go out by themselves, in order). Ruling on D103-q1: the credits of an account not using them stay in its tooltip only.
  - **Notices:** one Inbox item (dismiss-only, so the devices' D73 Inbox push) when an account starts using credits (once per time it does) and one when its credits left fall below 20 % of the limit (once per credit period).
  - What's new 1.18.0: "Credits: see them, and save them" (two steps). ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D103*.

## Session header on one line (added 2026-10-14)

- **D104 One-line session title; the chip row folds to one line.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "when it comes to the title of the session on the top bar, it shouldn't wrap to new lines, it should be one line, so the top bar doesn't take too much space. also scope should be collapsable if there's shitload of repos". Details: `docs/responsive.md` → *Session header: title and chips*; the visual oracle's note: `docs/visual/README.md` → *D104*.
  - **Title:** never wraps (cut with …; the tooltip and the rename field carry the full title). The root path gives way first, then the title, never the actions; from 1024 px the narrow-column overflow (D74 follow-up) counts the title at 160 px at most, so a long title is cut before an action moves into ⋯. The top row is one line at every width (it used to wrap at 1024–1279 px).
  - **Chips:** "scope" in the request is the header's chip row. The status chips (work, mode, phase, stack, ultracode, runs / loops) come first; the scope follows as one chip per solution (the API's `Session.chips` is unchanged; the header splits the scope). When the chips need more than one line the row shows those that fit plus **+N more ▾**; a click shows them all (wrapping) with **▴ less**. Remembered per session in this browser (`switchboard.chipRows`), folded by default; refitted on every size change (`ResizeObserver`), at every width (it replaces the compact layouts' sideways-scrolling chip row).
  - What's new 1.18.0 (coordinator ruling 2026-10-14): a one-step tour `chip-fold` on the chip toggle (`data-tour="session-chips-toggle"`, route: the newest session), with a short card when that session has no toggle.
  - ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D104*.

- **D105 Team members' own agents draft specific charters.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "id love to have switchboard come up with better charters to the team members … in the charter, it shouldn't be that generic - it should be generated basing on the summary of the session that the team members were created from", with the rulings: (1) the member's **own** agent drafts its charter from its own session history; (2) the developer **approves each one** and nothing changes until it is accepted; (3) existing members are offered "Draft better charters" after the update. Built on D100 (Team), D64 / D91 (the standing instruction), D103 (held messages) and D102 (no review cards).
  - **The ask** is a normal message to the member's own session (`supervisor.sendMessage`, origin `service`: queued while a turn runs, held while new turns pause, D103), naming its role, team and current charter and asking it to summarise its work and call the new MCP tool **`team_propose_charter`** `{ owns, produces, neverDoes, howItWorks?, notes? }` once (member sessions only, while Team is on, like the other `team_*` tools). At most one open ask or proposal per member (an unanswered ask lapses after 24 h). Triggers: **Redraft charter** (the member's Settings tab and ⋯ menu), **Draft better charters** (the Team page header: the team's members; on All teams: every team's), the Adopt form (the new member is asked right after adopting; the deterministic draft stays the starting charter) and a **one-time offer** banner for members whose charter is template-only (remembered per install once acted on or Not now).
  - **The proposal** is a Team request of kind `charter` (team.db `requests`, synced) shown in **Needs you** ("<member> proposes a new charter", before → after per field, the agent's notes, **Accept / Edit… / Dismiss**) and above the member's Settings form. **Accept** replaces the charter (synced; the standing instruction is re-sent through D64 / D91; an activity line); **Edit…** opens Settings with the proposal filled in and Save accepts it with the edits; **Dismiss** records it. Phones may ask, accept and dismiss.
  - **`howItWorks`**: an optional charter field (how the member works today: rounds, cadence, inputs, who gives feedback) in core, the synced member JSON (no migration), the standing instruction ("How it works: …"), `team_members` and the form.
  - **Templates**: the Developer produces "Commits on a work branch with tests; a pull request, or a merge into the epic branch, as the plan or its charter says." (no review-card wording, D102); the older texts still count as template-only for the offer.
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-16 · the feature tools appear without a restart.** Bug report 2026-10-16: a member sent the charter ask answered "there's no team_propose_charter tool in this session". The helper registered the Team / Plans / Project memory tools once, at its start, so a session adopted, a switch turned on, or a probe that timed out kept running without them. Now the helper re-probes on Switchboard's wake-up (or every ~25 s), adds / removes the tools with MCP `list_changed`, and reports every tool list its CLI fetched; before an ask that needs a tool (D105's charter ask, D107's draft ask) Switchboard makes sure the CLI listed it: live when the CLI re-lists, else **one** `--resume` restart when idle (never mid-turn; the ask waits for the turn), divider "Switchboard tools updated". Adoption and switches only wake the helpers; nothing restarts unless an ask needs the tool. Details: `docs/mcp.md` → *Tools that appear without a restart*; `.loop/questions.md` → *Feature tools without a restart*.
  - What's new 1.18.0: a one-step tour `better-charters` on **Draft better charters**. Details: `docs/team.md` → *Better charters (D105)*; contract → *Team (D100)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D105*.

## Chats (added 2026-10-14)

- **D106 Chats: start a chat instead of a development session; it plans in its CLI's read-only mode and is continued as a session to code.** Developer request 2026-10-14: "can we add a mode that I can just start a chat instead of development session?" Rulings: (1) **tools:** "full tools, but only planning if so. coding needs to be within session": reading, search, web search / fetch, pasted screenshots and files, Switchboard's MCP tools; never edits, code or commands that change things; Claude Code in its **plan** permission mode, held there (Codex: its read-only sandbox; OpenCode: its plan agent); the developer cannot switch a chat out of plan mode ("Chats can plan but not edit. Continue as session to code."); its own empty scratch folder `<dataDir>/chats/<id>/` (no workspace AGENTS.md, no router questions, no worktrees, no workspace standing instruction; the switchboard MCP injection stays); optional **Look at folders** (read-only, at the start or later); (2) **placement:** its own **CHATS** section with "+ New chat", ⌘K "New chat" and "Chats", a chat badge in the header; excluded from Team suggestions, Plans sources, review / diff / checkpoints, conflicts and Solutions; a minimal New-chat form (message + attachments, CLI / account, model / effort, folders), the title from the first message; (3) **Continue as session:** the header's ⋯ → "Continue as session…" opens the New-session form pre-filled with the conversation carried over; the chat stays in CHATS "continued as <session>" with a link.
  - **Storage:** migration **0044** adds `sessions.kind` (`'session'` | `'chat'`, CHECK, default `'session'`) and `sessions.look_at` (JSON, default `'[]'`); the "continued as" link reuses 0036's `continued_to` / `continued_from`.
  - **Read-only per CLI** (VERIFIED vs ASSUMED in `docs/chats.md` → *Read-only per CLI*): Claude Code `--permission-mode plan` on every spawn, `--disallowedTools Edit,MultiEdit,Write,NotebookEdit,ExitPlanMode`, `WebFetch,WebSearch` allowed, `--add-dir` per folder; Codex `sandbox: read-only`, `approvalPolicy: never`; OpenCode `edit` / `bash` denied and the `plan` agent. On every CLI Switchboard **refuses every permission request of a chat at once** (never an Inbox item) and sets a reported mode other than `plan` back to `plan`. Continue in terminal, Remote Control and take-over are refused for chats (409 `chat`). There is no permission-mode picker in Switchboard (D6); a chat's header shows a locked **🔒 Plan mode** chip with the hint.
  - **The chat's instruction** replaces the standing instruction (D64): what a chat is, its folders, and of Switchboard's own parts the write-it-out rule (D64), the todo tools (D68–D75) and `artifact_save` (D89); not `loop_create` (D94), not D100's charter.
  - **Continue as session** reuses D62 P5's history export: `POST /api/sessions` `{ fromChat }` exports the chat (`handovers/<chat>/<stamp>-continued-as-session.md`) and starts the session's first message with it, then the developer's message; no turn of the chat's agent, the same on every CLI. Linked both ways with D83's dividers; the chat is not closed.
  - **Routes:** `POST /api/chats`, `PUT /api/chats/{id}/look-at`, `DELETE /api/chats/{id}`; all three on `DEVICE_ALLOWED` and `PEER_API_ALLOW`. Search labels chat results "Chat" / "· chat" (`SearchResult.chat`). The visual oracle is unchanged: with no chats the CHATS section renders nothing.
  - What's new 1.19.0 (`V1_19`): a one-step tour `chats`. Details: `docs/chats.md`; contract → *Chats (D106)*; ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D106*.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-14 (on D106's questions).** (1) **No shell at all in chats:** Claude Code's `--disallowedTools` also names `Bash`, `BashOutput`, `KillShell`, `KillBash`, `PowerShell` and `REPL`; Codex's app-server starts with `-c features.shell_tool=false -c features.unified_exec=false` (besides the read-only sandbox, approvals never, and Switchboard refusing any request); OpenCode's `bash: deny` confirmed; the chat's instruction says it has no shell and reads with the read tools, Grep and Glob. (2) **The sidebar shows both sections, collapsible:** SESSIONS and CHATS always show (CHATS also with no chats, "+ New chat" on its header), each header a ▾ / ▸ toggle folding its list, its count kept; the states are the editable settings `ui.sessionsCollapsed` / `ui.chatsCollapsed` (per install, default expanded, like D101's `ui.toolsCollapsed`); the chat button on the SESSIONS label is gone. The desktop visual oracle changes by design (`docs/visual/README.md` → *D106*). (3) **Direct delete** (`DELETE /api/chats/{id}`, ⋯ → Delete chat…) kept as built. Details: `docs/chats.md`, `docs/sidebar.md` → *SESSIONS and CHATS (D106)*; contract → *Collapsible SESSIONS and CHATS (D106)*.
  - **Rename, developer request 2026-10-22** ("also add a possibility to rename the chats"): the sidebar row's ⋯ → **Rename…** (or a double-click on its title) and the header's title (a click) or ⋯ → **Rename…**, through D22's `InlineTitle` and `PUT /api/sessions/{id}/title` (publishes `sessionUpdated`; a peer's chat is forwarded like a session). No server change: the first-message title is written once, so a rename sticks. Details: `docs/chats.md` → *Rename*.

## Project memory (added 2026-10-14)

- **D107 Project memory shared across sessions: agents propose, the developer approves, only approved entries reach a session; experimental, switchable and removable.** Developer request 2026-10-14 (the todo "Project memory shared across sessions"), with a safety requirement: Claude Code's permission classifier once blocked an agent writing standing instructions ("Instruction Poisoning"), so **no agent-written text may reach any session's system prompt / instruction unreviewed**. Built on D64 (standing instruction), D91 (reload of open sessions), D68 (the `switchboard` MCP server), D18 (saved folders), D100 / D99 (experimental features in their own files). Details: `docs/memory.md`; contract → *Project memory (D107)*; removal: `docs/memory-removal.md`.
  - **Entries per folder:** the scope is the session's canonical root (the saved workspace or repo; a worktree session's is its repo; chats have none). Fields: id, scope, title, text, tags, author (agent: its session id and title; or the developer), status `proposed | approved | rejected`, pinned, timestamps, decided at / by, and the agent's own title / text when the developer edited them.
  - **The review gate:** agents only propose (`memory_propose`, `POST /agent/v1/memory`: always `proposed`). The block and `memory_list` read `approved` rows only. Approving writes the approved title and text into the row the block reads, so an edit made by the developer is the approved version; rejected and unapproved entries are never injected.
  - **MCP tools** (only while on, only for sessions with a folder): `memory_propose { title, text, tags? }` (durable, reusable facts and conventions, not task notes; short; reviewed; 10 per session per hour; near-identical titles refused) and `memory_list { }` (the approved entries).
  - **Injection:** the approved entries go next to the standing instruction (after it and any D100 charter) as a delimited block "=== Project memory (approved by the developer) ===" … "=== End of project memory ===" in new and resumed sessions, on every CLI (Claude Code `--append-system-prompt`, Codex `developerInstructions`, OpenCode `system`). Pinned first, then newest; at most a cap of characters (default 4,000, 500–20,000, set on the Memory page); the rest are named on one line pointing to `memory_list`.
  - **Open sessions (D91 reused):** the block is part of the instruction D91 compares (`instructionOutdated`); when a folder's approved memory changes, its open sessions get `reloadInstruction` (idle → one `--resume` restart, busy → after its turn, never mid-turn).
  - **UI:** a **Memory** cell in the D101 nav grid (badge: proposals to review) and the Memory page (folder and machine pickers, the review queue with Approve / Edit… / Reject and the proposing session linked, approved entries with pin / edit / delete, + Add entry, rejected, the cap); one Inbox item per folder with proposals ("3 memory proposals for Acme app" · Review), resolved when its queue is empty; "Memory: N entries" in the session's right panel.
  - **Experimental and removable (like Team / Plans):** Settings → Experimental → **Project memory** (`experimental.memory`), off by default; its own `<dataDir>/memory.db` with its own migrations (the shared runner), no main-database migration; code in `src/server/memory/`, `src/core/memory.ts`, `src/hook/sb-memory-tools.ts`, `src/web/memory/`, wired through hooks marked `Project memory (D107) hook` and listed in `docs/memory-removal.md` (verified on a scratch worktree; a test keeps the list complete). Off: no MCP tools, no injection, every route 404 `memory-off` but `GET /api/memory/state`.
  - **Devices (D73):** phones may read, approve, reject, edit and pin; adding, deleting and the cap are desktop-only. **Peers (D48):** memory is per machine; every memory route is on the peer allow-list, so a paired machine's memory is read and reviewed through the proxy (the page's machine picker).
  - What's new 1.20.0 (`V1_20`): a two-step tour `memory` (the switch, the review queue). ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D107*.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-14 (on D107's questions).** (1) **Approving only flags open sessions:** no restart; they show D91's "older instruction" state and the developer applies or reloads when they choose; new and resumed sessions get the latest approved memory (supersedes the reload above). (2) **A rejected entry blocks near-identical proposals for 30 days only**, then a similar proposal enters the queue again. (3) **Phones** keep reading, approving, rejecting, editing and pinning only. (4) **Approval presence:** asked for a confirmation an agent with the developer's OS rights cannot give; researched (a WebAuthn passkey on a `localhost` ceremony page, approval only from a paired phone, an OS dialog) and **not built**: such an agent can write `memory.db` (or `CLAUDE.md`) directly, so each option guards only the route, not the data, and the trust anchor of any signature would sit in the same user's storage. What the gate does and does not protect against: `docs/memory.md` → *Approval presence*, `docs/security.md` → *Project memory approval presence (D107)*; the stronger boundary is an open question.
- **D107 follow-up · scopes on Project memory and "Draft memory from this session" (2026-10-15).** Developer request: Team (D100) members each maintain one feature of the platform but work from the same workspace root, so folder-scoped memory would mix every member's knowledge into every prompt; "I cant afford agent losing context if it is orientated around a specific feature maintenance". Approved design:
  - **Scopes on every entry** (`scopeKind` / `scopeKey` / `scopeLabel`): `workspace` (every session of the folder: today's behaviour; existing entries became workspace), `repo` (sessions working in that repository, any folder; identified by its normalized remote key, else its main checkout's path, through the shared repo set `session-repos.ts`, so a linked worktree is its repo) and `member` (one Team member, by member id). Member entries live on the machine the member runs on and move with it (D65 / D110 move: copied to the target through `POST /api/memory/import`, deleted here once taken; if the target cannot take them they stay and the error is logged).
  - **Injection protects member context:** member entries first, then repo, then workspace (pinned, then newest within each); budgets of the cap per scope, 50 % / 30 % / 20 % by default; unused budget passes on (member → repo → workspace) and back to a scope that stopped early; a non-member's member share goes to repo / workspace (60 % / 40 %); entries that did not fit are named with a pointer to `memory_list`; the Team charter is never counted against the cap. A workspace-only block is unchanged.
  - **Proposals carry a suggested scope:** `memory_propose { title, text, tags?, scope?, repo? }`, default `member` for a member's session, `repo` with exactly one repository, else `workspace`; the tool asks for the narrowest scope. The developer confirms or changes it when approving (a scope selector in the review queue and the edit form). `memory_list` returns the entries visible to the caller, each labelled with its scope.
  - **Draft memory from a session:** the session's and the member's ⋯ menus ("Draft memory from this session"), the Memory page and the Team page header ("Draft memory for all members") send the session a normal message (queued / held like D105's charter ask) asking it to review its own history and propose its durable facts; nothing is applied (the review queue); one ask per session, duplicates ignored for 24 h.
  - **Memory page:** a scope filter, a scope chip per entry (Workspace / repo name / member role); the session panel's "Memory: N entries" counts what that session receives, the breakdown in its tooltip.
  - **Compatibility:** `memory.db` migration 2 (`memory_scopes`, its own list; migration 1 untouched, a test pins its checksum); approved entries are injected exactly as approved. New hooks: Team's `memberOf` / `memberList` and the move's `memoryMemberMoved` (`docs/memory-removal.md`), Project memory's `team-link.ts` (`docs/team-removal.md`). What's new: `memory-scopes` under 1.22.0. Details: `docs/memory.md` → *Scopes*, *Draft memory from a session*; contract → *Project memory (D107)* → *Scopes and draft asks*; ASSUMED items and open questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D107 follow-up · memory scopes*.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-16 (on the follow-up's questions).** (1) **Repo reach as built:** a repo entry reaches every session working in that repository, whatever its folder. (2) **A failed member-memory copy is retried, then raised:** the old machine keeps a pending copy (`memory.db` migration 3, `memory_pending_copies`; earlier migrations untouched) and retries with backoff (1, 5, 30 minutes, then hourly), when the other machine comes online (`machineState`) and when Project memory changes there (its `memoryChanged`, now streamed to paired machines); after 3 failures or an hour an Inbox item "<member> is missing its memory on <PC>" offers **Retry** / **Keep on this PC**; on success the old copies are deleted and the item resolved. Details: `docs/memory.md` → *Scopes* → *A pending copy*.

## Insights (added 2026-10-14)

- **D108 Insights: one page of numbers about the agents' work, from what Switchboard records, computed from a daily rollup.** Developer decision 2026-10-14 (todo, relayed by the orchestrator). Details: `docs/insights.md` (every metric's exact definition); contract → *Insights (D108)*; settings → `insights.prices`.
  - **The page:** `/insights`, a bar-chart cell at the end of the D101 nav grid (the grid's eighth cell, still two rows; the visual oracle's deviation: `docs/visual/README.md` → *D108*), ⌘K → Insights. One row of filters (period 7 / 30 / 90 days or a custom range of whole UTC days; folder, CLI, model, account; Machine with paired machines), kept in the address. Six sections: **time and tokens** (per folder / session / model, tokens-per-turn by day), **estimate accuracy** (actual ÷ estimate in < 0.5× · 0.5–1× · 1–2× · > 2×, median, per model / folder), **outcomes and rework** per model, **cost per todo run**, **usage-limit forecast** per account, **suggested routing rules**. Chats (D106) are counted apart. No chart dependency (inline SVG / HTML; one validated series colour, the app's tokens; dark only like the app); responsive 360–1920 (D74).
  - **Rework signals (review cards are gone, D102):** todos **reopened** (done → open / in progress, a new `todo_reopens` log from 1.20; Plans' Send back reopens the todo too); **Plans** items merged / failed / blocked (plans.db through a new Plans hook, only while Plans is on) and the D102 **Merge-step decisions** (the "Ready to merge" Inbox items closed by Merge / Send back / a failed merge, main database); **D80 reverts** minus Redos per 100 turns. Nothing else is counted as rework.
  - **Cost:** each completed todo's tokens by kind (input, output, cache write, cache read, accumulated per in-progress span) × the per-model prices of the new editable setting **`insights.prices`** (Settings → Notifications & usage → *Prices (Insights)*: currency and rows `match` / input / output / cache write / cache read per 1M tokens; first matching row). The default is an **example** table (USD, Anthropic API list prices of October 2026), labelled "Example prices — edit in Settings" until saved, never presented as authoritative. Totals, average, median, by model, the 10 most expensive runs; todos completed before 1.20 (only D78's total) are priced at the input rate and marked as a lower bound.
  - **Forecast:** per Claude Code account, the least-squares pace of the current window's readings (the last hour for 5 h, the last 24 h for the week) extrapolated in a straight line: full / fills at <time> / holds at ~n % by the reset / flat / unknown; D103's credit line beside it.
  - **Suggested routing rules:** per priority × estimate class (≤ 30 / > 30 min / none), a model with ≥ 3 priced completed todos, a reopen rate not above the other's and at most half its median cost; **Open in Model by task…** adds the rule to D82's editor unsaved (`/settings/sessions?suggestRule=`). Never applied automatically.
  - **Data and performance:** migration **0045**: the daily rollup `insight_turns_daily` (day × session × CLI × model × account; turns, error turns, tokens and their split, duration, reverts, Redos), written by the events repository as each turn's result is appended, and filled for older events by a background backfill like D97's (bounded steps, resumable, progress in each batch's transaction); `todo_actuals` gains run session, priority, CLI, model, account and the token split (`session_todos.actual_usage`); `todo_reopens`; indexes on `todo_actuals.completed_at` and `usage_readings (profile_id, received_at)`. The result payload records `usage` (the split) and `model` (the model the CLI reported, `modelUsage`'s main entry) from 1.20 (additive). `GET /api/insights?period=&from=&to=&folder=&cli=&model=&account=` answers the page in one response from indexed reads, cached 30 s per query while nothing changed. Bench (`node tools/bench/insights.ts --browser`, D95 world + 400 todos, as an upgrade): backfill done 2.1 s after start; an answer p50 2.7 ms / p95 3.9 ms computed, 0.4 ms cached; the page in Chromium p50 68 ms / p95 118 ms until every section shows (bar: < 500 ms).
  - **Machines and devices:** each machine answers its own data (`GET /api/insights` on `PEER_API_ALLOW`); the page's Machine filter reads one machine at a time through the proxy; no merged view (ASSUMED, below). Devices (D73) may read it (`DEVICE_ALLOWED`).
  - What's new 1.20.0 (`V1_20`; the Project memory lane adds the same constant: the merge keeps one): a two-step tour `insights` (the nav cell, then the filters, `data-tour="insights-filters"`).
  - ASSUMED items and questions: `.loop/questions.md` → *D108 · Insights*.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-14 (on D108's questions).** (1) **All machines, merged, by default:** the page's Machine filter is *All machines* (default) / *This machine* / a paired machine.
