The merged view **pulls** each paired machine's **rows** of the period (rollup rows, completed todos with their reopen counts, Plans outcomes, Merge-step decisions, the last 24 h of readings, accounts with their D98 identity; new peer route `GET /api/insights/rows?from=&to=`, on `PEER_API_ALLOW` and `DEVICE_ALLOWED`) when the page asks, never a background exchange: nothing travels unless someone looks, and an older or away machine simply answers nothing. Rows, not numbers, so medians, buckets and the forecast are exact. A machine's rows are cached 60 s; `GET /api/insights?peers=cached` (the page's first request) never waits (a machine without a fresh copy is *pending*, fetched behind), `peers=wait` (the page's second request) waits up to 2.5 s. Folders are per machine (`<path>@@<machine>`), labelled with their machine where two share a name (`acme · Studio`); sessions and todos are namespaced like D48's (`r~<machine>~<id>`); accounts merge by identity (D98) for the forecast (both machines' readings), an account only one machine has is `<name> (<machine>)`. An offline, slow or older machine leaves this machine's numbers with the notice "This machine only — <machine> not reachable". Bench (`node tools/bench/insights-peers.ts`, two paired processes, ≈5,400 rollup rows and 400 todos each, 90 days): the first request with a reachable peer 53 ms, the fill-in 84 ms, recomputed with the peer's cached rows p50 35 / p95 39 ms; a slow peer (SIGSTOP) never delays the first request (p50 9 ms), its fill-in gives up at 2.4 s; offline p50 26 ms. (2) **Every turn priced:** the cost section adds the period's cost of every turn (sessions; chats apart) per folder, per model and in total, from the rollup's token split × the price table (turns before 1.20 priced at the input rate, marked `≥`; tokens of unpriced models counted apart), alongside the per-todo costs and under the same example-prices note; it follows the merged view. `docs/insights.md` → *Merged machines*, *Cost per todo run*.

## CI / pull-request feedback loop and issue import (added 2026-10-14)

- **D109 Switchboard follows each session's pull request: red checks go back to the session with the failing job's log, green and approved PRs get Merge PR in the Inbox, and GitHub / Jira issues can be imported as todos.** Developer todo, adapted to today's code (the todo predates D102):
  - review cards are not resurrected;
  - Plans' PR-check logic was generalised into one shared PR watcher, which Plans now uses.

  Built on:
  - D30: the worktree PR poll and the "PR merged" clean-up item;
  - D99 / D102: Plans, its Merge step, `BranchMergeGit`'s Open PR;
  - D73: devices, push through the Inbox;
  - D103: held messages;
  - D68: todos;
  - the Plans Jira client and connection.

  Details: `docs/ci.md`, `docs/todos.md` → *Import issues (D109)*, `docs/settings.md` → *CI & pull requests (D109)*, `docs/system-items.md` → *CI items (D109)*; contract → *CI and pull requests (D109)*.
  - **The shared PR watcher** (`src/server/ci/`) follows every live session worktree whose PR is open. It learns about PRs from:
    - D30's poll;
    - the end of a session's turn (the agent may have opened one);
    - Plans' Open PR.

    It reads them with `gh pr view` every `ci.pendingPollMinutes` (default 2) while checks run and `ci.idlePollMinutes` (default 10) otherwise, and stops once merged or closed. State is in `pr_watch` (migration **0046**; 0045 is left to the Insights lane). Every change publishes `ciChanged { sessionId }`.
  - **Red checks:** the first failing Actions job's log (`gh run view --log-failed`) is trimmed to `ci.logTailLines` (default 150) and sent as a normal message: "CI failed: <check> — … Fix it and push." It is queued while the agent works, held while new turns pause for credits, and goes to a hooked session's mailbox. Rules:
    - one message per red head;
    - at most `ci.maxAttempts` (default 3) per PR;
    - the same failure signature twice in a row also stops it.

    Stopped, the Inbox gets "CI keeps failing on PR #12 (<check>) — 3 fixes tried" with Open PR / Open session / Try again. Settings → CI & pull requests → *Send CI failures to the session* (default on) switches the messages off.
  - **Green and approved** (or approval not required; gh's review decision): "PR #12 ready to merge" with **Merge PR** and Open PR.
    - Merge PR runs `gh pr merge` only on the click, after reading the PR again.
    - The method is `ci.mergeMethod`: `repo` (default, the repository's default through gh, else squash), `squash`, `merge` or `rebase`.
    - After the merge the item closes and D30's "PR merged" item offers Remove worktree (the clean-up).
    - A small chip in the session header shows CI running / failed / green (ready to merge) / merged, only with a PR.
  - **Plans:** `PlanGit` reads its items' PRs through the watcher (`PrWatcher.read`). With the watcher's messages on, Plans sends no checks message of its own. A running item's session gets no watcher "ready to merge" item: Plans keeps its own Merge UI and item (`registerCiMergeOwner`). `ciChanged` makes the scheduler look at the item at once.
  - **Import issues as todos:** **Import issues…** on the Todos page (with a session picker) and **Import…** on a session's todo strip. Sources:
    - GitHub (`gh issue list --repo`, a label, assigned to me, open);
    - Jira (a project key or JQL, through the Plans Jira client and its one connection, also with Plans switched off again; without one the dialog says where to set it up).

    The flow is a preview with checkboxes, then each ticked issue becomes a todo: its title, its body (cut) and a link back. It is recorded per session in `todo_imports`, so a re-import skips it. Credentials are never logged, answered or sent to peers.
  - **Devices and peers:** the routes (`GET /api/sessions/{id}/ci`, `…/todos/import/sources`, `…/todos/import/preview`, `…/todos/import`) are on `DEVICE_ALLOWED` and `PEER_API_ALLOW`. Phones may Merge PR (an Inbox action, an explicit click) and import.
  - **Push:** no new toggle. The two items are Inbox items, so they reach paired devices under *Other Inbox items*.
  - **What's new:** `ci-loop` under 1.20.0 (the chip, the settings, Import issues). ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D109*.

## Target folders of a move (added 2026-10-14)

- **D110 Moving a session or a Team member to another machine lets the developer choose the target folders; the target verifies that they hold every repo before anything moves.** Developer request, 2026-10-14: "when moving over a session or a team member, I should be able to select the target folders (I shouldn't assume that the path is going to be exactly the same), and then switchboard should identify whether the selected folder indeed does have all required repos". The Mac and the Windows PC keep their repos at different paths.

  Built on D65 (take-over: `source/inspect`, `target/plan`, the move), D100 (the Team member's move, `move/check` + `move`), D18 (saved folders, workspaces, `solutionCandidates`). Details: `docs/peers.md` → *Target folders and verification (D110)*, `docs/team.md` → *Moving a member to another machine*; contract → *Target folders and verification (D110)*.
  - **Before:** the target looked for the repos only among its **saved** folders (each saved repo folder, and the repos of each saved workspace resolved by the session's solution names), matched by remote. A clone at an unsaved path, or a workspace laid out differently, was cloned again or blocked.
  - **Choose the folders:** the move dialog (the session's and the Team member's, one shared panel) lists the session's repos (name, remote, branch, uncommitted / unpushed) and lets the developer choose folders **on the target**: its saved folders and home, a typed path, or browsing its folders through the peer API. One workspace root usually covers a workspace session; a repo elsewhere gets a per-repo checkout. The choice is `placement { roots, repoPaths? }` on the preview, the start, `target/plan` and `target/apply`.
  - **The listing is safe:** folders only, never a file's name or contents, only under the home folder, a saved folder or a saved folder's parent (links resolved first); read-only, peer-callable.
  - **Verify before anything moves:** each chosen folder is searched to a bounded depth (itself and up to three levels below it, breadth first, at most 10,000 folders: group folders like `microfrontends/`, the nested mobile clone, `<org>/<group>/<repo>`) for main checkouts; a repo matches by its **normalised remote** (host / owner / repo, any URL shape, any case), never by its folder's name (a same-named checkout without the remote is a hint only). Each repo is **found** ✓, **found but on another branch / dirty** ⚠ (proceeds by D65's rules; dirty blocks a main-checkout session ✖), **missing** (cloned into the chosen folder, in the workspace's layout; ✖ when it cannot be) or **ambiguous** (several: the developer picks one). **Move** is enabled only when every repo is found or will be cloned. The verification feeds `target/plan`, and the apply uses exactly those paths: no path guessing. A chosen folder that is not saved yet is saved by the move (undone on a failure).
  - **Remembered:** the last choice per (source machine and folder → target machine) is kept on the initiating machine (setting `takeover.targetFolders`) and pre-fills the next move. No migration.
  - **The remote key:** `normalizeRemoteUrl` now lower-cases hosted keys and folds Azure DevOps' forms; keys are never stored, and the target recomputes them from the source's URL (`remoteKeyOf`), so older sources still match. The failing `tests/core/takeover.test.ts` test was a hygiene-sweep edit that left two hosts in one list; fixed with the normaliser.
  - **Devices and peers:** the new routes are desktop-only like every take-over route (`/api/takeover/*` on `DEVICE_REFUSED`); `POST /api/takeover/target/folders` and `…/target/verify` are on `PEER_API_ALLOW` (read-only), the UI's `POST /api/takeover/folders` is not.
  - **What's new:** `move-target` under 1.21.0. ASSUMED items: `.loop/questions.md` → *D110*.
  - **Rulings 2026-10-14 (on D110's questions).** (1) The saved folders' **parents** stay allowed places (as built). (2) Mixed versions are accepted: update both machines (said in the release notes). (3) A chosen folder that is not saved is **saved by the move** (as built). (4) The search goes **three levels** below each chosen folder (was two), breadth first so a crowded deep level never hides a shallow checkout; the skips stay and the cap went from 2,000 to 10,000 folders (a 13,025-folder temp tree takes about 0.2 s).

## Deleting artifacts from the lists (added 2026-10-19)

- **D111 Artifacts can be deleted from the Artifacts page and a session's Artifacts tab, one or several at once, including those whose session is gone.** Developer request, 2026-10-19 (relayed). Builds on D89 (artifacts) and D48 (paired machines). Details: `docs/artifacts.md` → *Deleting from the lists (D111)*; contract → *Artifacts saved on purpose (D89)*.
  - **Row ⋯ → Delete…** in both lists, with the viewer's confirmation (shared, not duplicated). **Bulk** on the page only: a checkbox per row, Select all of the shown rows, Delete N; each artifact through its own route; a partial failure keeps the failed rows selected with how many failed and why; the selection keeps only rows still shown.
  - **Unreachable machine:** its rows cannot be selected and Delete… is off with the reason (as the viewer, D48).
  - **Orphans:** new `DELETE /api/artifacts/{artifactId}` (only without a live session, else 409 `has-session`); `artifactsChanged.sessionId` may be `null`. **A paired machine's orphans are forwarded** through the existing machines proxy (`/api/machines/{id}/api/artifacts/{artifactId}`; one `PEER_API_ALLOW` entry), not disabled: the rows already carry their machine, so it was a small addition. A machine before this version refuses it with an error.
  - **What's new:** `artifacts-delete` under 1.25.0.

## Import from Jira into a folder (added 2026-10-20)

- **D112 Import from Jira no longer asks for a session: the developer picks where it runs, and Switchboard starts a new session there that owns the plan.** Developer question, 2026-10-20: "im not sure why i need to specify session for importing a plan from JIRA?" A plan needs a source session: its items are that session's todos, its runs are cut from it and sit next to it, its agent is asked for the missing order, and its machine owns the plan. Of the options offered, the developer chose **A: pick a folder, and Switchboard creates a new session that owns the plan.** Builds on D99 (Plans, its Jira lane), D14 / D18 (saved folders), D38 (a workspace session's solutions), D42 / D62 / D63 (model, CLI, account), D48 (paired machines) and D56 (the Simple start). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Import*, `docs/plans-ui.md` → *Import from Jira*; contract → *Plans · Jira (D99)*.
  - **The dialog** (Plans → Import from Jira…) asks for the epic (Preview as before) and **Where it runs**: the machine (with a paired machine), the folder, the CLI and account, the model and effort. These are the New-session form's pickers and data sources, not copies. For a workspace, the repositories come from the dialog's existing Repositories picker. The plan's base branch stays the Plan section's field. The last import's place (machine, folder, CLI, model, account, repos, base branch) is kept in this browser (`localStorage`, like the Todos page's view) and pre-fills the next import.
  - **Create plan** calls the new `POST /api/plans/jira/import` (and the preview calls `POST /api/plans/jira/preview`: there is no session yet). The server checks the body, then the folder and repos, then reads the epic, so a refusal there creates nothing. Then it starts a session **"Plan · <plan title>"** (the plan title the developer can edit in the dialog, e.g. "Plan · PROJ-10 Acme checkout"; the epic summary only when it is empty — developer decision, 2026-10-20; name `plan-<epic key>`, `-2`… when taken) through `POST /api/sessions`' own path (`startNewSession`), as a Simple start with no message. A workspace's chosen repos become its solutions (and the plan's repos). That session is the plan's source, so todos, runs, worktrees, sidebar placement and Propose order work through it unchanged.
  - **Its CLI starts at once and waits, idle**, like any New session without a first message (chats start with their message, so there is nothing idle to copy there). The ask for the missing order goes to it the normal way when items have no links.
  - **No leftovers:** if the plan cannot be made after the session started (the core refuses the target branch, …), the plan (if made) is removed and the session is closed (its process stops) and deleted with its attachments and exports, like a deleted chat; then the error is answered. Cancel and a failed preview never create one.
  - **"Use an existing session instead"** in the dialog goes to the session picker of before (with "Pick a folder instead" back). A session's **Plan strip** still imports with that session (no folder asked).
  - **Paired machines:** the dialog's machine runs both calls there (`/api/machines/{id}/api/plans/jira/…`, on `PEER_API_ALLOW`; the import is a long call like a start); the answer's plan and session are namespaced (`plan-import`). Phones may call both (D73 list).
  - **No migration** (the session is an ordinary one; nothing new is stored server-side). **What's new:** no new entry (a change to an existing step, not a new feature); the Plans tour's Import from Jira step text mentions picking where it runs.

## Loading and syncing the merged lists (added 2026-10-20)

- **D113 The Artifacts page and the Inbox show a loading state, and say when a paired machine's copy is still syncing or the machine is offline.** Developer request, 2026-10-20 (relayed): "we also should add loading state to the artifacts menu if we are syncing it with another pc", then "same goes for inbox". Builds on D89 (artifacts), D48 (paired machines) and the peer-reconnect fix. Details: `docs/artifacts.md` → *Loading and syncing (D113)*, `docs/inbox.md` → *Loading and syncing (D113)*, `docs/peers.md` → *List sync (D113)*.
  - **First load:** placeholder rows / cards (the D45 skeleton shapes) and `Loading…` instead of an empty list and a blank count; a failed first load says so with Try again. Re-queries and reloads keep the rows (a small spinner by the Artifacts count).
  - **Per paired machine:** "Syncing with <machine>…" (spinner) while its copy has not arrived or is being fetched again; a static "<machine> is offline — …" note while it cannot be reached. Local rows never wait for it; when the copy arrives the note goes and the list reads again. A polite live region says when it starts and ends.
  - **How the client learns it:** an additive `Machine.listSync` `{ artifacts, inbox }` (`syncing`, `syncedAt`), published on the existing `machineState` event. One mechanism and one shared web hook/component for both pages. `GET /api/artifacts` and `GET /api/inbox` keep their array shapes (older paired machines and phones read them). The merged Inbox count now goes out once a reconnected machine's Inbox has arrived.
  - **Session Artifacts tab:** placeholders while its list loads; a paired machine's session says "Loading artifacts from <machine>…" while the forwarded read runs, and "<machine> cannot be reached — …" with Try again if it fails.
  - **What's new:** none (a status that shows only while a machine syncs, not something to do; nothing to point a tour at).

## Queued send feedback (added 2026-10-20)

- **D114 A message sent while the agent works leaves the composer at once and shows as queued right away.** Developer report, 2026-10-20: "it doesnt send a message from the message box as waiting in the queue after clicking enter. the only way to see whether it is actually sent is to leave and re-enter the session, then we can see that the message is waiting in the queue, but the same exact message is loading back to the message box. we should get rid of that lag". Mid-turn the service can take seconds to answer `POST /api/sessions/{id}/messages` (the D80 checkpoint snapshot, the process's line queue); the composer waited for that answer, and leaving the session before it saved the text back into the draft (D88). Builds on D26 (the composer), D80 (checkpoints) and D88 (drafts). Details: `docs/chat.md` → *Composer*.
  - **Enter / Send** clears the draft and the field at once and shows an optimistic queued bubble (`src/web/views/session/outgoing.ts`, kept per session outside React), replaced by the message's own user event when it arrives, so it never shows twice. A session's sends go one after the other.
  - **A refusal** removes the bubble and puts the text back with the error (into the server draft when the session was left meanwhile), so the text is never lost.
  - **Developer rulings, 2026-10-20:** the server keeps taking the checkpoint snapshot before it records the message (unchanged; only the client stops waiting). The header **Pause** does not wait for in-flight sends; Stop, the stop-timeout note's Pause and answering a question card do.
  - **No migration, no API change.** **What's new:** none (a fix).

## Plan repositories picked by the plan's agent (added 2026-10-20)

- **D115 A workspace plan's agent picks each item's repositories; the Jira import no longer asks for them.** Developer request, 2026-10-20: "when importing plan from jira and directing the sessions withing the folder, i shouldnt be required to specify which repositories should be worked on. agent should be able to pick the solutions itself". Until now a workspace import (D112's folder import, and the session import from a workspace session without solutions) was refused without repos (422 `repos`, the dialog's required Repositories picker). Builds on D99 (Plans and its Jira lane, D99-Jira-q1's ask), D112 (the import into a new session), D76 (item runs) and D38 (a workspace session's solutions). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Repositories picked by the plan's agent (D115)*, `docs/plans-ui.md`; contract → *Plans (D99)* and *Plans · Jira (D99)*.
  - **Repositories are optional** for a workspace in both imports, and the same core rule applies to `POST /api/plans` / `plan_create`: a workspace without repos makes a plan with a target and no repos of its own (`Plan.reposPerItem`). The dialog says the plan's agent picks them and keeps an optional, collapsed **Limit to repositories…** (never pre-filled; ticked repos make the plan's repos as before). A repo folder is unchanged.
  - **The plan's agent picks**, through the new `plan_set_solutions { plan_id, items: [{ item, solutions }] }` (with the other plan tools, only while Plans is on; all four hints and a title; destructive because it overwrites a pending item's solutions). **Applied at once, not a proposal** (the developer asked for the agent to pick itself). Names must be writable solutions of the workspace; unknown or read-only ones refuse the whole call with the reasons. The plan's repos are the union of its items' solutions. The developer can still change a pending item's solutions (the item edit).
  - **The ask** goes to the plan's source session in the same message as the missing order (or alone): the workspace's solutions (the picker's list) and the items' keys, titles and descriptions. A failed send, and no pick 15 minutes after a sent ask, are lines of the plan's Jira activity; the plan page shows the waiting items with **Ask again** (`POST /api/plans/{id}/solutions/ask`, forwarded to a paired machine, allowed to phones).
  - **Until an item has solutions it cannot start**: it is `waiting` with "Waiting for the plan's agent to pick repositories". Starting the plan is allowed; the scheduler starts such items once they are set. A repository's target branch is made or checked when it first joins the plan (at Start, or when solutions bring it into a started plan), with the protected-target rule and `allowProtectedTarget` as before.
  - **No migration:** the marker is an empty `repos` on a plan with a target branch, which could not exist before (it was refused), and item solutions were stored already. **What's new:** none (the Plans tour does not mention picking repositories).
  - **Follow-ups 2026-10-20 (the agent's picking is reliable and never stalls silently).** The developer: "I mostly let agents use the repositories that it thinks it need to use after the planning alignments". Of the four follow-ups offered, the developer chose **3 (ask everywhere)** and **4 (a durable "not picked in time" check with a Needs-you Inbox item)**, and skipped a manual repository picker in the UI and a stricter meaning of "Limit to repositories". (3) Not only the Jira import asks now: an item added without solutions to a plan whose repositories are picked per item (New plan → `POST /api/plans` then its items, the plan page's add item) sends the source session the same ask, **once for a burst** (the service waits 2 s after the last added item, so New plan's items go out as one message). When the plan's own agent adds the item itself (`plan_add_item`) or creates the plan (`plan_create`), there is no separate message: the tool's answer is the ask (it says the repositories are picked per item, by it, and names the workspace's solutions; the agent routes answer them as `solutionChoices`), since a message to the agent that is making the very call would arrive after it and say what the answer can say at once; the check is armed all the same. (4) The time of the ask is stored in `plans.db` (migration 4: `plans.repos_asked_at`, `plans.repos_ask_raised`; migrations 1-3 untouched), so the 15-minute check survives a restart (re-armed when Plans activates; a check that fell due while Switchboard was down runs at once). When it finds items still waiting, or when the ask could not be sent, the Inbox raises a Needs-you item for **every** plan (not only Jira ones): "<n> items of "<plan>" wait for repositories", label **Pick repositories**, with **Ask again** (asks again and re-arms the check; the item closes), **Open plan** and **Dismiss**; one open per plan. It closes by itself once every item has repositories (or the plan ends or is deleted). The plan page's line and, for a Jira plan, its activity lines stay. A generic system item, so paired machines merge it into their Inbox and forward its actions, and phones may act on it, like the other Inbox items. Tests: `tests/server/plans/repos-per-item.test.ts`, `tests/server/plans/migration.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/plans-repos-ask.spec.ts` (`SWITCHBOARD_PLAN_PICK_REPOS_MS` shortens the wait for tests).

## Jira tickets moved to Done when merged (added 2026-10-20)

- **D116 Plans' Jira write sync moves a ticket to Done once its item is merged into the plan's target (feature) branch.** Developer request, 2026-10-20: "the plans should be able to merge the implementation into the feature branch, and once its merged to the feature branch, they should be able to move the task to done". Merging already worked (the agent modes and the developer's Merge merge into the target); the developer confirmed that the missing part is only moving the Jira ticket to Done. **Reverses D99's "never Done" (the team's own process moves the ticket there) on request.** Builds on D99 (Plans · Jira, its write sync and idempotent writes) and D102 (the Merge step). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Status sync*, `docs/plans-ui.md`; contract → *Plans · Jira (D99)*.
  - **A per-plan setting "Move to Done when merged into the feature branch"**, part of the write sync (`doneOnMerge`, in the import body and `PUT /api/plans/{id}/jira`, both optional, backward compatible), with an editable transition name (`transitions.done`, default "Done"). A checkbox in the import dialog's sync section (shown with write) and in `PlanJiraPanel`.
  - **On by default for new plans** (the dialog's box is checked; the API default when omitted). **Existing plans get it off**: no surprise for plans already running. Stored as `plan_jira.done_on_merge`, a column added when the Jira store opens an older `plans.db` (`0` for its plans); the Jira tables are created outside the core's migration list (D99), so this is not a `plans.db` migration and migrations 1-4 are untouched.
  - **Fires only on a real merge into the target**: an agent's merge (its PR merged or its branch merged, as the plan already detects it) or the developer's **Merge**. **Not** when the developer uses **Mark done**, and **not** for `mergeMode: 'none'` plans (no merge step).
  - **The write**: on the existing merged event, after the "Merged into <target>." comment, through the same transition resolver (the transition's name or its target status' name, case-insensitive). An issue already in that status (or any done-category status) is left alone; a missing transition is a soft failure (`syncNote` and an activity line, never retried, never blocking); network, 5xx and 401 failures are retried with the pending writes (at most 5); idempotent with its own event `done:<item>` in `plan_jira_writes`. Only with the write sync and the setting on. Once the ticket is Done, a still-pending In Progress / In Review retry of that item is dropped, so it never moves the ticket back. The activity says "Moved PROJ-12 to Done (merged into <target>)".
  - **Drift**: the item is done before the ticket moves, so the "Jira says Done; the plan item is …" note cannot show for a merged item, also between the merge and the transition. **What's new:** none (the Plans tour has no status-sync step).
  - **Follow-up 2026-10-20: no "Merged into" comment for plans without a merge step.** A `mergeMode: 'none'` plan with a target used to comment "Merged into <target>." when an agent finished an item, though nothing was merged; the comment now uses the same real-merge condition as the Done transition (one helper in `src/server/plans/jira/service.ts`).
- **D117 Jira Blocks direction fix** (D99 follow-up). Developer report, 2026-10-20: a real epic imported every dependency backwards (the end-of-chain E2E items were ready, the backend waited on them). Jira REST v3 puts the **other** issue on a link entry under the opposite side's verb: on issue I, `outwardIssue: O` means "I is blocked by O" (I waits on O) and `inwardIssue: N` means "I blocks N" (N waits on I). The import read it the other way; the fake Jira encoded the same mistake, so the tests agreed with it. Fixed in `blocksLinks`; the fake now answers real Jira's shape. Existing draft Jira plans get **Re-read dependencies from Jira** (`POST /api/plans/{id}/jira/dependencies`): plans do not record where a dependency came from, so a dependency that is the reverse of a Jira link is replaced (an accepted proposal is kept), anything else is kept, and the change is listed for confirmation first; refused once the plan has started. Same follow-up: plans can be deleted from the UI (the Plans list's row ⋯ and the plan page; a running or paused plan is cancelled first). Developer ruling 2026-10-20: deleting a running or paused plan also stops the item sessions still working (their Stop, server-side through `DELETE /api/plans/{id}?stop=1`; the sessions and worktrees stay); Cancel itself is unchanged. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Import*, *Re-read dependencies from Jira (D117)*; `docs/plans-ui.md` → *Deleting a plan*.

## The full Jira context for a plan's agents (added 2026-10-20)

- **D118 A Jira plan's agents get everything in Jira they need to fulfil the plan, read from Jira when it is sent.** Developer request, 2026-10-20: "I just want to have plans read all the items necessary that we have in jira in order to fulfill the plan". Until now an item's todo carried the ticket's summary, description, acceptance criteria, components, status, assignee and estimate (cut to 4,000 characters), and from the epic only its summary and key. Builds on D99 (Plans · Jira), D115 (the asks to the plan's agent) and D117 (the link direction). Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Jira context (D118)*; contract → *Plans · Jira (D99)*; `docs/mcp.md`.
  - **Read at run start, nothing new stored.** When an item's run starts, Switchboard reads its ticket and the epic from Jira (this machine's connection) and puts a "Jira context" block in the run's first message, after the item's brief and before the merge rule: the ticket in full (acceptance criteria first, description, sub-tasks with their status and description, all comments oldest first with author and date, remote links, attachment names, linked tickets outside the epic) and the epic shortened (about 3,000 characters, acceptance criteria first). The first message is capped at 40,000 characters: the ticket's oldest comments go first, then the epic, then the ticket's text, and the block says what was trimmed. Everything is derived from what every Jira plan already stores (its epic key, its items' keys), so a plan imported before D118 gets it too.
  - **The todo keeps a short summary** (the description cut to 1,200 characters, the acceptance criteria to 1,500, the Jira line, and a line that the full context is read at start), so the 4,000-character cap no longer drops anything silently. When the fresh read succeeds, a todo description that is the import's own text (it has the "Jira: <ticket link>" line, before and after D118) is left out of the brief, so a plan imported earlier (with its long todo) gets the ticket once; a description the developer rewrote without that line is kept.
  - **If Jira cannot be read at start** (no credentials, network), the run still starts, with the imported text and "Jira couldn't be read at start; use plan_jira_context" (and a line of the plan's Jira activity). It never blocks a run.
  - **The plan's agent** (its source session) gets the full epic (comments trimmed first past 30,000 characters) at the end of the order and repositories asks (the import's ask, Propose order, Ask again, the automatic ask).
  - **`plan_jira_context { plan_id?, item?, part? }`**, a read-only MCP tool with the plan tools (while Plans is on): the full current context of an item's ticket and/or the epic, read from Jira now. Answered only for the plan's source session and its item run sessions (a run session's default is its own item and the epic). Route `GET /agent/v1/plans/{planId}/jira/context` and, without a plan id, `GET /agent/v1/plans/jira/context`.
  - **Scope (developer):** attachments are **names only** (never downloaded); linked pages (Confluence, Figma, other remote links) are passed as **URLs** for the agent's own tools; the context is **read at run start** (and when asked or the tool is called). Jira stays read-only for this feature: no writes. Switchboard's own sync comments (ending with the `[switchboard:…]` marker) are left out everywhere (developer ruling, 2026-10-20: noise for the agents).
  - **Preview:** the import dialog shows per ticket small counts ("2 comments · 2 sub-tasks · 3 links · 2 attachments") and the epic's description collapsed, so the developer sees what the agents will get.
  - **No migration.** **What's new:** none (the Plans tour does not describe what agents receive).

## An imported plan in its own sidebar subfolder (added 2026-10-20)

- **D119 Import from Jira into a folder puts the plan's owner session (and so its runs) in a sidebar folder the developer picks, in the plan's own subfolder, instead of at the root.** Developer report, 2026-10-20: "the imported plan doesn't land in the folder, but in root of sessions". D112's "Plan · <plan title>" session started through `startNewSession` is unplaced, so it showed at the root of SESSIONS, and the plan's runs followed it there (*Where runs appear*). Builds on D112 (the import into a new session), D54 / D58 (sidebar folders and subfolders), D71 (layout records and the shared layout), D99 / D76 follow-up 2026-10-19 (`placeRun`) and D48 (paired machines). Details: `docs/sidebar.md` → *Where runs appear*, `docs/plans.md` → *The plan's own sidebar subfolder (D119)*, `docs/plans-ui.md` → *Import from Jira*; contract → *Plans · Jira (D99)*.
  - **Decision 1, a sidebar-folder picker** in the dialog's Where it runs (folder mode): "Sidebar root" and the existing folders and subfolders as an indented tree. **Smart default:** the sidebar folder holding the most open sessions whose working folder (saved folder or path) is the chosen one; on a tie the most recently active; none → the developer's last choice for that working folder (remembered per working folder in the dialog's `localStorage` place store); otherwise the root. The default follows the working folder unless the developer changed it by hand. ASSUMED (D119-plan-subfolders): a session inside a "Plan · …" subfolder counts for the folder that subfolder sits in, so the default never nests a new plan inside an earlier plan's subfolder; a folder too deep to hold another level gives the nearest one above it.
  - **Decision 2, each plan gets its own subfolder** "Plan · <plan title>" (the session's title, cut at a word break to the sidebar's 60 characters), made in the chosen folder (or at the top level for the root) with the owner session first; an exact name already at that level → " (2)", " (3)"…. Made only after the import succeeded, so the D112 rollback leaves no folder. Item runs then follow the session into it through the existing `placeRun` rule (verified end to end).
  - **Decision 3, written server-side** in `POST /api/plans/jira/import` through a new optional `sidebarFolderId` (`null` = the root; omitted = no placement, so older clients and API callers behave as before), so phones and paired machines behave the same: the folder and the place are one D71 layout transaction, published as `sidebarLayoutChanged` and handed to the shared-layout sync, like a manual move. `sidebarFolderId` is checked before anything is created (404 / 422).
  - **Paired machines (the cross-machine choice):** the placement is written in the layout of the machine the developer picked from, by the session's remote id, not in the layout of the machine the import runs on. The picker therefore lists this machine's folders, which are the shared layout when D71 sharing is on. Reasons: the D54 routes are not on the peer API, so the other machine's layout can be neither read nor written from here; its folder ids are its own unless the layout is shared; and a write there would leave this sidebar showing the session at the root while sharing is off, which is the reported bug. This is how this machine already places that machine's runs (`RunPlaces`). `POST /api/machines/{id}/api/plans/jira/import` checks `sidebarFolderId` here, strips it from the forwarded body, and places the answer's session after the other machine succeeds. With sharing on, the records reach the other machine and both show the same subfolder; with it off, the other machine's own sidebar shows the session at its root.
  - **Decision 4, unchanged:** "Use an existing session instead" and the Plan strip (no new session, no placement; runs still follow that session's folder).
  - **No migration** (the layout records exist since D71). **What's new:** none (it fixes where the plan lands; the picker is a field of an existing step).

## A plan item that stopped shows red, and one click opens its session (added 2026-10-20)

- **D120 A plan item whose agent ended its turn without finishing shows `stopped` (red, "Stopped — needs you") instead of `running`, raises an Inbox item, and a click on an item opens its run session.** Developer report, 2026-10-20: an item's run session ended its turn with its todo open ("the item isn't finished yet, so I haven't marked the todo done… one CI check failed for an unrelated infrastructure reason and needs a re-run (I'm not allowed to re-run Actions); then merge the PR…"), idle, its PR's checks red, and the plan showed it running for 3 hours. The developer: "can we make sure i can see it in plans as I was waiting for 3 hours for task to complete because in plans it looked like its still working instead of being stopped and red? Also it would be nice if I click on task I would be able to navigate to the session responsible". Builds on D99 (Plans), D102 (the Merge step, the *Ready* rule), the 1.23.x rule of what counts as working (*Session status* → *Waiting on subagents* / *Waiting on background work* in `docs/derivations.md`), D99's spans and the D115 follow-ups' Inbox item. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Stopped items (D120)*, `docs/plans-ui.md`, `docs/inbox.md`; contract → *Plans (D99)*.
  - **When.** A started item (stored `running`) whose run session's status is `done`, `idle` or `paused` (its turn is over and it is not working: subagents, a GitHub Actions wait and the background work 1.23 counts keep the status `run`), whose todo is not done, and which no other state covers (`question`, `ready-to-merge` / `to-confirm`, a second conflict's `blocked`, an error's `blocked`), since at least **2 minutes** (`PLAN_STOPPED_GRACE_MS`; tests: `SWITCHBOARD_PLAN_STOPPED_GRACE_MS`), so the usual "turn ends, then the todo is marked done" never flashes red. It applies to every merge mode, also `none`. Red checks while idle are `stopped` (not `running`, not `ready-to-merge`); green PRs while idle in a developer-merged item stay `ready-to-merge`.
  - **Derived, no migration.** "Since when" is the session's last activity (`lastActivityAt`, the last stored event: the turn's end), else the item's start; the todo and session are read as for the rest of the view. `PlanItem.stoppedSince` (additive) says since when; the item's `reason` is the agent's last message (whitespace folded, cut to 300 characters; "its agent ended its turn without finishing its todo" when there is none). `plans.db` is unchanged (still migration 4).
  - **Scheduling.** A stopped item stays stored `running`: it holds its concurrency slot, its dependents keep waiting on it (they are not blocked), and it is never moved to blocked. Its time counts as `waiting-you`. When its session works again (the developer replied, a turn started, Plans' own red-checks message), it is `running` again by itself.
  - **Inbox.** Entering `stopped` raises one system item per stop episode (`kind: plan-stopped`, label **Stopped**, status `need`, source `plans`, on the item's run session): "Stopped: <key> <title> (<plan>)" with the excerpt, **Open session** (a link to the run session) and **Dismiss**. It closes by itself when the item leaves `stopped` (runs again, done, failed), when the plan ends or is deleted; a new episode is a new item, and a dismissed one is not raised again for the same episode. Like every system item it is merged and forwarded on paired machines, phones may act on it, and the devices' push announces it.
  - **Click → session.** A click on an item (graph card, list row, Enter on the focused one) opens its run session when it has one, also a paired machine's (`r~<machine>~<id>`); an item without a session shows its detail as before. The detail stays one click away behind a small **ⓘ** on the card and the row (44 px on touch screens), and gains, for a stopped item, "Stopped: <excerpt>" and a prominent **Open session**. Chosen over "click → detail with a bigger Open session button" because the developer asked for one click to the session, and the detail's actions (Answer, Merge) stay where they were: a question row's Answer and a ready item's Merge are unchanged.
  - **Visible on the plan.** The red ■ state on cards and rows and in the graph legend; "■ N stopped" on the plan's header and its Plans list card.
  - **What's new:** none (an existing page's state and click). ASSUMED (D120-paused): a paused session (Switchboard stopped its process) counts as a turn that is over, like `done` / `idle`.

- **D121 Plans: a fresh item branch is never "merged"; falsely done items are reopened.** Developer report, 2026-10-20, on a real plan with merge mode `agents`, write sync and `doneOnMerge` on: several items got the note "merged into feature/… without your Merge", with no PR, 1.5 to 8 minutes after they started. Their agents kept working for over an hour. Their todos were marked done, their dependents started on a target without their work, and Jira got "Merged into" and the D116 Done. Correction from the developer: one suspected item really was merged 12 minutes later through its PR on GitHub, and a local `--is-ancestor` said "not merged" only because the local target was behind `origin/<target>`. Builds on D99, D102, D116, D120. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Merge step*, `docs/plans-ui.md`.
  - **Root cause.** `PlanGit.inspect` counted a branch as having work when its tip differed from the recorded `cut`, and as merged when that tip was an ancestor of the target. The cut is the target's tip read in `#startItem` **before** the run's worktree is made. When another item merged into the target in between, or the agent fast-forwarded its fresh branch to the target, a branch without one commit of its own had a tip ≠ cut inside the target, so it read as merged.
  - **The rule.** Merged = in every repo with work: its PR into the target is `MERGED`, or the branch has commits of its own (beyond its reflog's creation commit, else the cut; made on it, not fast-forwarded) and its tip is in the local target or `origin/<target>`. The remote-tracking ref is read as it is: no new `git fetch` in the developer's repos. A branch that is gone, or a PR that cannot be read, is unknown and never reopens anything. Chosen over "tip ≠ cut" alone, which still misreads the cut race; the reflog is the one local record of where the branch really started.
  - **Auto-repair and Reopen.** At activation and once per scheduling cycle of a running or paused plan, every item done by the git detection (not the developer's Merge, not Mark done) is read again. Not merged → it is reopened:
    - `running` again, or D120's `stopped` when its session is closed or gone;
    - its todo is open again, and `merged_at` / `done_at` are cleared;
    - a note, and an Inbox item "Reopened <key>: …" that lists the early-started dependents and the Jira key that was told;
    - those dependents get a warning but are not stopped.

    A proven merge is not read again until the next activation. The developer's **Reopen** does the same for any done item. No migration (`plans.db` stays at 4): the session-less marker is the item's stored `reason`, and the Jira generations live in `plan_jira_writes` rows.
  - **Jira.** No automatic correction (no comment, no transition back): the developer decides, so the item's note and the Inbox item say "Jira was told this was merged/Done — check <key>". The false `merged` / `done:<item>` write records are **retired** (renamed `retired:<n>:…`, plus a `reopened:<n>` generation record) rather than deleted. The history stays readable, and a real later merge writes its comment (a new marker) and its Done again under `merged#<n>` / `done:<item>#<n>`.
  - **Other ancestor checks looked at.** None of them can mark anything done:
    - the cleanup's "merged" (`src/server/cleanup/service.ts`) only proposes removing a worktree or branch the developer confirms, and a fresh branch holds no work;
    - D47's merge-kind check runs only after gh says the parent PR is merged;
    - `BranchMergeGit.check`'s up-to-date / fast-forward reading belongs to the developer's own Merge.
  - **What's new:** a Reopen action on a done item (an existing page's detail and a list row's ⋯).
- **D122 Plan PRs and commits reference only their own ticket.** The developer, 2026-10-20: "plans shouldn't add references to other tickets in the prs it opens - it collided with our jira automation (if the ticket 2 is mentioned in description of pr for ticket 1 and is merged, the task is being moved to done)". Builds on D99, D102, D118. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Merge step*.
  - **The brief.** Every item brief carries the rule right after the D118 Jira context (`planOwnKeyRule`): the PR title, the PR description, the branch name and every commit message may name only the item's own key, never another (not the epic, not a dependency, not a related ticket), because the tracker's automation acts on every key it finds; other work is described without its key. The context keeps the other keys: the agent needs them.
  - **Switchboard's own texts.**
    - The developer's Merge commit was git's "Merge branch '<branch>' into <target>"; the target, e.g. `feature/PROJ-10-…`, carries the epic's key. It is now "Merge <key> into the plan's target".
    - Open PR's description quoted the plan's title, which a Jira plan names after its epic. It is now "An item of a Switchboard plan."
    - The PR title and the branch slug leave out any other key in the todo's title (`withoutOtherKeys`).
  - **What's new:** none.

## Waiting for you at a turn's end (added 2026-10-21)

- **D124 A session whose turn ended waiting for the developer reads "needs you", with the ask: the agent's `turn_status` tool, else a text check of its last message.** Developer request, 2026-10-21 (names replaced with placeholders): "is there a way for switchboard, out of the context of last message provided by the agent, identify, whether the job is done vs. when its waiting for my input? … when agent is answering something like 'So today I'd: You: publish workspace 42. Me: build the … <tag-manager> workspace … Two of my questions are still open: who creates the <analytics> dimension …, and whether you run <ad network A> or <ad network B> ads.' its simple to say that it is waiting kind of on my follow up or answer rather than just being inactive because the job is done". Decision: **options 1 + 2, no AI classifier (developer, 2026-10-21)**. Builds on D64 (the standing instruction), D68 (the `switchboard` MCP server), D73 / D87 (push), D75 (the finish reminder), D100 (Team), D120 (Plans' stopped items) and the 1.23 rule of what counts as working. Details: `docs/derivations.md` → *Session status* → *Waiting for you at a turn's end*, `docs/mcp.md`, `docs/inbox.md`, `docs/plans.md` → *Needs-you items (D124)*; contract → *Turn status (D124)*.
  - **Option 1, the agent says it.** A new always-on MCP tool `turn_status { status: done | waiting_for_you | blocked, ask? }` (ask ≤ 300 characters, one line, required for the last two), its title and all four hints explicit (read-only false, destructive false, idempotent true, open-world false). Its description asks the agent to call it as the last action of every turn that ends without a question card. `POST /agent/v1/turn-status` stores it on the session for the turn it is made in. The D64 default standing instruction gains one short line: "End every turn without a question card with the switchboard turn_status tool." (the 1.30 default reads as the new one, D68's rule).
  - **Option 2, the text check.** For a turn without a declaration (Codex, OpenCode and hooked sessions that may lack the tool, an agent that forgets it): conservative rules on the last ~600 characters of the turn's last assistant text (a final question; asking phrases such as "let me know", "shall I", "should I", "do you want", "your call", "questions are still open", "once you"; a "You:" item), lowered by done guards ("no action needed", "nothing left", "all done", ending on "done."); polite closers are dropped first. Its ask is the last question sentence, else the last sentence, cut to 200 characters. Shown as **detected** (subtly: a dashed ring on the row, a "detected" hint with a tooltip in the header, "· detected" on the Inbox label), so a false positive is understandable.
  - **Precedence.** The declaration wins over the text check both ways; working (`run`: subagents, background waits) wins over `need`; a failed turn stays `fail`.
  - **Keyed to the turn.** A new turn (the developer's message, any turn the CLI starts, a hooked session's prompt) clears it. **Not waiting** on the session's needs-you line, or a Dismiss of its Inbox item, clears a false positive for that turn.
  - **Stored** as `sessions.turn_wait` (migration 0047) so a restart keeps "needs you"; the stored status stays the process's (restart, loops, checkpoints, close and take-over read it as before) and `Session.status` is the read one, with the additive `Session.turnWait`.
  - **Where it shows.** The session row and header (amber, the ask; the header's Not waiting); one Inbox item per episode, "Waiting for you: <session>" with the ask, Open session and Dismiss, closed by itself when a new turn starts (system item, so paired machines and phones see it); the devices' push like a question (quiet-push rules unchanged); Team (the member is needs-you, the item on its Needs you list); Plans: an item whose run session waits shows amber **Needs you** with the ask instead of D120's red Stopped, and no Stopped Inbox item is raised for it (red Stopped stays for turns that ended unfinished without a waiting signal). The D75 finish reminder does not fire for a turn that ended waiting. The composer's Stop, its queued-send clock and the fresh-session offer read such a `need` as a turn that is over.
  - **What's new:** "Sessions that wait for you" (1.31.0), one step on the sessions list.
  - ASSUMED (D124-chats): a chat (D106) is never text-checked (every chat reply waits for the developer by nature, so it would always read "needs you"); its agent's `turn_status` still counts.
  - ASSUMED (D124-hooked): a hooked terminal session's Stop hook carries `last_assistant_message` (current Claude Code); without it the text check does not run for that session (its `turn_status` still works).

## Machine power (added 2026-10-21)

- **D125 Each Switchboard host shows its power and shares it with its paired machines: a laptop its battery, a desktop an estimated total.** Developer request, 2026-10-21: "If a computer hosting switchboard is a laptop, it would be nice to share the state of battery and remaining time basing on the usage. For PCs, you can show current wattage usage of the system. Would be nice if thise stats would be shared when machines are paired. We dont need to show the phone device info as its not a hosted of switchboard". Decision: **desktops: an estimated total** (developer, 2026-10-21). Details: `docs/power.md`; `docs/peers.md` → *Machine power (D125)*; contract → *Machine power (D125)*.
  - **Laptops (any OS):** battery %, charging or discharging, the OS's own time estimate (to empty, or to full while charging) and the draw in W when readable. macOS `pmset -g batt` + `ioreg -rn AppleSmartBattery`; Windows one PowerShell call (`Win32_Battery` + `root/wmi` `BatteryStatus`); Linux `/sys/class/power_supply`. No sudo, no admin.
  - **Desktops: an estimated total**, shown as "~210 W (estimated)" with the parts in the tooltip: the GPU measured where possible without admin (`nvidia-smi`, Linux amdgpu hwmon), the CPU from its use (`idle + util × (max − idle)`, defaults by core count; a readable Linux RAPL counter replaces it), and a fixed baseline for the rest (40 W; Apple silicon 15 W). The three numbers are adjustable per machine in Settings → Machines → *Power estimate* (the `power.estimate` setting, no migration). Always labelled estimated, never presented as measured. A Mac desktop works the same way (no `powermetrics`).
  - **Sampling:** on demand, at most every 30 s, never overlapping, each command with a time limit; a missing source (no battery, no `nvidia-smi`, …) is not asked again for an hour; nothing is stored.
  - **Where it shows:** the sidebar footer (a PWR line under RAM; shorter on phones), Settings → Machines (this machine and each paired machine), the machine tags of a paired machine's sessions. Phones (paired devices) are not hosts: they report and show nothing of their own.
  - **Peers:** additive `machinePower` on the peer event stream; the other machine shows it as `Machine.power` (with `machineState`) while the machine is online. An older machine simply shows nothing.
  - **What's new:** "Battery and power draw" (1.32.0), two steps (the footer line, the Power estimate).
  - ASSUMED (D125-defaults): the default CPU idle / max watts by logical core count (≤ 4: 5 / 35, ≤ 8: 8 / 65, ≤ 16: 12 / 125, more: 20 / 200; Apple silicon 4 / 5 + 2.5 per core) and the baselines (40 W, Apple silicon 15 W) are rough typical values, to be tuned per machine in Settings.
  - ASSUMED (D125-ac-draw): on AC a laptop shows a draw only where the OS reports the system's own draw (a Mac's `SystemPowerIn`); a charge rate is never shown as the draw.

## PDF artifacts (added 2026-10-22)

- **D126 An artifact can be a PDF: the agent saves the file itself (`artifact_save` kind `pdf` with its `path`) and the viewer shows it in the browser's own PDF viewer.** Developer request, 2026-10-22, after an agent said "Switchboard artifacts can't hold a PDF, so it's an HTML page with the same content as the PDF…": "can we add support for the PDF in switchboard artifacts?" Builds on D89 (artifacts), D97 (search), D48 (paired machines), D57 (PDF attachments' serving) and D74 (responsive layout). Details: `docs/artifacts.md` → *Kinds, versions and limits* and *PDF (D126)*, `docs/security.md` → *Artifacts*, `docs/mcp.md` → *Artifacts: `artifact_save`*; contract → *Artifacts saved on purpose (D89)*.
  - **Kind `pdf`**, binary like `image`: by `path` only (the image rule; the developer's Save as artifact stays text-only), the file must start with `%PDF-`, stored as `artifacts/<id>/<n>.pdf` with media type `application/pdf`. **Up to 25 MB**, its own constant (`ARTIFACT_PDF_MAX`): the image limit (`ARTIFACT_IMAGE_MAX`, 10 MB) is lower, and is a separate constant, not a shared one, so it stays as it is. Versions as for images: a new version is a new file, no Source, no Compare, no Copy.
  - **Viewer:** desktop: an `<object type="application/pdf">` of the raw route (not sandboxed), with Download and **Open in new tab**. Phones and touch screens (`(max-width: 767px)` or `(pointer: coarse)`): a card with **Open PDF** (a new tab) instead, because iOS Safari draws only an embedded PDF's first page; no first-page preview, which would need pdf.js. **No pdf.js** or other dependency.
  - **Serving (the CSP choice):** `application/pdf`, `inline` (`attachment` with `?download`), `nosniff`, and `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'self'` only. Not the static artifacts' CSP: its `sandbox` stops Chrome's and Firefox's PDF viewers, and a `default-src` / `object-src` directive risks blocking Chrome's viewer (an embed in a document the browser makes under the response's CSP) and Firefox's pdf.js. `nosniff` with the PDF type is what keeps it from ever running as HTML; the D57 PDF attachments are served the same way without any CSP.
  - **Lists:** the kind tag **PDF**; the Artifacts page gets its own **PDFs** filter chip (between Images and Tables) rather than sharing Images, whose name would hide PDFs.
  - **Agents:** `artifact_save`'s kind list says "pdf (give path to a PDF file the session made)" and its description says a PDF is saved as it is, not converted to HTML or Markdown first. The D64 standing instruction is unchanged. The tool's annotations are unchanged.
  - **Search (D97):** a PDF's title is indexed, like an image's; no text is extracted from the file (no new dependency).
  - **Peers:** the raw route's proxy already passes the bytes with `content-type`, `content-disposition`, `nosniff` and the CSP (`PEER_RAW_HEADERS`), so a paired machine's PDF shows inline. An older machine refuses `pdf` with its kind error.
  - **Migration 0048** (needed): `artifacts_saved.kind` has a CHECK listing the kinds (0039), which SQLite cannot change in place, so the table is rebuilt (foreign keys off, D59), keeping every row and rowid; the 0042 search view and triggers are created again unchanged.
  - **What's new:** none (an addition to an existing feature, as D112 / D119).
  - ASSUMED (D126-object): `<object>` rather than `<iframe>`, so a browser without a PDF viewer shows the Open PDF card as its fallback instead of a download prompt.

## Processes sessions leave running (added 2026-10-22)

- **D127 Switchboard controls the processes agent sessions leave running: agents build .NET without lingering build servers, each session's processes are tracked and listed, and they are stopped when the session is closed or idle.** Developer report, 2026-10-22: "I think that switchboard is turning on dotnet for debugging c# solutions, but never closes them. that fills up my RAM quite quickly (fx I have plenty of dotnet solutions running, one of them is like 8gb in ram) - can we have a control over what agents turn on and turn it off automatically after its not needed anymore?" Seen on the developer's Mac the same day (read-only `ps`): eleven MSBuild worker nodes (`/nodemode:1 /nodeReuse:true`, ~190–300 MB each, ppid 1, 10–27 minutes old), one Roslyn compiler server (`VBCSCompiler`, 1.8 GB) under one of them, and an active `dotnet test` whose test host was 8.5 GB (a session still working). The idle build servers pile up across many agents' builds in many worktrees. Decisions (developer, 2026-10-22). Details: `docs/processes.md`; `docs/settings.md`, `docs/mcp.md` → *`process_keep`*; contract → *Session processes (D127)*.
  - **1. No build-server reuse for agent sessions.** Every CLI Switchboard spawns (all providers) gets `MSBUILDDISABLENODEREUSE=1`, `DOTNET_CLI_USE_MSBUILD_SERVER=0` and `UseSharedCompilation=false`. The last one is the documented MSBuild property of the Csc / Vbc tasks; MSBuild reads environment variables as properties, so it keeps the Roslyn compiler server (`VBCSCompiler`) from starting. No variable is named for VBCSCompiler itself. Only Switchboard's own spawns: never the developer's global environment, shell profiles, terminals or IDE. Hooked terminal sessions cannot get it. Trade-off: agents' .NET builds are slower (nodes and compiler start fresh each build).
  - **2. Each session's processes are tracked.** The spawned CLI also gets `SWITCHBOARD_SESSION=<session id>`, which its descendants inherit even once detached.
    - Every 30 s a read-only listing finds the processes: macOS `ps -E`, Linux `/proc/<pid>/environ`, both readable without root for one's own processes.
    - Windows cannot read another process's environment without native code. There the process tree from the session's CLI is followed (`Win32_Process` parent pid + creation time), keeping known descendants after their parent exits and guarding against reused pids.
    - Left out: the CLI itself, Switchboard's own MCP helpers, and the CLI's own helpers while it runs (a direct child that is not a shell).
    - Classified as `build-server`, `test-host`, `dev-server` (D30's long-running patterns) or `other`, with name, RSS, age, CPU % where cheap and the command line.
    - Hooked sessions: by tree from their terminal's reported pid, best effort.
  - **3. The UI.**
    - A **Processes** section in the session's right panel (name, kind, RAM, age, Stop, Keep).
    - Settings → **Processes** (the idle delay and every session's processes, paired machines' too, with totals).
    - A `procs <memory>` line in the sidebar footer while any are tracked, opening it.
    - Phone widths (the panel's bottom sheet) and paired machines (their sessions' routes are forwarded: listed and stopped there).
  - **4. Auto-stop.**
    - A session's processes (not those marked Keep) are stopped when it is **closed** (at once), or when it has been **idle for 10 minutes**: its turn over and not working by the 1.23 rules, so no running subagents, no waited-on background task, derived status not `run`.
    - This applies to build servers, test hosts and dev servers.
    - Never while its turn runs or it waits on a background task.
    - SIGTERM (the process and what runs under it), SIGKILL after 5 s; Windows `taskkill /PID <pid> /T`, then `/F`.
    - Never a process without the session's marker or tree link, never Rider's, the developer's own or their running Switchboard. Each pid is checked again right before it is signalled.
    - Each stop is noted on the session's timeline ("Stopped 6 build servers (1.9 GB) after 10 min idle").
    - The delay is the setting `processes.idleStopMinutes` (default 10, 0 = off).
  - **5. `process_keep`.** A new always-on tool of the built-in `switchboard` MCP server, `{ pid | match, keep, reason? }`, for the caller session's own processes only. It has a title and all four hints explicit: read-only false, destructive false, idempotent true, open-world false. Its description tells agents to keep a server only when the developer needs it running.
  - **6. Docs:** `docs/processes.md` (new), `docs/settings.md`, `docs/mcp.md`, the contract.
  - **What's new:** "Processes sessions leave running" (1.34.0), two steps (the footer line, Settings → Processes).
  - ASSUMED (D127-delay-zero): the delay 0 turns off the idle stop only; closing a session still stops its processes.
  - ASSUMED (D127-keep-family): a kept process also protects its ancestors and descendants among the session's processes from the automatic stop (stopping `npm run dev` would end the kept `vite`); the developer's own Stop stops the process and what runs under it, kept or not.
  - ASSUMED (D127-helpers): a direct child of a running CLI that is not a shell is the CLI's own helper (an MCP server) and is not listed; a CLI's tools run commands in a shell (a shell under such a helper is the agent's work again: a CLI behind a launcher). Under a CLI that still runs, an `other` process is listed but never stopped automatically (it may be an MCP server behind a shell wrapper); only build servers, test hosts and dev servers there are. Once the CLI is gone, whatever it left is listed and stopped.
  - ASSUMED (D127-hooked-root): a hooked session's root is the pid its hooks reported, taken only when that process's command line names claude (the registry is not asked every 30 s).

## Multi-select answers (added 2026-10-23)

- **D128 A multi-select question takes one or more answers: several options, plus optionally the developer's own words alongside them.** Developer request, 2026-10-23: "sometimes agent needs multi choice question for choosing answers. can we add support for that ?" Builds on M3.1 (the question pipeline), D39 (own answers: "for a multi-select question, the typed text is one more picked item"), D48 (paired machines), D73 (devices) and D88 (drafts). Details: `docs/questions.md` → *Multi-select answers (D128)*; contract → *Multi-select answers (D128)*.
  - **1. Contract.** The answer body gains `{ questionId, answerIndexes, text? }` for a `multiSelect` question (unique options, at least one choice, the own text optional). `answerIndexes` on a single-select question is 422. The single shapes keep working for older clients, phones and peers.
  - **2. What the CLI receives.** One string per question, the picked labels in option order and then the own text, joined with `", "`; an item holding `", "` or `"` is a quoted JSON string. VERIFIED: the M0.2 recording `ask-multiselect` and the CLI 2.1.303 bundle's own join helper and parser (read-only). A stale batch's message lists the joined string. The Codex / OpenCode bridges read it back into items (OpenCode's `multiple` questions get every item; Codex has no multi-select questions).
  - **3. Storage.** `0049_question_multi_answers.sql` (additive): `questions.answer_indexes` and `questions.answer_own`. `Question.answerIndexes` (additive) carries the picks.
  - **4. UI.** A multi-select question shows "Choose any" and checkbox options; Other… is a checkbox that opens the text field. Send needs a choice for every question. Space toggles, Enter sends. The answered view lists every choice. In the Inbox, the session chat, Team's Needs you and at phone widths (44 px touch targets).
  - **5. Peers and devices.** Same route, only the body changed: the peer API and the device allow-lists pass it. The card sends the single shapes when one option alone, or own words alone, are picked, so an older paired machine still takes them.
  - **What's new:** "Pick several answers" (1.35.0), one step on the question card.
  - ASSUMED (D128-other-needs-text): a ticked Other… with no text does not count as a choice; Send stays disabled until it has text or is unticked.
  - ASSUMED (D128-older-peer): several choices for a question on a paired machine from before D128 are refused there (422); one option alone, or own words alone, still work.

## Plan agents edit items already in the plan (added 2026-10-23)

- **D129 The plan's agent can change items already in its plan: `plan_update_item`, `plan_remove_item`, `plan_remove_dependency` and `plan_set_mode`, through the developer's own service methods and refusals.** Developer report, 2026-10-23. An agent said (verbatim, real names removed): "There's no tool for setting hold on an item that's already in the plan, so I'll try re-adding item 2's existing todo with hold set … ✕ plan_add_item … I can't set hold on item 2 from here: holds can only be set when an item is added, and item 2 is already in the plan." The developer: "also, extend plan mcp". Builds on D99 (Plans, its agent routes and tools), D102 (holds and the Merge step), D115 (`plan_set_solutions`) and the 2026-10-19 tool annotations. Details: `docs/plans.md` → *Editing items already in the plan (D129)*, `docs/mcp.md` → *Tool annotations*; contract → *Plans (D99)*.
  - **`plan_update_item { plan_id, item, hold?, title?, description?, estimate_minutes? }`** (`PUT /agent/v1/plans/{planId}/items/{item}`, `item` an id or a key): `PlanService.updateItem` as the agent. `hold` until the item is merged or done, in a draft, running or paused plan; a running item whose merge rule changed is told (as `setHolds`). The todo's title, description and estimate only before the item starts, through the todo service (its checks). The developer's item edit takes the same three fields. Every check runs before anything is written.
  - **`after` left out:** no edge API replaces an item's dependencies in one step, and doing it as removes then adds could stop at a cycle halfway; the agent uses `plan_add_dependency` and the new `plan_remove_dependency`.
  - **`plan_remove_item { plan_id, item }`** (`DELETE …/items/{item}`): `removeItem`, an item that is not running or done; its todo stays (as the developer's route). **`plan_remove_dependency { plan_id, from, to }`** (`DELETE …/edges/{from}/{to}`): `removeEdge`.
  - **`plan_set_mode { plan_id, merge_mode, allow_protected_target? }`** (`PUT …/mode`): `setMode` with its safeguards (no change to or from `none` once started, the protected-target acknowledgement). Built: it lets the agent do nothing `plan_start` does not already let it do (the same two inputs, the same rule to ASK the developer for both, the same refusals), and the plan page shows the change. Holds stay in `plan_update_item`.
  - **Each tool** has a title and all four hints: `plan_update_item` read-only false, destructive false, idempotent true, open-world false; `plan_remove_item` and `plan_remove_dependency` destructive true, idempotent true; `plan_set_mode` destructive false, idempotent true, open-world true (like `plan_start`: the mode decides whether agents merge PRs, and the protected-target check asks gh). Registered with the other plan tools (only while Plans is on, kept in step by the tools refresh), on the plan's source session's plans only. `plan_add_item`'s description now sends agents to `plan_update_item` for an item already in the plan.
  - **No migration.** **What's new:** none (agent tools; no UI change). Tests: `tests/server/plans/agent-item-tools.test.ts`, `tests/core/plans.test.ts`, `tests/server/todos/tool-annotations.test.ts`.
- **D130 A plan's runs go into a sidebar subfolder the developer names at Start; the source session stays where it is, and a folder with that name is reused.** Developer request, 2026-10-23 (verbatim): "I see plan spawns sessions under a fx. Swap Language folder which is good, but it would be better if it will create a subfolder with name that I should specify before plan starts". Builds on D99 (Plans), the D99 / D76 follow-up of 2026-10-19 (*Where runs appear*: `RunPlaces`, `placeRun`), D58 (subfolders, 5 levels), D71 (layout records, the shared layout, a deleted folder's sessions shown in the nearest live folder above), D119 (a Jira import's "Plan · …" subfolder) and D129 (`plan_set_mode`). Decisions (orchestrator, following the request):
  - **Start asks for it.** The Start dialog's **Sidebar subfolder** field is prefilled with the plan's title cut to 60 characters at a word break; when the source already sits in a D119 "Plan · …" subfolder, with that folder's name, which is then used as it is (no second folder nested in it). Cleared = no subfolder: the runs go next to the source as before. `POST /api/plans/{id}/start` takes `sidebarFolder` (omitted = as before, for older clients and phones); `plan_start` takes `sidebar_folder` and tells the agent to ask the developer for it with the merge mode.
  - **Where:** inside the source session's sidebar folder (the top level for a root or pinned source). **The source stays where it is**; only the runs go into the subfolder, at its end in the order they start.
  - **Same name = reuse**, no " (2)", so a re-start or a second plan with the same name collects there. D119's import keeps its unique-name rule.
  - **Depth:** a source folder five levels deep refuses the Start with 422 on `sidebarFolder` before anything changes.
  - **Stored on the plan** (`plans.db` migration 5, `plans.sidebar_folder`: the folder's id and name), not in `answers` (the plan's start answers have their own meaning and checks). Each run carries it on `todoLink.sidebarFolder` (the existing JSON column, no `switchboard.db` migration), so runs after a restart or a resume go there too, and `RunPlaces` needs no knowledge of Plans.
  - **Moved folder:** runs follow its id. **Deleted folder:** D71's rule: the nearest live folder above it, else next to the source.
  - **Paired machines:** placement stays in the layout of the machine that owns the run, as `RunPlaces` does: the owner makes the subfolder and places its runs by its ids; a machine looking at it places the runs by their remote ids into the same folder when the layout is shared, else into the folder so named next to the source there (found or made). Consistent with D119, where each machine's sidebar shows the plan's sessions together.
  - **Not built:** changing the folder of a running plan (`plan_set_mode` does not touch it) and a "Move future runs to subfolder…" action: not trivial (a route, validation and plan-page UI); runs can be dragged.
  - Details: `docs/plans.md` → *The runs' sidebar subfolder (D130)*, `docs/sidebar.md` → *Where runs appear*, `docs/mcp.md`; contract → *Plans (D99)*. Tests: `tests/core/plan-run-folder.test.ts`, `tests/server/plans/run-subfolder.test.ts`, `tests/server/sessions/run-place.test.ts`, `tests/server/plans/migration.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/plans-run-subfolder.spec.ts`.
- **D132 Team, Plans and Project memory graduate to general release (2.0).** Developer decision 2026-10-25 (the Switchboard 2.0 plan, Direction D v3): "in order to do that we would need to pull out or experimental functionalities to general release". Team (D100), Plans (D99) and Project memory (D107) were experimental behind Settings → Experimental, off by default and built as switchable hooks. Decisions (lane G, no questions to the developer; assumptions in `docs/design/v2-decisions.md`):
  - **Always on, no off-switch.** The keys `experimental.team`, `experimental.plans` and `experimental.memory` are gone from the settings (`PUT /api/settings` refuses them with 422); the three services open (and migrate) `team.db`, `plans.db` and `memory.db` at start; no route answers `404 team-off` / `plans-off` / `memory-off` any more (a database that cannot be opened still answers 503 `*-unavailable`). The hook structure stays (the markers, `docs/*-removal.md` as the wiring map); the off-state is unreachable.
  - **Existing installs:** whatever was stored, the features are on; the three rows are deleted once at start (`forgetGraduatedSwitches`, `src/server/settings/graduated.ts`; a settings value, no migration). Plans a 1.x build paused when the switch went off keep "Plans was switched off" until resumed.
  - **Nothing changes for someone who never used them:** with no member no session leaves SESSIONS and no charter is added; the sidebar shows no team entry while only the first team exists untouched (default name, no member); with no plan nothing runs; with no approved memory entry no session gets a memory block. The nav grid shows the Team, Plans and Memory cells from the start (eleven cells, three rows of five).
  - **MCP tools** keep their per-session probes (`src/hook/sb-feature-tools.ts`): plan tools for every session (each acts on the plans it owns), memory tools for a session with a folder, Team tools only in a member's session.
  - **Classic UI:** Settings → Experimental is removed; the Jira connection that lived under the Plans row moved to its own section, **Settings → Jira** (`/settings/experimental` opens it). The off panels ("Turn on in Settings → Experimental") became "unavailable" panels for a broken database. What's new: the Team / Plans / Project memory tours lose "(experimental)" and point at the sidebar cells; a new `graduated` tour (2.0.0).
  - **Peers and devices:** the peer and device allow-lists are unchanged; a paired 1.x machine with a feature off still answers `404 *-off`, read as before (no plans there, "Project memory is off there").
  - **Demo mode** is unchanged: Team, Plans and Project memory still do not run there (except Team / Plans with `SWITCHBOARD_DEMO_EXTRAS`).
  - Details: `docs/plans-removal.md`, `docs/team-removal.md`, `docs/memory-removal.md`, `docs/settings.md`. Tests: `tests/server/api/graduation.test.ts`, `tests/server/plans/switch.test.ts`, `tests/server/memory/switch.test.ts`, `tests/e2e/graduation.spec.ts`.
## Resolved spec gaps (accepted as proposed)
1. New-session worktree: branch `session/{name}` from the repo's current HEAD, at `../{repo}-wt-{name}`.
2. "Move … to worktree": create the worktree, then pause + resume the session with a message telling it to move its work there. Never stash / reset / checkout the developer's working tree.
3. Remove worktree: refused if uncommitted or unpushed changes; never `--force`; the branch is kept.
4. "Reindex n now": starts a background session from a built-in reindex prompt (uses the codebase-memory MCP), no direct binary calls.
5. Terminal-started sessions: listed in History; "Attach here" warns if the transcript changed less than 2 minutes ago.
6. No default schedules. The prototype's four schedules are demo data only.
7. Timeline kinds: Read/Grep/Glob/search → plan; Edit/Write/Bash → impl; `/loop`, ScheduleWakeup, rebuild/self-heal → loop; question/permission → ask; successful result → ok. Documented in `docs/derivations.md`.
8. Agents = the main session + one per Agent/Task tool call (plus workflow agents if visible in stream-json). D51: they are, through `task_progress` and the CLI's run files.
9. Artifacts: PR (from gh output / PR URLs), BRANCH, DIFF per solution+branch, CONTRACT `contracts/*.md`, QA `coverage-matrix.md`, FOLLOWUP `mobile-followups/*`, DOC other written `.md`; TICKET not auto-detected in v1.
10. Diff = worktree vs merge-base with its base branch, including uncommitted changes; in-place sessions diff against HEAD.
11. Machine footer: machine-wide CPU/RAM; process count = live supervised `claude` processes.
12. `phase-ledger.md` parsed leniently (table or bullets); absent → "no phase-ledger.md".
13. Tool URLs are saved by Switchboard (per contract), so the copy "saved in this browser" becomes "saved in Switchboard".
14. Settings → Embedded tools can add and remove tools (name + URL).
15. The Solutions view also lists `other/` (group "other/", on request only), visible under the All filter.
16. The scanner skips worktree folders (where `.git` is a file).
17. Paths and copy follow the current OS (the prototype shows Windows paths).
18. DB + token in the per-user app-data folder; tests always use temp folders.
19. Geist / Geist Mono self-hosted (OFL), no font CDN at runtime.
20. The `sb_token` cookie is set when the UI page is loaded from a loopback Host with `Sec-Fetch-Site` none/same-origin; API and `/hub` reject a missing cookie or a foreign Host/Origin.
21. Demo seed with the prototype's mock data, used only by the visual oracle and screenshots, never in normal runs.

## Open for the developer (not blocking the build)
- Whether driving Claude Code from a local tool fits the Max subscription terms (handoff README open decision).
- **Multi-user mode** (several human developers sharing Switchboard; not D100 Team): design proposal, not decided — `docs/design/team-mode.md` (its *Decisions for the developer* list).
- The router `AGENTS.md` asks agents to always confirm session-start answers via AskUserQuestion; Switchboard pre-fills them from its form (M5.2). If agents keep re-asking, the router may need a Living-document amendment. Outside this repo, so not touched.
- **D67 The built-in account of each CLI can be renamed.** Developer request, 2026-10-04 ("I'd like to be able to change names for the accounts"). Accounts added in Settings → Accounts could already be renamed inline; the built-in **Default** was locked (D63). Its name is only a label (everything keys off `cli_profiles.builtin`), so `PUT /api/accounts/profiles/{id}` now accepts `name` for it too (1–40 characters, unique per CLI as before). It stays the built-in account: not deleted, signed in or out, or sharing settings here. Settings → Accounts shows its name in an input like the others, and the hints that name it ("Same settings as …", "Its sessions go back to …") use its current name. The footer grid and pickers already show profile names. No migration.
